"Sona-chan!"

"Rias-chan!"

"Enough!" Grayfia stepped between them, and Ajuka followed suit beside her. Magic circles glowed in their hands, fully formed and levelled at the two of them, primed to fire the one type of magic that was mortally effective against both the Satan Lucifer and the Satan Leviathan...

"Don't make us call Lady Gremory and Lady Sitri again!"

... The Calling their Mothers spell.

"Tch. / Tch☆"

Demonic Power settled between them like ash, the last echoes of their almost clash sinking into the cozy brown carpet. "So…" Serafall's blue eyes slid back to him. "We settle this the usual way, then?"

"The usual way." Sirzechs nodded back, a thin smile tugging at his mouth. "I believe it's your turn to send the word?"

"It is. So, I guess it's neither peerage for now," she exhaled with a smile. "I better get everything ready. But we should convene here again! Doing the whole 'monitoring the situation as it unfolds' here has been a whole lot more fun than doing it from Aju-kun's command center!"

"Hrm." their researcher Satan just snubbed the comment.

"It definitely has!" Sirzechs-chan laughed as well. "I've been inspired by Falbium's genius. I think I want my command center designed like this too!"

"No, Sirzechs-sama," The no-fun maid immediately vetoed the idea. "It would be disastrous to your work efficiency."

Serafall just giggled at how the Crimson Satan pouted. "I'll go send word. Give everyone involved a warning in advance, but just a little though~" she said. And with a miniscule flex of her willpower, a pink magical circle flare up beneath her shoes. "Looks like Mal-kun will have a big surprise waiting for him. And also a smaller one for both Rias-chan and Sona-chan too."

"Good," Sirzechs-chan chuckled. "The new generation deserves a little excitement in their lives too."

"One last point of concern." Ajuka spoke up. "How closely should we have the Nebiros Boy monitored?"

Serafall just winked.

"Heh☆! I'm more concerned about other people approaching him. After all..." she twirled on the teleportation circle, letting the last thing they see be Magical Girl Levi-tan's smug smile. "... Mal-kun likes me too much to leave town before my letter reaches him☆~"


(Three days later) [The Griffin Grand Hotel]

A devil like me was left with my idle hands.

For three whole days, I was left alone with nothing to do but explore what [Intermediate Item Construction], [Bronze Creation], [Structural Analysis], [King of All Trades] had granted me: The world.

My takeover began when I ventured out into the city, using [Structural Analysis] on everything I could get my hands on.

TVs, vending machines, cash registers, cars and trucks that ran on demonic power generators, and everything in the local appliance center with all their fancy home appliances. More than a thousand blueprints permanently seared into my memory like a mental encyclopedia of magitechnology. Hadn't gotten my hands on some real magitech though. No airships or real weapons... No Sacred Gears either.

But it was enough.

Demonic Power was an untamed creature of the imagination after all. And in this, it was finally free to roam. Head stuffed to spilling with knowledge of all the magic that made things cool or heat or insulate or move in a thousand different ways. All that knowledge had barely settled before [King of All Trades] and [Intermediate Item Construction] picked apart all that raw knowledge like devil-buzzards on a carcass. Trying to fit together what they knew, extrapolating what they didn't, nearly came to blows when two different pieces fit the same slot.

And my brain was the whiteboard they used to fight over it all.

They filled it end-to-end. Scribbled the board full with thaumaturgic hierarchies, magical equations and around forty devil-university degrees more than a country-devil like me was ever supposed to hold. And once [King of All Trades] and [Intermediate Item Construction] settled and toasted their success...

There [Bronze Creation] was. The honest tradesman who'd been sitting quiet in the corner, nursing his beer, waiting with his toolbox until the eggheads and clean-vests finished with their theorizin' and the time came to actually make it all real.

And we did.

We rolled up our sleeves and made it all real.

By the end of the first night, the room had stopped being a hotel room and became my workshop.

Bronze lay everywhere. Solid pillars of amber-brown held up metal ribs that arched across the ceiling. Hooks and cranes sliding back and forth on them like a car body shop. Bronze tread plate covered the floor and completely obscured the patterned carpet. The desk had become a lathe, the coffee table a weapons rack, and the queen-sized bed lay forgotten underneath a massive bronze workbench with all the bronze doohickies and whatchamacallits that I'd formed to experiment and test my theories.

It still don't feel right: Having 'Theories' and 'Thesises' as if I'm some devil of science. But here I was anyway, barely having gotten a proper shut eye after three whole days of science work. Should have tired me out, except... it didn't. Even the fact that I hadn't learned any new spells didn't stop me. If anything, it only made me hungrier. Hungry for what I could learn. For what I could craft.

I would have gone longer, fabricated even more. But then, just an hour ago...

The Letter arrived.

Sitting back from my workbench now, I glanced over the rectangle of fancy parchment sitting at the edge of the workbench. It was a small thing now, but impossible to ignore how it got to me: sitting on a polished gold platter, hand-delivered by the general manager of the entire hotel, herself with her head bowed obeisantly as if I was an emissary from some greater power.

And in a way, I was.

Because handwritten in fine ink was a request, or maybe even an order, straight from a Satan herself.

The letter said that there's a train heading north, to the human world.

It said that there's a seat with my name on it.
​
The phone rang on the other side of the room.

"Get that for me," I muttered the verbal command as my hands went back to the workbench, giving my last project here its finishing touches.

The phone clacked softly as it was picked up entirely off the table. And heavy footfalls followed— a slow deliberate thuds of bronze striding on bronze—and crossed the room behind me. A shadow spilled across the far wall, swallowing the wallpaper and climbing toward the ceiling until the room felt smaller for it. Like an adult trying to navigate a kid's playhouse.

The receiver was lifted up from its cradle and held to my ear.

"Sorry to disturb you, sir," a young woman said, voice bright and careful on the other end. "But we're glad to inform you that your checkout's been processed ahead of schedule, and we have a complimentary taxi service to anywhere within Glasya-Labolas, if you wish to avail it."

I watched my reflection in the dark window as she spoke.

"I'd be much obliged, miss," I said. "I'm vacatin' now. So, if you'd be so kind and send that cab my way."

"Right away, sir."

The call ended. The receiver stayed held steady near my ear for a heartbeat longer before the massive hand lowered it back into place.

"Pack it up. Long haul mode." I said, and the words dropped into the room like some reality-warping spell.

Behind me, a strange symphony played out: the rasp and keen and creak of metal sliding across metal, layered over itself from every corner of the room. Seams unlocked, joints folded, and hard bronze seeming to turn to liquid. Streamed inward, vanishing into itself like it was being sucked into a vacuum cleaner going to nowhere. The air thrummed as Demonic Power rushed back into me, a sudden flood returning to its reservoir, hot and electric beneath his skin.

Then... silence.

I didn't need to glance back to confirm.

The room was ordinary again. Just another immaculate five-star hotel suite with its bed, carpeted floor, and floor-to-ceiling windows framing a Glasya-Labolas city skyline. All that was left was a single, bronze drag-along suitcase sat innocent on the carpet, only a bit larger than the hotel minifridge, its amber-brown surface brushed to a soft, expensive-looking sheen. The corners were tastefully rounded, and a tidy emblem of House Nebiros rested near the clasp.

All the bronze that I'd generated over the past few days reabsorbed and topped my Demonic Power Levels.

"Good," I stood up, drinking in the energy as I crossed the room, lifted it by the handle, and felt the reassuring flash of [Structural Analysis] that told me the springs had set just as designed. The whole thing coiled up tight like a rattlesnake in its den, ready to strike the moment it was called on.

The letter sat on the desk, thick parchment paper, folded it up and tucked it into my suit pocket.

The letter said that there's a place, out in the frontier, called Kuoh Town.

It said that there's people that I had to find.
​
And all that was left was...

I picked up my latest project off the desk and turned etched bronze once in my hands. Two barrels and no frills; Couldn't make anything fancier without more spells and magitech under my belt. I sighed. It was as good as it's gonna get right now. I slid a bronze Totem in each barrel—Hawk in one, Eagle in the other—and snapped it shut.

And as I walked out, dragging the large bronze luggage behind me, my finger traced in my pocket the folded edge of the letter.

And it said that there's work.

For a devil like me.

- A Deal with Satan End -
​

#848
Chapter 3: Double Bare Kings of Kuoh


Dear cutest little sister Rias,

You got this letter instead of a call, so you know what time it is! Serafall and I couldn't decide who deserved a prize again. So, it's up to you and Sona to find out who should get it.

Quick catch-up, then the interesting business.

Remember the Lucifer Six and how we all thought had been gone for good except for Grayfia's family? It turns out that they weren't permanently gone. A devil from one of them, House Nebiros, showed up in Glasya-Labolas a few days ago for the Middle-Class Devil Examination. And for a couple of reasons, he impressed all of us. Serafall even personally talked to him and found him to be very cooperative and even asked us to put him in a peerage. He's around your age, so we told him there's one in Kuoh Town.

That's right! You and Sona get to decide which of your peerages he'll officially join!

Work it out however you two usually do; I'm staying out of it just like I promised. Just keep in mind that although he's officially a Middle-Class Devil, he's effectively High-Class in every way that matters. In the exam, he also fought against Riser Phenex's Queen in a battle of magic and won with barely a scratch if that means anything to you. *wink* *wink* But we'll let you and Sona draw your own conclusions about his power and skills. Just watch out, he's cleverer than he looks.

Let us know when the two of you come to an agreement!

Lovingly,
Your Sirzechs-onii-chama


[Occult Research Club Room - Kuoh Academy]

Rias Gremory reached up into her crimson hair and nursed her growing headache.

"Onii-baka, pushing everything onto your little sister again. And just after midterms too! You know that we can probably just refuse, right?"

But the letter didn't reply to that threat, and Rias just sighed.

The clubroom sighed with her as she sat back into the couch's maroon leather. The midnight air so quiet that the candelabras sounded like they were whispering as they lit up the room in a soft orange. Mahogany furniture walls drank the light, varnish dark as old wine, and the velvet drapes pooled against the carpeted floor, folds swallowing any outside sound. Except for one.

Knocking came from the door, not someone from her peerage either: Too firm to be her graceful Akeno, too formal to be her relaxed Kiba, and too uniform to be her carefree Koneko. Besides, she didn't expect them for a few more hours, and with this letter in her hand?

There was only one person who could be that right now.

"Come in, Sona." Rias called out.

The Student Council President did, the dark mahogany door creaking open to let her slim frame, still dressed in Kuoh Academy uniform, slip inside. She held a clipboard to her chest, likely holding her own letter. And violet eyes behind violet rims landed on the letter held in Rias' fingers.

"Good, you received your letter." Sona noted, voice soft and business-like, but having known Sona for years, Rias could definitely hear how her words frayed just at the end from how worried she was. Could see the faint frazzle in her short dark hair.

"My letter barely says anything worthwhile," Rias said with a weary smile, almost apologising for it. "What does yours say?"

"Far less than I'd like either..." Sona sighed as she sat down on the couch across her. "My sister says that she and your brother are sending over the Pure-Blooded scion of one of the Lucifer Six. And despite being deserving of his own Evil Pieces, he strangely still wishes to join a peerage until he chooses to accept them. Which is soon. Nevertheless, Serafall-onee-sama made sure to stress that it is vitally important for Devilkind's interests that we at least be hospitable to him. How he's treated may directly affect whether his House reconnects with the wider Underworld as a partner or becomes a long-term problem. 'No pressure, but the four of us are counting on you.' my sister says. And I can somewhat understand her reasoning, even if I don't agree with having this considerable responsibility foisted onto us so suddenly."

"So, we're supposed to be the welcoming party, huh?"

Sona exhaled, clearly that wasn't the answer she was looking for. "May I assume your letter from Sirzechs-sama didn't supply any more details as to say who he is?"

"Sorry, mine has even less to say about him," Rias chuckled helplessly, holding up her own letter. "Only that he showed up a few days ago to take the Mid-Class Exam, and that he's strong enough that he won against Riser's Queen piece in the examinations in a duel of spells."

"Middle-Class Devil Examinations? Mine didn't include that information... But he's supposed to be the heir, right? High-Class should have been a given."

Rias just shrugged. "I don't know the reasons why, only that he won against Riser's Queen Piece."

"He won against Yubelluna?" she murmured, scribbling something onto her clipboard, her pen casting a long shadow on the candlelight. "I guess that would place him solidly at Mid-Class at least..."

"And he did it with barely a scratch."

Sona's pen stopped, and so did its long shadow. Then began moving again, faster this time. "I see... So, he's a High-Class Devil. And of all the recently matured Devils in our generation, Riser's peerage sits comfortably in the top ten in both performance and popularity polls. To have beaten a well-trained Queen piece like that in a duel easily..."

"You don't have to keep praising him so much, you know?!" Rias snapped.

Putting 'Riser' and 'Her being told what to do' in the same conversation never failed to grate at her. Whatever, her otou-san will be breaking off the engagement soon anyway. Not that it'll make her like him any less. If she could, she'd never look at anything even related to Riser Phenex. But Rating Games are too big of a deal, especially for their generation. And it was hard enough that she couldn't open the Rating Game section of Devitube without seeing his stupid face.

"Ah... right." Sona winced, "Sorry, I know it's been a sensitive topic."

Rias could only exhale. Sona had always been a tender puppy underneath that thin shell of business. And unlike Riser, she didn't deserve it.

"Sorry for bursting out like that. Just... forget it," She sighed again. "Going back to the topic now... Yes, I've watched a few of his games with Akeno. She estimated that she would definitely beat Riser' Queen in a head-to-head duel. But does it honestly matter? There's more to joining my peerage than just fighting ability, and I definitely wouldn't let someone join just because someone else told me I should, even if it is onii-sama. I think he and Serafall-sama just want to give us another 'Learning moment'."

"It does sound like their usual games." Sona agreed with a tired nod.

It was always the same old story.

Their well-meaning—sometimes overbearing, always annoying—older siblings wanted to impart a practical life lesson. But instead of just sitting down and talking about it like normal people, they always had to make it into a game! It's why they even had this arrangement in the first place: This joint management of Kuoh Academy. Their older siblings had wanted her and Sona to learn how to work together: Talking, coordinating and growing closer. And they did. They learned those lessons years ago, and she could appreciate each and every one of them too! It made handling things around Kuoh town so much easier when she could communicate well with Sona. Some of it even carried over to her own duties as King of her peerage.

Something still didn't make sense though.

Rias furrowed her brow. "What I really want to know is how this Lucifer Six heir is so important that they want us to accommodate him in either of our peerages?"

"Hmm," Sona tapped her pen on her chin in thought, "I believe Serafall-onee-sama mentioned that the Old Kings House won enormous support when 'they' convinced House Belphegor to rejoin through Roygun-san. But if the Four Satans' political faction can get one of the Six Houses of Lucifer to reintegrate? That'll be an unbelievable political victory. This heir might just be a once in a millennium opportunity."

"I see..."

She was not an unreasonable person. She was willing to do a lot for her brother, for the good of devilkind, and even for a stranger she didn't know. But she just drew a line at people dictating what she should do with her own peerage.

"So, how bad can it really be if we both refused him?"

"I think..." Her short-haired counterpart murmured. "... Sirzechs-sama and Serafall-onee-sama will be very disappointed, but they'll respect our decision and send him for someone else to evaluate and possibly accept into their peerage."

"Like Seek-chan?" Rias perked up, leather couch creaking under her. Was this their easy way out of the situation?

"... Yes, like Agrares-san," Sona voiced with just the slightest annoyed barb. "But you know their expectation as well as I do. If we can't say we made an honest effort, they won't accept our rejection."

"I know," Rias settled back, the excitement now gone. "I just wish they told us more about what kind of person this heir from House Nebiros is."

Sona's violet eyes flew almost as wide as her glasses. "House Nebi— Your letter says he's from House Nebiros?!"

Rias glanced down at her letter, and rubbed her head sheepishly. "Oops... I kind of skimmed over that part, didn't I?" She said through Sona's annoyed stare, "But... does that really change anything? Are they supposed to be a big deal compared to the other Houses in the Lucifer Six? I thought House Lucifuge, Grayfia-nee-sama's original House, was their leader and the most important one?"

"Yes, House Lucifuge was the most militarily decorated. but House Nebiros were the original Lucifer's dedicated research and development House from long before the Great War ever began. A good amount of devilkind's modern biomedical magisciences came from the Original Lucifer's House before it trickled down to the 72 Pillars. You can even say that they were collectively the Ajuka Beelzebub before Ajuka Beelzebub."

"A House full of Ajuka Beelzebubs..." Rias muttered.

She could see why getting this House back would be a big deal. The Underworld is already benefitting a lot from just one, but a whole house of him? She could just imagine an entire family of green-haired, cape-wearing super geniuses.

"So, this guy must be pretty smart too?"

"Likely so," Sona replied, already halfway down scribbling another column of notes in her clipboard. But Rias already knew the three most interesting items on that list.

"Respected bloodline, fighting ability, and brains on top of it. Make you wonder why he'd ever turn down getting his own—" She stopped. And suddenly, it all clicked for her. "Sona… tell me I'm not the only one getting a weird feeling about this."

"Now that you mention it… Yes, it does feel irregular." Her more bookish friend shifted the notes on her clipboard, papers rustling and eyes scanning them. "If we collate what we know: he deferred his right to Evil Pieces, lowered himself to take the Middle-Class Devil Exam—"

"—And then," Rias cut in, "He requested peerage placement from two Satans who, coincidentally, both have younger sisters with vacancies. Meaning us."

"But... Serafall-onee-sama has met him personally, and she doesn't overlook red flags."

"Unless she thinks we can handle it. Or she thinks it'd be funny," Rias countered. And Sona just sighed in response.

"Often both, actually."

And Rias knew that she'd convinced her. "So, then..." She sat back, leather creaking against her back. "... what's our strategy going to be, Sona?"

Sona's violet eyes shone hungrily at the word 'strategy.' "Our older siblings want us to evaluate his suitability for either of our peerages," her bespectacled friend said, excited now that she was in her element, "But given the circumstances, I suspect he might be as much of a candidate as he is a test or an object lesson. Sirzechs-sama's wording says that he's from House Nebiros. But it is just as likely, if not more likely, that he's one of those pretenders who we sometimes hear on the news. In either case, the lack of information is itself part of the test. Like an opponent declaring a move without us ever seeing the chessboard. So, that would be our first move: fully identifying the piece and deducing his intent. Once we have more information, we reconvene and develop our strategy accordingly."

"Sounds good. Very chess-like," Rias muttered, glancing at the walnut-wood clock on the wall, just past midnight, "Still, this is a lot of work just after midterms."

"I'm sure they'll find a way to compensate us for all these additional responsibilities."

"Oh, onii-san sure will!" Rias huffed.

"He will likely be arriving today." Sona glanced at her collection of notes. "I'll organize these so that we can brief our peerages in the morning."

Rias nodded. "And I'll call the station master. Have him on the look out for our 'prospective peerage member.' Kind of hard to do that since we don't know what he looks like, but I'm sure he's on a passenger list somewhere. Maybe delay him and buy us more time..." She said. Kuoh Station was busy this time of year, being the only station in this section of Japan that was authorised to cross the Earth-Underworld border. And though she may not be in charge of it, as heiress apparent of House Gremory, she could boss the staff around, just a bit. It was even more justified because her baka-onii-san was pushing some of his work onto her: Pitting her and Sona against this Nebiros heir.

"Well if there's nothing else..." Sona stood up, straightening her Kuoh uniform on her slender frame and nodded to her. "We'll meet an hour before homeroom?"

Rias smiled back. "See you then, Sona."

She had heart and Sona had brains. And between both of their peerages, on their home turf, she could say that they'd be able to handle any surprises sent there way. But... A part of her was still slightly nervous though. It always was when meeting a new heir or heiress to some 72 Pillar house. But this time, it was outside of balls, soirees and other social events. This was much more personal: In her territory and regarding her own peerage.

She had to take him seriously. Who knows what kind of sophisticated chessmaster the heir from the Devil house known for its super-geniuses was?


(Two hours later) [Kuoh Town Station]

Funny how, as the train slowed its run to my destination and those dark tunnel lights lit up my cabin longer as they passed by, I found myself wanting to just go back home and inbreed with my two sisters.

My dad told me this'd happen one fine evening way back when.

He said that there's a point in every male Nebiros Devil's life when he gets the urge to go home, bend his sister over a haystack and extend the family tree. I'd know for sure when it happens, he said. As if Satan himself pointed a finger at my sis' crotch and told me to get to work. I just didn't think that point would come this soon.


Feat Achieved! — Out on the Ole' Frontier
Cross the barrier between worlds and arrive in the Human World
+1 Bronze Gacha Ticket

...

[Bitch Breaker] |Uncommon Trait|
Sometimes all a bad attitude needs is a good fucking, you are an expert at correcting attitudes through sex correction.

...
​

The moment the train had come chugging out the tunnel between the Underworld and the Human world, I received the achievement and got this payout from the gacha ticket. And now, I can go make my parents proud by having raw, incestuous sex with my two twin sisters. And this time without the risk of CBT or pegging.

And ain't that the darndest thing?

I rode this far just to earn myself a reason to quit the trail. To just... turn around and head straight back home. Back to my small-devil-town in that cozy valley, back to the family farm and the room I grew up in, and back to the family. I'd just hang up my spurs and spend the rest of my days living quietly on the farm, extending the family line with my two sisters, putting buns in those tiny ovens until the end of time.

It sounded like a dream, but this was no time to get cold feet! What kind of devil on a hellish crusade would I be if I quit the moment I step foot in the Human World?

Besides, I'm sure I'll find a use for [Bitch Breaker] before I head home.


"Kuoh Station. End of the line. Platform 2 now boarding for Lucifaad Central."
​

The announcement sounded over the hissing of train brakes engaging and with just the slightest lurch... the whole train stopped fully.

"Well, better get to it." I muttered, settling the black cowboy hat onto my head and tugged it low.

Time to break some bitches, and meet with my new boss. Here's hoping I'd make a good first impression with her. And if luck keeps riding with me, it'd be half as fine as the one I'd left with the Satan Leviathan. She'd even done me a kindness and gave me a private cabin all to myself. More expensively decorated than my bedroom back home to boot—All fine woods, Persian carpets and silky couch pillows. It was a hell of a lot more comfortable than the freight car that I stowed away on getting out of Nebiros Town, that's for sure!

"One of these days, I'm gonna get my sisters a fancy train car like this..." I dragged my hefty, fridge-sized, bronze 'luggage' behind me. Thankfully, I was the only one ready in the 1st-class car and didn't have to squeeze past people getting off the train, but I did have some trouble once I got onto the bustling station platform: Dozens of people embarking and disembarking on Kuoh Station.

It was quite a collection of characters too!

There were people dressed like normal ningen with their jeans and t-shirts, and then there were the people in plate armour or wizard robes. Of course, I was in no place to judge, seeing as I was a devil-cowboy and dressed as such in my all-black seersucker suit and cowboy hat. In fact, I reckon I was the strangely dressed devil in the station. The most lost looking one too, I'd wager. Because I couldn't make heads or tails of the directions. Corridors going this way, elevators going that way, and everyone going every way.


"Kuoh Station. End of the line. Platform 2 now boarding for Lucifaad Central."
​

And just as I was looking for someone to ask for directions, a short, fat bearded old man came rushing down the platform. The uniform and the peaked cap on him, I vaguely recognised as the station master thanks to my sisters' place of work.

I met the station master's beady eyes and tipped my hat to him, "Can you—"

"I'm sorry, sir, but I've been assigned to assist a VIP," He said, eyes glued to the train that he was almost jogging in place to rush to. "You'll have to ask one of the other staff to help you."

"I see. Thanks for..." I managed to get out before he hurriedly waddled away like a duck with its tail-feathers on fire, "...your time?"

That was fine. I could find my own way.

With barely a thought, I eased [Byakugan] and [Structural Analysis] into my two 'ability slots.' Long range, 360-degree x-ray vision and the ability to gain a mental blueprint of any object/structure I touched, respectively. Byakugan showed me that this expansive train station was more than 150 feet—or about ten floors—below ground, and that there was also an entire labyrinth of underground facilities under Kuoh Town. Didn't tell me the quickest way up to the surface though, so I tried my luck with Structural Analysis and tapped the button on the closest elevator. That ability could reach just as far and maybe even farther than the Byakugan when it involved electrical wires or elevator cables after all.

The elevator's blueprint burned itself into my mind.

"Ah, this one goes straight to the ningen station!" I smiled as I stepped inside. The doors slid shut, then a gentle lurch as the elevator began its climb. The hum of cables filled the narrow space. And...

Unease began filling me up as the numbers counted down to ground floor. The reality of what was about to happen hit me. My hands fumbled for my double-barrel, Hawk and Eagle Totems resting warm in the bronze tubes.

I barely held it proper before the elevator stopped.

Then... the doors parted, slowly, as if reluctant to reveal what waited beyond.

I stepped out, gripping my gun and my 'luggage' tight.

The cool night air felt like a cold curtain I had brushed past. And after it came the glow of scattered streetlights lighting up quiet roads and closed storefronts. The town, or at least this part of it, was quiet after midnight. And above me, the night sky opened wide and endless, stars sharp and innumerable, stretching from horizon to horizon.

As a devil, it didn't feel right to be under the twinkling, star-studded firmament like this.

It felt unnatural, even a smidgen frightening.

Up there in the sky lies Heaven after all, with all its angel legions, the Ten Seraphim and the Four Great Seraphs. And now that I'm here on Earth, it feels like I'm walking across their front lawn, in full view of all their windows. Because Kuoh Town might be devil turf, but... 'In the beginning God created the heavens and the earth' were the very first words of the Holy Book. Earth as a whole was Heaven's territory, made for Humanity. Their private nature reserve where they let all the ningen run wild. Exorcists, Heaven's ningen guard dogs who were ordained by the Vatican, were wandering around with their light guns and light swords. But I wasn't all tense up over those, no, I was tense for something else.

I stood there, eyes darting at every star, knuckles white on my gun as I waited for it.

The moment any one of those twinkling star up there turns into a shining halo and a legion of those many-winged bandits comes swooping in with spears of light to banish me back to heck.

Seconds ticked by, then minutes.

But no one came and nothing happened. Just an empty street in a quiet, human town after midnight.

I glared up at the stars that cropped up my nerves.

"One day the shoe's gonna be on the other foot." I promised to the stars, and to myself. "And it's gonna be all the heavens that stared down in the Underworld's direction and feared me."

Today however was one big step forward towards that goal.

My [Byakugan], through the already sharp Devil eyes and powered by Demonic Power, cut through the dark and past the sleepy buildings away from the direction of Tokyo and found...

A school with an old vine-covered schoolhouse to the side, barely half a mile away.

Tuning my eyeballs, I took a peek through its wooden walls and found a poshly decorated room, candles lit and the shadows of four people moved around. Off to one side, a very buxom girl with a long dark ponytail brewed tea in a teapot. Another petite girl, her hair white as snow sat with an impassive expression as she snacked on a cake. A blond boy stood behind a leather couch, on which lounged the last one person in the group, her long crimson hair streaming past her shoulders.

Found 'em.

Well, I shouldn't keep them waiting.

Luggage in hand, I gave one last glance at the glittering Heavens above, one last whisper drifting into the dark as I went on my way

"One day. One day soon."


[Occult Research Club Room - Kuoh Academy]

He was never meant for a drawn out brawl, just a target too large and too dangerous to ignore. And it sure as heck was hard to ignore a big, mean bronze son-of-a-gun stampeding at you with metal fists the size of backpacks! If this was a videogame, he'd be the off-tank bruiser and I'd be the high-damage carry. Old Ichabod here was meant to give me all the time and space I needed to freely gun them down with my totems.

That is, until he ran out of juice in a few minutes of course. Minutes that you could count in one hand.

Now, it's gonna take me two whole days with [Bronze Creation] to gradually rebuild and power him up, though I could cut it down to about twelve hours if I feel like passing out for it.

It would have been so much easier if I actually fought back.

I could've won the fight if I tried. Rias had just been sitting there looking pretty with Koneko too! But that meant firing my totem gun, this same gun that I crafted explicitly to punch through the natural magic resistance of higher class beings. It's a blunt instrument of violence—it didn't exactly have a knob on it to make it less lethal. So, what was I supposed to do? Shoot both Satans' little sisters dead? That was about the dumbest thing I could have done. I even gave it an order before high-tailing it: 'Don't target Rias or Sona.'

The second dumbest thing would have been killing any member of their peerages.

So, it was a real stroke of luck that I slapped that anti-killsteal directive on my automaton when I crafted it! Kills were reserved for me, as it should. Old Ichabod the Imperfect was just there to rough them up and tire them enough for me to shoot 'em dead.

"Heck, if they'd surrendered or flew away, Old Ichabod would have just stood there until he ran out of juice!" I guffawed. "Speaking of... I gotta fix that sometime."

But as it stood, that anti-killsteal feature ended up saving their lives. Otherwise… he'd have twisted their pretty little necks like those plastic bottlecaps, or played wishbones with their limbs. It was a nasty thing fueling an automaton with Demonic Power. It didn't make him sentient, but you don't have to be sentient to be sadistic. Because if those bronze practice dummies I'd set him loose on a couple days before could have screamed, they definitely would have.

But Rias' and Sona's peerage were all devils. Reincarnated or not, they're built from sturdier stock now. They'll be back on their feet in a few days. Yep, they'll be back to hunting me down in no time at all.

And next time, I probably won't have the Imperfect with me.

Setting the sphere aside, I laid back in my sticks-and moss bed, munching on my rabbit-on-a-stick and drinking in the increased regeneration of [Nature's Child], just in time to get another surprise.


Feat Achieved! — Double Bare Kings (High-Mid-Class)
In a single battle, fight and defeat two peerages until only their Kings remain. On your own. Without getting hit once.
+1 Platinum Gacha Ticket

...
​

More than the surprise of a platinum ticket....

A date with the Satan Leviathan was the same as beating what was essentially a middle-class-devil peerage?

I guess... one is just a single test of charisma, practically a charming smile and a single question, and the other was a whole brass can of whoopass. So, it could be that combat and relationships achievements are 'graded' differently. Then add the 'On your own' and 'Without getting hit once' modifiers and who really knows at that point? I can't tell from where I'm currently standing neither.

"Please let it be a good roll this time." I muttered, despite already knowing it was gonna fall on deaf ears.

The platinum ticket burned away, and....


[Neferpitou] |Epic Familiar|
Hunter x Hunter - As a Chimera Ant Royal Guard, Pitou is an extremely powerful Nen user with a massively superhuman physique and agility as well as incredible mastery over Nen. Alongside her extreme sensory abilities, she possesses the ability to puppeteer corpses to use as minions and summon Doctor Blythe, a potent healing ability that requires her to stand still and lower her guard to restore a target over time. Her trump card allows her to puppeteer her own body to strengthen herself.

...
​

... A cat girl was suddenly in my bed, snuggled up into my side. Fully dressed in blue overcoat and tight orange shorts.

Spoiler: Nerferpitou

She just stared at me, not saying anything. Just big red eyes blinking slowly as cats do. And seeing as things were already awkward for a first meeting/snuggle-in-bed, I took a chance, slowly reached up and stroked the downy-soft, snow-white hair between those cat ears.

"My King," Neferpitou said with a wide, happy and fanged smile, eyes closed and leaning her head up into my palm, "I am unworthy of your touch, but if your humble servant may make a selfish request... Please don't stop nyaa~"

I indulged her, just lightly running my fingers through her scalp. And a literal purr was her response, her chest rumbling against my side. I grinned.

Well, she wasn't a horse that I could ride off into the sunset, but a cat was fine too. Having an extra pair of capable hands and eyeballs was gonna be a big help. And I don't know if it was the good gacha roll, or [Nature's Child] charging me up faster now that I'm lying down near the earth, or the calming effect of having something soft to stroke. But...

... I'm feeling better with my situation already!

Well, I'll still up Four Satans creek without a paddle if this misunderstanding with two heiresses/Satans' little sisters ain't resolved soon. But I just knew that, any hour now, the Satan Leviathan is definitely gonna receive my letter for help!


[Lilith City - Underworld] (Three days later)

Serafall settled her favourite pink monitor headphones over her pink magical girl cap. The recording studio smelled faintly of papers and her cheerful floral perfume. Scripts lay neatly stacked on the table in front of her, and fan mail sat in a pastel basket at her elbow.

She swung the microphone close, tapped it a few times.

Her blue eyes glanced at the studio manager behind the glass. He gave a thumbs up, and Serafall flicked a double thumbs-up with her fingerless black gloves right back at him. The sign lit up a bright red.


ON AIR
​

"And we're back!" She chirped into the microphone. "Magical Girl Levi-tan☆ coming to you live from Hellywood Heights! And you know what time it is~ Time for magical fan correspondence! If you wrote a letter to me, today might be the day your fate is sealed! Ehe~☆!"

She struck a cute pose in her pink magical girl outfit for the benefit of the unseen crowd.

"Let's see what we have today~!"

The microphone caught the soft rustle of paper as she dipped her fingers into the basket and plucked an envelope that felt the most 'right.' Held it high before she looked at what she managed to pick out.

Her smile widened. "Oh! We've got one from a new listener☆! Lev-tan's favourite!"

"Dear Satan Leviathan," she read aloud, then puffed her cheeks and frowned. "Mou~ I keep telling you all to make it out to Magical Girl Levi-tan☆~ Satan Leviathan is so formal!"

She cleared her throat, sliding back to reading. "' Dear Magical Girl Levi-tan☆, I did as directed in your last letter.' Eh☆?" She tilted her head cutely despite it being a radio show, muscle memory now from centuries in front of the camera. "What do you mean I wrote you back? Aren't you a new listener? Ah, you must have very strong delusions! That's good☆! The Underworld needs more creative people like you!"

Serafall laughed lightly into the microphone. A practiced beat to let the listeners absorb her lines. Then her cadence slowed, just a fraction. A soft reset of pace.

"'I believe there's been some manner of misunderstanding as they took one look at my demeanor and now they're all trying to kill me.' Uh… what kind of fan letter is this? 'I eagerly await your prompt reply. Sincerely yours, Mal—' Oh."

"Oh."

- Double Bare Kings of Kuoh End -​

Chapter 4: The Fantastic Political Sh☆tstorm of Magical Girl Levi-tan☆!


[Occult Research Club Room - Kuoh Academy]

Sona did not know how, but word had somehow reached the Underworld, and the Satans...


"By the direct command of the Four Satans of the Underworld, all hostilities will cease. Effective immediately."
​

... They had sent Grayfia.

Sona froze in her seat on the couch, teacup still steaming in her hands mid-sip by her emergence from that sudden magic circle. Even Rias put down her phone for what seemed like the first time in days.

The light died down, and she stepped forth.

The blood red gaze of the Underworld's strongest Queen and the right hand of the Satan Lucifer settled on them. And even more intimidatingly, Grayfia was in her maid outfit. Her blue, white and frilly outfit stood starkly against Rias' moody clubroom and signifying that she was here on official capacity. And if she was the one dispatched for the task at hand, Sona knew that it meant the Satans had deemed the situation serious enough for everything short of rising from the Underworld to handle it themselves. She especially did not like the tone in her voice when the Grayfia delivered the Four Satans' command. Nor how her pale, slender hands were not folded in front of her, but by her side and straightened as if on the brink of casting magic.

It almost looked like the Underworld's strongest Queen piece arrived ready to enforce compliance. Immediate compliance.

"Onii-sama sent you, Grayfia?" Rias found her voice first, in a casually annoyed tone too. "And what does he mean 'stop fighting?' Does he know what that Nebiros guy did?!"

The silver-haired maid released them from her gaze and scanned the rest of the room, her voice filling the quiet. "Both Rias and Sona bear no signs of any recent harm," she noted aloud, clearly talking to someone through a communication spell. "However, the Nebiros youth is not in attendance."

Rias frowned. "We don't know where—"

"It is good that you are impatient with your answers." Grayfia calmly interrupted her. "As the Satans are similarly impatient for you to surrender all the answers you have to them."

"What do you mean by that, Grayfia?"

The older woman said nothing. And both Sona and Rias could only watch as she closed the curtains over the windows and shut out the bright sunny day outside. Only when all natural light was drowned out did she turn to them.

A snap of her fingers, and all the candelabras lit up.

So did the entire room in fact.

The official sigil of the Satan Leviathan shone like neon lights from the carpeted floor. And from it, Sona witnessed a hologram of her onee-sama shimmer into existence, dressed in her usual fluttering pink magical girl costume. Life-sized and life-like, if not for the faint, off-pink shade coloring her entire body, it was like she was actually in the room with them. Except something was wrong. This time, her normally playful older sister didn't pose or wink or smile or flash her panties. In fact, it looked like they'd caught her in the middle of pacing back and forth.

Brilliant blue eyes immediately locked onto her.

"Sona!"

Her Serafall-onee-sama half-shouted. Not 'Sona-tan' or even 'Sona-chan', just her name. In fact, she has never seen her sister look so... anxious, confused and almost angry, but definitely frustrated. Her older sister's hologram rushed forward, almost to her face, gloved hands reaching for her as if wanting to shake her by the shoulders as she demanded.

"What is happening up there?! Where is he?! Did you kill him?!"

"He attacked Koneko!" Rias blurted out instead, "Of course we were forced to stop him!"

Serafall whipped her head towards Rias so fast that her dark twintails whipped in the air. Pure horror in the Satan's voice. "So you DID kill him?! The first guest from the Lucifer Six that the 72 Pillars had in over five hundred years! We've barely had him over for a WEEK, and now he's dead?! By our own hands?!"

"He's not dead. He got away!" Rias protested, her red eyebrows furrowed on her confused face. "Why are you so concerned by him? He's the one snuck that golem—"

"He snuck a what?!"

"May I suggest..." Grayfia interjected smoothly, her eyes drifting toward the papers spread across the dark wood of the coffee table, "that Miss Sitri provide her full account, rather than this piecemeal presentation of information?"

All eyes in the room immediately went to her, and Sona stiffened on her couch seat, sweating under their stares and stumbling for her words. "I-it's not yet complete! I haven't even finished the first draft, and there's still some inconsistencies—"

But her older sister, as Grayfia said, was impatient.

"Sona, report."

"... Very well," She stood up, fixing the papers as her audience waited. As Student Council President, it was almost routine for her to deliver these kinds of reports actually: administrative reports and event reports among others. The familiarity made it slightly more bearable. Slightly.

As for that night's events, they were easy to enunciate and even easier to follow. Short even.

They received their letters and met together in this very room to combine their intel. Soon after the meeting, the Nebiros heir arrived suddenly, forcing Rias to receive him in this building... wherein he immediately attacked Koneko with a mind spell. Rias attempted to deescalate the situation, but he attempted to misdirect her by pleading ignorance while continuing his attack, forcing her to physically stop him. The golem was then activated, and injured Akeno and Yuuto in between more attempts at deceit. It was at that point when she arrived with her peerage, causing the Nebiros to quit the field, but left the killer golem behind.

"And this brass construct..." Serafall interrupted, her tone unsure, "It managed to beat all of you? At once?"

"It almost did," Sona replied. It stung to admit her own peerage's failure out loud. But it had been their first real life-and-death battle, and the thought of losing someone from her peerage... Thank Satan that they didn't lose anyone. Sona took a deep breath and said, "Tomoe managed to record almost the entire battle, except for the start when it was just Rias."

"Show me."

Nodding, Sona pulled out the blue, marble-like recording crystal where the recording was inscribed into.

Over the coffee table, the magical panel played out the scene. From her peerage's aerial approach to Kuoh Academy where Akeno's lightning flashed for the first time... to the point where Tomoe's leg was broken and recorded the entire battle as she sat against the goal post... and to the slow losing battle of attrition as their peerage members fell under those gargantuan fists, one-by-one. Seeing it played out on a display felt surreal in Sona's opinion, as if it was some kind of action movie with actresses and stunt doubles rather than the life-and-death battle that it really was.

Nevertheless, she could only wait and wince quietly with Rias as the Satan Leviathan watched along. A kaleidoscope of colours flashing over her sister's pale face as if she was really there in the room, rather than watching it from a screen all the way from her office down in the Underworld. The sounds of battle filling Rias' clubroom.


"—dammit! Let's talk this over! Gremory, call off your knight!" ... "Gosh darn cavalry!" ... "Don't let him get away!" ... "Give me time to charge up!" ... "Tactical shift! Interdiction maneuvers! Buy time for Rias now!" ... "AKENO!" ... "No... Tsubaki! Retreat! That's an order!" ... "Don't... underestimate... US!"
​

The recording stopped abruptly just as the effects of Rias' Power of Destruction sundered the brass golem, because Tomoe, the one recording, saw that the battle had been won and promptly lost consciousness.

"Grayfia, send this recording to Ajuka for analysis. Top priority." Serafall quietly spoke up, and the maid picked up the recording crystal between her slender fingers. And with only a brief glow of a teleportation circle, it disappeared from her hand.

"It is done."

"Sona, was that the only copy?"

"I have a backup," She said, fishing the spare crystal from her skirt pocket. "Here."

"Grayfia."

"Yes, my Satan Leviathan." This time, the maid crushed the crystal in her hand. Followed by a flash of lightning then ice then fire that left nothing but very fine sand which she sprinkled in the trash bin in the corner. The only real proof of that night's events was now with the Satan Beelzebub.

Sona supposed that being in the possession of a Satan was as safe as it could be then.

Her Serafall-onee-san looked calmer now, but not by much. More distracted by her thoughts, but still taut. Her pink lips pressed together in a thin line, a curled finger pressed over them, and her blue eyes looking at something far off.

Rias tentatively raised her hand. "Is this a bad time to mention—"

"Yes, it is," Serafall bluntly stated. "Save it for later, Rias."

Sona gulped. No, she was clearly still upset.

"There's more I haven't included in the report, but... everyone's expected to make a full recovery by tomorrow." She decided to conclude on a more optimistic note, fixing her glasses to hide the look of raw relief in her eyes. Thank Satan that reincarnated devils healed just as fast, she didn't think she could bear looking her injured peerage in the eyes for longer after almost leading them to their deaths.

"I'm glad that you two weren't hurt," Serafall finally said with a sigh, "and that neither of you lost anyone from your peerage."

Sona sat back down, deciding not to point out that she didn't look so glad.

"But correct me if I'm wrong," Serafall said, her gaze shifting towards Rias, "The moment he greeted you all, Koneko started screaming. You accused him of being behind it. Then he pleaded his innocence and held back from deploying his super tough construct until after you tried to cut off his head. And even after you tried to kill him, even after the fight had started, he tried to talk some more instead of chasing after the Rook piece that he had been trying to stealthily assassinate with mind-altering magic... right in front of you? If this was an attempt at someone's life, it's the most random, nonsensical attempt I've seen in more than five hundred years!"

"But it totally was him!" Rias insisted, "You should have seen how Koneko-chan reacted; all of it was directed at him from the moment he saw her! This Nebiros guy can't not be behind this!"

"It sounds like you were convinced that it was him even before Koneko reacted!"

"But to be fair, onee-sama..." Sona jumped in to back up her friend. "... Judging by the circumstances surrounding his declinement of his Evil Pieces, we had reason to believe he was harbouring ill-intentions. Every illogical action he took can be explained away once viewed from that lens."

But her Serafall-onee-sama shot her an annoyed look, cheek puffing up in a severe pout. "Mou! That's just a fancier way of saying that you two made way too many assumptions before even meeting him! And even if all that was true and we really did intend to test how you handle a horrible person as a prospective peerage member, that was like twenty-percent of the letter! Did you just ignore the other eighty-percent where we told you that this was really, really important for the Underworld?!"

"Oh..." Sona winced at that, suddenly not feeling so confident.

She hadn't thought of that. In the back of her mind, she had assumed from the beginning that this entire affair with Nebiros Heir was all a test with a single question. And the answer she had written down was: 'The Nebiros Heir was evil and threatened her friend, thus he needed to be stopped.' Except... it's now revealed that it hadn't been a single-question test, and in her haste, she had left all the other questions blank.

Truly a nightmare scenario for her.

In contrast however, Rias, in typical Rias fashion, still wasn't about to back down. The redhead sat on the couch across from her, her arms crossed over her chest as she fearlessly (and annoyedly) staring Satan down. And just like how it was with the Riser betrothal, the answer she arrived at was that her freedom and her peerage were threatened.

And to her, that answer was the only one that mattered.

Damn any other questions.

"Well, everyone from otou-sama said the same thing about my engagement with Riser!" Sona's crimson-haired friend fired back, "He went on and on about how my eventual marriage to Riser was important for the Underworld, how it would change the political landscape with a Gremory-Phenex union, and all that garbage!"

"But this time, it really was that important!" Serafall actually shouted, ripping off her winged magical girl hat to run her fingers frustratedly through her dark hair as she anxiously pacing left and right. "We had a lot counting on this, you know! Argh! This is really bad!"

Sona threw a worried look at Rias.

Because when the Head of Foreign Relations for their entire world (incumbent for the past five hundred years) stamped her feet like a kid in a tantrum and described the situation as 'really bad' because of their actions... Yes, it was time to start getting worried.

"You have no idea what this means for us!" Her older sister paced even faster now, her blue eyes flicking as if seeing the consequences flash in her vision, "Now, the Old Satans Faction are gonna have—! And the Grigori are going to keep—! Urgh! And even if Mal-kun wasn't as brilliant, if word of this gets out the fallout would ruin us! It'll be 1689 all over again! But this time, even Aju-kun and Falbi are going to be super upset over this! A mess enough for Four Satans! We're gonna have this hanging over our heads for the next five hundred years! Maybe longer! In all the negotiations going forward with every lost House thinking of rejoining the 72 Pillars, they're gonna ask us 'Didn't your sisters try to murder the last guy who tried to join your side?'"

"We uh... failed though?" Sona said weakly, and her older sister paused in her pacing just long enough to throw a tired look at her and Rias.

"But you tried, Sona-chan. You two really tried, didn't you?" She said with a sigh, "He's probably run back to his House' lands by now, telling his whole family that no one should ever rejoin the 72 Pillars again."

Sona glanced down to the papers in her hands. "Onee-sama," She hesitantly began, "I wasn't able to include it in my report, but... we've been keeping in contact with him."

Serafall froze, foot mid-step and eyes wide. "Wait, you mean to say that... he's still somewhere in Kuoh Town? And he's still willing to talk?!"

"Yes?"

"Mal-kun, I knew I could count on you!" Her older sister smiled wide in relief, "If we have his cooperation, then... Yes, this could work! How does he contact you?!"

Sona shuffled to the unfinished pages of her papers. "He's been sending local birds that he somehow controls to act as a messenger for recording spells. The first bird, a brown-eared bulbul, arrived at noon after the battle, but it was uh... disposed of before we could talk properly," She glanced over to Rias who had blasted the first bird with her Power of Destruction the moment it began transmitting his voice. "The second bird, a Japanese paradise flycatcher, arrived a few hours after. He said that he would 'give us time to cool down some more before talking.' We are expecting the third bird sometime today."

"Mm, the situation can definitely still be salvaged! We just need a strategy..." Serafall said, pink outfit already swaying as she went back to pacing. "Since no one else has seen the fight or heard the whole story yet, we can still repackage the narrative and prevent a diplomatic disaster! Maybe even outright flip this and look better in the process!"

"You... you can just do that?" Sona muttered incredulously, "After what you said about the Old Satan Faction and the Grigori and everything?"

Her Serafall-onee-sama just winked at her.

"I sure can, Sona-chan☆!" She raised a manicured finger. "First, compensation! We'll need Mal-kun to accept it publicly. So, even if he changes his mind later on for whatever reason, he can't hold it against us since he's already accepted. However we can't just call it compensation, because that would make people ask what we're compensating him for. Instead, we'll make it some kind of bilateral gesture. Maybe a joint thaumaturgical research venture with my and Aju-kun's supervision? We'll find something appropriate. But either way, we'll be able to repackage the apology gift for accidentally almost-murdering him as the start of a cooperative relationship with a Lost House. No one will bat an eye! And even better, this joint venture with Mal-kun will be our foot-in-the-door for a better relationship with House Nebiros down the line!"

The Satan in pink reached the end of the room, turned around sharply, her dark twintails whipped in the air as she paced the other way, another finger raised.

"Second, the political fallout. Even with Mal-kun and his House appeased, rumours over the incident will still irreparably damage our standing. So, we'll need to get ahead of the rumour mill. Denying it would be the amateur move, so we're going to do what the humans call a 'Modified Limited Hangout.' We'll volunteer the information that something went wrong ourselves! But instead of it being a diplomatic blunder ending in an outright attempt on his life, it was a... misunderstanding that was settled through a spar."

"A... spar?" Sona parroted before she could stop herself.

"It was a very enthusiastic spar," Serafall just nodded in agreement. "We'll frame it as proof of the 72 Pillar's strength: Just the new generation of Devils being strong and fit and raring to go☆! It'll be a hit with the Great King faction, and the Angels and the Grigori will just shake their heads. Just Devils being Devils after all, so it shouldn't affect any peace talks either!"

"Then for the final touch, we'll emphasize how swiftly we moved to clarify matters, especially how I'm personally invested in ensuring his safety. Reassert that the Four Satans are committed with the building bridges that were tragically severed in the Civil War five hundred years ago! He's definitely not going to be joining either of your peerages now. So, you don't have to worry about that anymore either!"

Serafall stopped pacing and twirled in place, ending with a cute pose and bright smile aimed at her.

"And just like that, we'll have House Nebiros' expertise on our side, the Lost Houses will still be willing to rejoin, and Devilkind as a whole will even come out of this mess looking slightly better!"

Sona was in awe.

She knew that her older sister was always smarter than she looked. She had to be, seeing as she had been the Satanic Head of Foreign Relations for the past five hundred years. But she'd never seen it demonstrated so vividly. It was like watching a chess grandmaster turn—in a single move—what would have been a game-losing blunder into an advantage; a tactical mastertroke that only the greatest in their craft could pull off. But more than that, it was seeing her older sister fix the mess that she made so elegantly that gave Sona a new appreciation for what she is able to do. And for the first time in a long while, Sona thought to herself.


'My onee-sama is so cool.'
​

Sona hung her head low in apology. "Sorry for doubting you, onee-sama..."

But a holographic hand simply slid beneath her chin and tipped her face upward. And there, her beautiful and capable Serafall-onee-sama stood over her, shining down a brilliant smile.

"You worry too much, Sona-tan☆" she said lightly, "It's very easy to survive a scandal when you're the one deciding how the story gets told! So we're fine, as long as no one else sees the video before we can impart our own spin onto it. That's why even Aju-kun will destroy his copy when he's done with it!"

"You're right, onee-sama. I see what you—"

Then, in the corner of their eyes, Rias tentatively raised her hand, and Serafall smiled graciously. "Right, sorry to keep you waiting. What did you want to ask, Rias-chan?"

"I already posted my copy on the Devilnet."

...

"... what?"

"I posted the video of the fight on my official Devitube account," Rias clarified with a small shrug. "I thought if everyone saw how he's the one who attacked us with his golem, he wouldn't get away with it like Riser does."

"And how long has the video been up?" Serafall's voice came out calm. Very calm.

(Meanwhile) [Woods near Kuoh Town]

Neferpitou's ears flicked at the curious sound that came echoing through the forest, sounding like it came from that place.

With that strange sound came a strange feeling, a burst of someone's presence rolled through the treeline and spilled over the hill like a cold, creeping mist. The unmistakable tremor of a power so vast and dense that she could almost feel it with her En. It was enough to send every strand of white fur on her tail puffing up. And only when she was certain, absolutely certain, that the source was beyond her area of concern, did she lower it.

There would be time for omens and distant threats; for now, something came first. Always came first.

And that was her King, Malal Nebiros.

She deserved no praise for such a catch. But despite her words, her King's hand still found and stroked that spot on her head, between her ears, that made her bones loosen and her chest rumble in a rolling purr.

Warm.

Her King was warm. It was the only word to describe his presence and his touch. Warm like her egg before she emerged from it. Warm like the seconds after waking up from a restful nap. Warm like the golden rays of sunlight streamed down through the thick forest canopy at that very moment. And she had experienced much of the world.

Her nose twitched, and she smelled a thousand different scents from a thousand different pitiful prey. Hopping, prancing, stalking and burrowing all around them. All drifting through and mixing with the thousand scents of leaves, flowers, bark, soil and clear forest air.

Her ears turned, and she heard the rustle of every leaf and the creak of every branch. She heard the singing chirps of every bird and the furtive footsteps of every landborne prey. The rumbling thunder of humans and their machines echoing in the distance.

Time!

"First time I've heard of it." I shrugged, unbothered. "But... Is this going to hurt my chances with getting access to tomes and grimoires? I don't care much about public relations, but it did open doors. Doors to private libraries to be specific."

She blinked her blue eyes. "That's what you're concerned about?"

"I don't mind being the villain in her version of the story." I replied with a shrug. "Like I've said before, I'm not chasing fame or fortune; achievements are what matters to me."

"That's quite the—" She stopped mid-sentence, eyes narrowing slightly. "Wait. Say that first part again."

"I don't mind being the villain in her version of the story."

Serafall's blue eyes looked past me for a moment, calculating as if numbers were written on the trees around us. Then she said, almost muttering to herself. "If I'm being honest, I've taken the brunt of Rias's stunt. The rumours and the whispers and the potential loss of credibility...it's mostly fallen to me to fix it. And she's definitely made it difficult☆. However..."

The light shone in her blue eyes as she looked at me,

"If you're willing to go that far for me, you can help me fix this all in one move. Yes, I know we already owe you a considerable amount of compensation, but if you help me clean up this mess… I'd be willing to increase that compensation. I'm going to need Sirzechs-chan's approval for this and maybe Zeoticus' as well, but... how would you like to have two Satans in your debt? And get back at Rias in one fell swoop☆?"

I met her stare without hesitation. "I'm listening."


(Later) [Kuoh Academy]

The teleportation circle dimmed beneath my boots in pink light as their purpose, delivering me here, was fulfilled. The geometric sigils of the Satan Leviathan turning on the floor, slowing before completely disappearing. And for the first time in my life, I set foot inside the Occult Research Club, in all its dark, moody glory.

The air smelled like someone spilled a pot of tea on old paper.

On one side of the coffee table, seven girls including Sona Sitri herself, stiffened at my appearance. And on the other side, the crimson-haired king of the club froze mid-motion. Her mouth parted. For a single second, Rias Gremory looked entirely unprepared in her Kuoh Academy uniform. Then she surged to her feet, her arm snapping up, finger leveled at me like a drawn blade.

"Why is he here?!" she demanded.

Beside me, the Satan Leviathan stepped forth in her pink magical girl garb, looking reenergised as if drawing strength from Rias' outrage like a flower under the sun. She raised one hand with airy composure like gesturing a reporter to quiet down rather than reigniting a political landmine.

"A formal investigation is currently underway," Serafall said lightly, her detached though carrying a faint breath of smugness. "Mal-kun's Evil Pieces—previously approved for issuance—are now officially on hold. And until the investigation concludes, he will be confined within the borders of Kuoh Town. Unless personally escorted, of course."

Rias' glare sharpened.

"And as for your question," Serafall continued, her smile never faltering. "He has come to formally issue you a challenge. An unofficial Rating Game. To be enacted once his Evil Pieces are granted."

"What?!" Rias' voice almost cracked form shock. "What for?! You just said he's under investigation!"

"It does not concern the incident in question," Serafall replied smoothly, though just a bit too happily seeing Rias' furious reactions. "The inquiry pertains to your public remarks on DeviTube. Slander or Libel. Whichever term best fits the situation. Regardless, it has escalated into an official dispute between House Gremory and House Nebiros. As Satanic Head of Foreign Relations… this matter is beyond my jurisdiction. Though I have informed Zeoticus and Sirzechs-chan, not that any of them can or would stop this match. Not when it's perfectly legitimate."

Silence spread outward.

"Howdy," I tipped my black cowboy hat at her. "I came here with peaceful intentions. But somehow, I ended up cast as the outlaw."

Rias' blue eyes flashed angrily. "Because you are one!" she snapped. "You're the one who attacked Koneko and dragged everyone into this mess!"

I nodded slowly, conceding a minor point in a debate I had already lost days ago. "Well," I said quietly, "that's exactly why I'm here: if you're dead set on painting me as the bandit… I suppose I'll accept the part. And if I'm the bandit,then I may as well make off like one."

"What... are you talking about?"

Serafall's expression did not change. "If he loses, he'll plead no contest to the investigations and compensate House Gremory an amount commensurate to the emotional distress caused," she said, right before her eyes glossed over Yuuto and Akeno. her voice curling to an almost teasing threat. "But if he wins..."

I stopped at the center of the room, smiled at the poor crimson-haired girl. "... If I win, I'll be getting myself one of your peerage members. To be chosen at my leisure."

The air went dead, quiet enough to hear the candeflames flicker... then the shouting began.

The Fantastic Political Sh☆tstorm of Magical Girl Levi-tan☆! End ​

The six dice options were 1) Rias's Peerage, 2) Sona's Peerage, 3) Seekvaira's Peerage, 4) Other Peerages (Sairaog's/Roygun's/even Serafall's peerage though this would have been much delayed), 5) Start his own Peerage, and 6) Independent. As you have probably read, we landed on 'Independent' or 'No Peerage.' (I was hoping for Mal to join or have Rias/Sona trade him to Seek's peerage to be honest due to the Gundam angle). So, that's 20k-30k words of building up joining a peerage straight down the drain. I almost disregarded the dice roll for this, but I'll stick to my guns and the rules of the exercises.

I know. It's super unrealistic for Rias to... post her L's online. No one does that IRL. People don't just post videos of themselves committing crimes on their social media for internet sympathy points, and they definitely wouldn't post any classified military/government documents. /s Lolololol.
