Chapter 5: Rias Gremory's Number One Hater


[Occult Research Clubroom - Kuoh Academy]


"—And up next, for your midnight news, the Gremory-Nebiros incident: Youthful rambunctiousness or old wounds resurfacing? Right after these commercial messages!"
​

"I'm done," Rias announced, slapping down the Japanese literature homework onto the dark wood coffee table.

Her pen, she all but tossed aside in favor of her phone. However, she growled impatiently as advertisements flashed on her screen, keeping her from what she wanted to see: the midnight news, one of the Underworld's most popular 'morning' TV programs. It wasn't her usual watch—she was almost strictly a Devitube user while this was more her parents' type of show—but she couldn't NOT watch something that talked about her current situation, even if it did grate on her nerves. All this talk of 'misunderstandings' and 'old wounds.' She found it unbelievable that there was even a question about what happened that night, right in this very building, against her friends!

Paper rustled.

"Let me see..." Sona's whisper drifted through her candlelit clubroom.

In contrast to her, Sona didn't look any worse than usual as she sat across from her. Well-rested violet eyes behind violet-rimmed glasses scanned the homework she'd slapped down. And also unlike her, it looked like her friend even had the spare time to memorize the correct test answers too. Not an answer key in sight as Sona's pen glided down the paper, leaving red checks on every item.

"Perfect score. Good work," Sona nodded to her. "You won't get trouble for any absences incurred now."

"Great, now I can be anywhere except the place I actually want to be."

There were always times when they'd have to go to attend another boring social event in the Underworld on a school day, just the inevitable overlap between schoolwork and Devil duties. But they've thankfully managed it through Sona administering and grading their own schoolwork in advance. So far, no one—school staff included—has dared to question the integrity of the Student Council President; even Rias implicitly knew that Sona would sooner restart the Great War before she allowed anyone to cheat on a test.

Rias settled back into the dark leather couch with her phone, and scoffed. "If only otou-sama believed in me as much as the teachers do in you, I wouldn't even need to take all these absences in the first place."

"There are worse things than having to attend remedial etiquette classes in your family's own castle, Rias."

"Maybe on its own, it's not," She replied, not looking away from her glaring contest with the advertisements, "But I know it's just an excuse to keep a closer eye on me. They've already locked me out of my Devitube account, and they definitely won't let me browse while remedial etiquette is going on. I have a feeling that this might be my last chance to see the news myself, instead of hearing it second hand."

Sona's eyes went to the phone in her hand, and she sighed. "But you know that it will only upset you more, right?" She spoke gently, "Maybe it would help you more if you kept it out of your mind, and just enjoyed your time with Milicas, Venelana-oba-san and everyone else. Spending time with family always helps."

"My peerage is part of my family." Rias growled.

"Sorry, I could have worded that better..." Sona murmured. "But just know that Serafall-onee-sama is doing her best to set all this right."

"She can start by throwing that Nebiros bastard in the deepest, darkest jail in the Underworld!" Rias huffed, but before she could go on a fresh tirade about how much she was coming to hate the guy...


"And welcome back to 'Good Midnight Lucifaad!' "
​

... the ads finished playing.

Rias leaned in close, almost pressing her nose against her phone screen, but a blue light suddenly lit up the room. She looked up to see a magic circle spin in Sona's palm for a moment before winking away, the light relocating from her friend's palm to the far wall where a floating screen expanded, taking up the entire wall like a magical home theatre.

Sona quietly raised a dark eyebrow at her, and Rias looked away embarrassed.

"I-I just haven't gotten around to working on that spell, okay?"

"It's alright," her friend stood up, "Would you like some tea?"

"I'd appreciate it..." She muttered, turning her attention to the new widescreen. There, some smiling devil in a grey suit sat in one of those cheerful studio sets. Beside him, his blonde co-host in a tight orange dress smiled with him. Their leathery wings spread out behind them as they looked directly at the camera.


"Tonight's top story: the Gremory-Nebiros Incident. Youthful rambunctiousness, or old wounds resurfacing? We take a closer look!"

"Oh, but what's there left to talk about?! If you want explanations, you've got every possible and some impossible one down in the comments section of Miss Gremory's Devitube video! Half of the people there are all forensic analysts, while the other half are experts in inter-House law!"

"Hey, we've all been that person; we've all argued in the comments section from time to time. Even that controversial ruling for that Abbadon-Belphegor Rating Game a few months back is still a hot topic today! Devils just love a good debate! But what I'm talking about are actual answers, not the conclusions of armchair analysts. And I can tell you all now, real answers are what we're about to get! Because our palace sources have recently confirmed that the Office of the Satan Leviathan has just stamped a formal inquiry into the incident. Yes, that is indeed a Satan-approved 'who-really-started-it' commission! So if I was a betting devil, I'd start making polite wagers now. Because if a whole investigating commission wasn't enough, our sources also say that the one overseeing the entire operation is one of Her Lady Serafall Leviathan's top men as well."
​

Rias muted the stupid broadcast.

"An investigation!? What even is the point of one?!" She folded her arms tight beneath her chest with a sharp huff. "Between Sirzechs-onii-sama and Serafall-sama, they run half the government of the Underworld! They should already be wrapping this up, and this whole news show should have just been the announcement of the Nebiros bastard's trial date!"

Fine porcelain clicked on wood as Sona placed a steaming cup of tea in front of her.

"It's all about due process," she said.

And for the first time in a long while, Rias stared at Sona Sitri like she was stupid. "They already have everything and they already know everything! What part of this 'due process' is taking so much time?"

"They can't simply decide that we're innocent, he's guilty and that the matter is finished," her friend said calmly, sitting down with her own cup of tea.

"Either of them absolutely could!"

"They could," Sona conceded, her eyes going from the steaming teacup she held to the talkshow that ran muted in the background, "but with how public this incident has become, that's not how this works anymore."

Rias rolled her eyes. "Find out who's at fault, make him pay. What's the point of making it more complicated than that?!"

"Serafall-onee-sama explained it to me once," Sona said after a sip from her own teacup, "When something like this captures public attention, most devils aren't thinking about us or about him, they're thinking about themselves. They worry about a day when they themselves would be the ones under investigation, and they ask themselves: 'Should that day ever arrive, would the proper procedure be followed for me too? Would I be treated fairly?' And onee-sama is trying her best to let people know that the answer is 'Yes.' Above everything else, that's what onee-sama wants as the Satanic Head of Foreign Relations; she wants people to know that the Underworld that she and the other Satans are building is different from the Underworld that the Old Satans ran."

"Even if the result is already obvious?! People just want a public spectacle!"

"Rias... You're the one who made it into a public spectacle by posting the video on Devitube."

"Fine, I guess that part was my mistake." She grumbled, taking an angry sip from her teacup. "So, what now? We're supposed to sit still, smile and pretend like those show hosts?"

"We don't have to do anything," Sona said, leaning back into the couch and crossing her legs as if it was really time to relax. "The Underworld wanted to see the process of justice being done, so Serafall-onee-sama is showing she's personally involved. Her and her department, I mean. Though it wouldn't surprise me if Sirzechs-sama's department was also involved one way or another. She's likely preparing or is already on a press tour right now."

"Well, as long as the result doesn't change..." Rias muttered thoughtfully and allowed herself a slower and calmer sip of her tea, "And it is kind of reassuring that things were being done right and by the book. Do we know who the guy in charge of the investigation is?"

"I have no clue," Sona shrugged. "Several names from onee-sama's department of Foreign Affairs do come to mind, but it is just as likely the agent came from other departments. Maybe even from Sirzechs-sama."

Rias just shook her head.

"Let's hope the Satan Leviathan's top agent is really as good as he sounds, I want to see that Nebiros guy hang for this sooner than later."


(Meanwhile) [Mal's Homestead - Edge of Kuoh Town]


"—one of Her Lady Serafall Leviathan's top men as well."
​

The campfire crackled as if laughing along with Lilith City talkshow host, the orange flame burning away behind the magic screen displaying the Underworld 'morning' TV program.

An elbow playfully nudging my ribs, followed by a teasing voice. "And how is the formal inquiry going, chief investigator☆?"

"This Nebiros kid is a shifty-looking fella," I fixed my black cowboy hat solemnly. "but I've been investigating him all day long and I haven't dug up a lick of wrongdoing! Sorry to say, but all this hard work just might be for nothing."

"Well, keep looking☆!"

"Will do!" I laughed along, and raised my whiskey glass, "I'll investigate the bottom of his glass for one!"

"And I'll investigate the bottom of mine too, cheers☆!"

It was a helluva thing, sitting down by the fire, pressed up against a very beautiful and very busty Serafall Leviathan. A slender arm linked around mine and soft, warm curves pressed up against my side. Just a cowboy in black and a magical girl in pink-and-white sitting together as we watched the moon and firelight sparkle through our half-filled whiskey glasses. Strange to think how we've only met a week ago but were now thick as thieves. I guess that's just the sort of thing that happens when two people become co-conspirators.

And we were definitely in on it together now, Serafall and I, in this plot to pull the wool over the eyes of everyone currently alive (and with a Devilnet connection) like the wiley devils we were.

It even came upfront with its side perks!


Feat Achieved! — Famous: One Million Devil-views!
Get on national (Underworld) news and have your name be known to at least 1 million devils
+1 Silver Gacha Ticket

...
​

I shook my head at the thought. Well, I'm pretty sure the folks at home have seen me on TV now. Still, a million people speaking my name... Everyone sure do love their scandals, especially ones involving the Satans' sisters and the people who they tried to kill! Sure, this wasn't how I thought I'd get famous, but I'd happily take a silver ticket for it!

"One thing I'm still puzzling over though..."

Blue eyes glided from her drink and in my direction. "Hmm?"

"Why make me chief investigator?" I asked the Satanic Head of Foreign Relations, "The 'investigation' isn't gonna change none whether or not I'm heading the commission, right?"

"It doesn't! But it makes my job easier☆" Serafall winked playfully, letting me in on her secret. "It's cleaner when the facts technically stick with the story. Half-truths, if you want the not-as-pretty name for them. In this case, I can step up to every news conference and tell them that there was an actual commission with actual people looking into the matter, and that I actually had handpicked my best man for the job. And all of that would technically be true! I could even tell Michael about it and all he'll pick up is that I'm not telling him the whole truth... which is to be expected since no leader of a faction does that. Not even him. We all understand that a lot of things fall under our usual, classified 'leader-of-the-entire-race' stuff, and so we don't take it personally if someone is holding back some details."

"I'm flattered you think I fit the bill as your best guy."

"But you really are!" Serafall grinned. "I wouldn't have been able to pull this off without your help, Mal-kun!"

"Glad to be able to help with the plan!" I chortled into my drink. And what a grand old plan it was: A dog and pony show to fool all of Creation in just three easy steps.

This was Step One: Rig the investigation.

For the folks who were angry at me for 'attacking the beautiful, helpless Rias Gremory', just the fact that there was an underway investigation was solid proof to them that justice was being done. For the Great King faction and the other Houses in the 72 Pillars, the investigation was a reassurance from the Four Satans that they weren't gonna let anyone—not even a Lucifer Six runaway like myself—just walk all over their heirs and heiresses. And for the angels and the fallen, the investigation was a demonstration that devilkind was 'civilized' and 'followed the rule of law' and could be trusted with other deals.

Truth be told, that last one didn't sit right with me, however...

I glanced up, past the dark brim of my cowboy hat and at the starry sky beyond the forest canopy where Heaven lay, unburnt.

... I knew I wasn't ready to take on all those angels even if I wanted to. I was gonna need a lot more of everything for that endeavor. More spells, more demonic power, and more Chaos Gacha tickets. Whatever I can get from wherever I might find them.

That did make me wonder: Was this the right call?

Sitting here drinking with the Satan that was working with the angels? Helping her cover for the princess who'd damn near took my head clean off my shoulders? Tying my name to their trouble, fixing their mess?

I stared into the campfire.

Asking those questions... It made me wonder what would have happened if I'd listened to what my mother said instead, and bent Serafall over when she was still desperate. All-in'd instead of this far more cordial relationship I'm playing with her. It sure as hell would have been the highest-risk-highest-reward move I could have played at the time! Risk, because that was all assuming that a Satan would let herself get coerced into being a middle-class devil's plaything for the night, and that I'd live long enough to enjoy whatever I rolled from the ticket. Reward, because that would've won me a Diamond ticket. Maybe even a Legendary one.

It would have been one hell of a gamble to take. If I'd taken it. Maybe I should have taken it.

Doubt's an awful companion like that.

Sure, a devil could sit down, drink his whiskey and tear himself apart over what he should've, could've, ought've done like I am now. But this was afterwards. Back in that heart-pounding present—with everyone watching, the clock ticking, and the weight of everything you could possibly win and everything you could possibly lose pressing down on your shoulders—you got a split second to either stake a wager or walk away.

That's all the time you'll get, and there's no turning around or asking for do-overs either. You make your choice, and that's the trail you ride... to live with for the rest of your life. Much in the same way as any other life-changing decision.

And I had made mine.

A slender finger poked my cheek, and looking over, I found the dark-haired beauty in pink that was Serafall Leviathan pouting at me, a slight whiskey-blush across her pale cheeks. She leaned in, close enough for her hot, drunken breath to brush over my chin. "Mou~ What're you looking all serious for, Mal-kun?" she said, "We should be celebrating, you know!"

"Nothing," I just smiled back at her, pouring her another glass. "I'm thinking about how it looks like we'll be working together for a while."

Couldn't rightly say whether I should be regretting this or not, nor can I say if working with the Satan Leviathan would pay out better down the line. But that's what I'm gambling on; and now, there's nothing left to do but see just how far down this long, crooked trail we'll end up riding together...

"And here's to a fruitful partnership☆!" Serafall grinned, before downing the rest of her glass. Just tipped it bottoms up. A drop of the amber liquid escaped the corner of the dark-haired girl's lips though and it ran down her pale neck, leaving a shimmering trail under the firelight that led straight down under her neckline and into her creamy cleavage. The drop didn't leave out the bottom of her crop top though, the shiny trail likely finding a dead end, caught tight somewhere between her swaying, heaving handfuls as she gulped her drink down. "Haahhh~"

... Yep, I can't deny that this trail had some gorgeous views though.

She wiped her lips with her forearm before scooting closer to me, pressing my arm closer to her soft warm body. "Honestly, Mal-kun, if you're worried about getting found out, then don't be☆! I have everything covered! It's not like any official documents will be signed off with your real name, so the odds of the whole truth coming out is slim to none... Unless you plan on posting a video on your own Devitube channel about this?"

Though it was told like a joke, her pointed blue-eyed stare told me it was definitely an implicit order NOT to make the same mistake as Rias.

"I don't even have one!" I guffawed.

"Really? Finally, some good news!" Serafall said before she levelled a serious look my way. Well, as serious a look that a girl with twin-tails, in a fluttery pink magical girl outfit and a drunk blush could at least. "Okay, my turn to ask a serious question! Rias-chan. No hard feelings about her, right?"

"None at all!" I said with a smile, but she just looked at me in disbelief.

"Really?"

"As real as the dirt we're standing on!" I said, pouring myself another glass. "While I can't deny that I'd be well within my rights to hold a grudge against her for trying to kill me and framing it as my fault, I already knew the truth of it: the Satan Lucifer's favoured little sister was never gonna be punished, not in any real capacity at least. I knew it even before folks wrote what they did: how she was just a frightened young lady with delicate nerves, or how the moon shone too bright in her eye and made her see things, or how this was plainly my fault! Nope, Rias was always gonna get away clean... because that's just how the world works: Consequences are for the weak. And the Gremory princess? Well, she was engaging in good ole dishonest tyranny, just as Satan—er, the original Satan—intended."

She winced. "We're not like that anymore, I promise! Sure, we might bend the rules when it comes to our little sisters, but— Uh, Rias-chan is actually really nice when you get to know her! It's just been a messy—"

"No need to justify to me," I laughed, patting the Satan on the shoulder. "Like I said, I'm holding onto no grudges against Miss Gremory. Matter of fact, I oughta thank her proper for messing up our first meeting. 'Cause that did me so much more good than if she hadn't!"

"And you're honestly just going to let all that—everything that's happened—slide... Just like that?"

"Just like that," I nodded with a small, sincere smile. And while I wouldn't say it out loud, I thought this Satan looked downright cute with her furrowed brows and dumbfounded look. So, I expanded on my answer. "Without her, I'd never have found my way into all this opportunity working alongside you. And it sure has been a pleasure seeing you work your magic!"

Now that got a different reaction, her blush intensifying on her pretty face. "Ehehe! I'm just doing my best~!" She said. "And you should give yourself some credit too, Mal-kun, you definitely deserve it for being so easy to work with!"

"You make it easy, Lady Leviathan!"

"Mou~ If you're going to go on about ranks, then your Satan commands you to never call her 'Lady Leviathan' ever again! Call her Serafall instead☆!"

"Sure thing, Serafall!" I raised my glass at the magical girl, who raised hers to mine and clinked them softly.

Yep, thick as thieves, gonna make off like them too! A devil could win a hundred Rating Games and I still wouldn't have a favour from a Satan. But thanks to Rias jumping the gun, I haven't even fought in single Rating Games and somehow had two whole Satans owing me a favour! One now with Serafall, and one after with Sirzechs Lucifer! And that even came with the promise of steady work (and by extension, even more tickets)! And if those weren't already enough, I got Neferpitou out of that whole debacle. A chimera-ant cat-familiar who's a perfect fit for the family and farm! But above all of that, it was because of Rias Gremory that...


Feat Achieved! — Satan Owes Me One
Strike a bargain that Satan now must honor. A great favor to be collected... by you.
+1 Gold Gacha Ticket

...
​

... I struck gold!

"Miss Gremory may as well have tried to kill me by slapping a cheque for a billion devil-dollars at my cheek for all the harm she's actually done!" I laughed out loud, raising a glass to Rias Gremory. "At this point, I'm half-hoping she'll try to kill me again!"

"Well, she just might! So, be ready just in case! Hehehehe☆!" She laughed with me before her blue eyes widened, realising what she just said, "Oh, uh... contact me if she does try though! Because she's definitely not supposed to do that anymore."

"Will do!"

Between my [Byakugan] and Neferpitou, we'll see any assassination attempt a mile away. And between my almost-rebuilt bronze construct and my new chimera-ant-catgirl familiar, Miss Gremory wasn't about to outmaneuver or overpower through my security anytime soon. Nope, no harm will come to my person, not from her at least. In fact...

I looked over to Serafall. "If there's one thing I'm actually worried about, it'd be the slanderous remarks she put out over the Devilnet. I ain't keen on letting her keep dragging my House's name through the dirt, even if I have run away from home. You sure her words won't stick?"

"Of course!" the dark-haired girl smiled cheerily. "The 'investigation' will cancel out most of her accusations, but the unofficial Rating Game will be the one to negate the rest of her more personal insults. Once we break the news about the Rating Game between you and Rias-chan, all her mean comments about you and your House can just be interpreted as pre-fight banter, instead of the Gremory heiress and sister of the Satan Lucifer genuinely calling for your public execution☆. They'll even be quite tame compared to what a lot of people post before their Rating Games!"

And that was Step Two: Challenge Rias Gremory to an unofficial Rating Game.

"Guess that's one way to discredit someone's words," I muttered, pushing another log into the campfire. "Just make people think they're full of hot air... Like how in that Rating Game match a couple months back, Bedeze Abaddon was rambling to the cameras about how Roygun Belphegor had grown fat and weak and how he was gonna destroy her and such. No one believed him, especially after Roygun handed him his rear on a gold platter. And if no one took the third highest ranking king seriously then, I guess no one'll take Rias Gremory seriously now."

"It's amazing how a bit of violence makes slander go away so fast, right☆!? And it's not like Rias-chan can even make comments like that anymore, she's locked out of her account while her parents put her through some remedial etiquette tutoring! And between you and me, she didn't need to take them, but..."

The magical girl leaned in with a grin full of personal satisfaction, and winked.

"... Satan's orders☆"

"Alright," I smiled along with her. "That sounds deserved."

"Completely deserved!" Serafall raised her glass with a laugh. "Oh, and while we're on the topic of her parents... Lord Gremory asked me to pass along his apologies for his daughter's behaviour, and also his gratitude for letting House Gremory save face with this unofficial Rating Game. He also stresses that—no matter the outcome of the Rating Game—Rias-chan will be putting out gracious comments about House Nebiros afterwards! I'm pretty sure that means he'll be inviting you to dine with him at Gremory Castle afterwards, likely to personally assure you that House Gremory holds no grudges towards House Nebiros."

[Gremory Castle - Underworld]

"Where are you...?"

Rias' pinchy heels clicked a fast muffled staccato on the finely carpeted stone.

Like some fairytale princess lost in a castle, she hurried down the ancient corridors of her birth home, the pointlessly-intricate blue dress for her remedial etiquette class swishing around her legs as she ran, glancing down each grandly decorated corridor looking for her Gremory Prince. The drawerful of jewellery she wore glittering under every ray of light that shone through the castle's windows, distracting enough to hide her frown, her worry and her desperation.

Her mother said her Sirzechs-onii-sama would be here, so why wasn't he—

There!

At the far end of the corridor, her Sirzechs-onii-sama stood—with his gold-trimmed white uniform, his usual crimson mane and his usual calming smile—as he talked and laughed with some of the older maids.

"Onii-sama!" she called out, and running to him as fast as her dress let her. And as she got closer, the maids obediently dismissed themselves with a bow. But Rias barely noticed them leave, too focused on her lifeline as his eyes widened and focused his smile on her.

"Rias! You look gorgeous in that dress. It's good that you finally got to w—"

"Win?" Rias laughed hollowly, shaking her head. "With my three pieces?! Both my and Sona's peerages combined couldn't stop him!"

"Rias, listen to me," He said, looking into her eyes. His hands squeezing her shoulders reassuringly, "Even if he manages to fill all fifteen pieces of his peerage, you will still win. I'm sure of it. He only gains his Evil Pieces after the investigation concludes, and the Rating Game is set immediately after. That leaves him no time to assemble a cohesive peerage. Because even when a devil has gathered the strongest individual pieces, their strength doesn't matter if they haven't been trained to work together. Whatever peerage he manages to scrape together in that window… they will not compare to what you've built in Kuoh."

His voice softened, and he smiled like it really was going to be alright.

"You'll be fine, Rias-chan. You and your little family are stronger than you believe."

"I... When you put it that way... Thanks, onii-sama." She replied just as softly, coming down from her panic. And as she looked up at her older brother, she even felt the corners of her lips tug up in a hesitant smile.

Of all the people in both the human world and the Underworld, her Sirzechs-onii-sama was the one who knew exactly how to make her feel everything will be better... or at least, like things wouldn't get worse. Reality was less harsh and less cruel with him around... But the reality of the situation was still there: As an eight-foot-tall brass construct that would tear her peerage apart in the Rating game.

Not a formal objection just yet, but they haven't forgotten to remind me of the rules this time around. Specifically, the rule about bringing an autonomous construct that is neither Sacred Gear nor bonded familiar to participate in a Rating Game. It's a broad and vague rule; and effectively, it's a case-by-case basis. But that still gives you a lot of ground to stand on to motion for his construct to be banned from the match. And if the Nebiros kid tries to push back, I'll add my voice to yours. I won't let him or anyone bully you, Rias-chan. I promise."

"Hahaha! Anything for my Rias-chan!" Her brother laughed, his hand ruffling her crimson hair and ruining her elaborate hairstyle. But at that moment in time, she couldn't care less.

It was amazing how brighter the world was without an eight-foot-tall magical engine of metal looming over her and blocking her light!

Yep, no nigh-invincible killing machine charging at them on wings of steam. No metal fists the size of torso swinging around with the force of a runaway train. Because although he had cheated by using a golem that was against the rules for any normal Rating Game, that was it. He'll never be able to use that golem again. Now, she only had to fight the Nebiros bastard and whatever unruly gang he could scrape together at the last minute!

Being outnumbered didn't scare her either, because it never had!

Those fifteen hastily assembled lackeys wouldn't stand a chance against them, and now that the Nebiros bastard didn't have that oversized brass golem to use as a shortcut and crutch anymore. And though she still hoped Serafall-sama would be able to stop this before it comes down to a Rating Game, at least...

He had nothing else that would catch them off guard.

It was finally, finally going to be a fair fight.


[Mal's Homestead]

Neferpitou hid from view. Out of sight from all her King's enemies.

It was a new experience for her using In for this long. She had never held it this long before and never needed to. For in all her existence, there had been no opponent worth hiding from. No Hunter that could threaten her when she had strength to break their spines, the speed to pluck their eyes before they could see her, and the Nen that allowed her to develop all the abilities she could ever wish for.

She had always been the thing others feared after all.

Yet this world… it was wrong in all the right ways.

Beings that radiated power in ways she had never encountered walked this universe. Powerful enough to make her cautious, and powerful enough to warrant her utilizing In for the first time. And naturally, it came to her as easily as breathing just like all the other Nen techniques. Pitiful ningen would spend years of their lives training, refining and struggling to master what she could do instinctually. Her aura folded inward, compressing, transmuting into something that slipped beyond conventional senses. To those who could see and sense energies, she was as unremarkable as a statue or as any regular, powerless ningen walking their hives of glass and stone and metal. And for those that couldn't see her directly... she may as well not exist. Just thin air sitting quietly inside a dark and motionless cabin.

This was her duty.

Her King bade her to remain hidden until his signal, or until the moment revealed itself.

It wasn't the signal to fight unfortunately, but the anticipation was still there. Watching the unaware from her perch, in the middle of the night, wondering if the next heartbeat will be the instant when she sprung into action and when she'd get to see startled eyes barely see her as she pounced faster than sound... It spoke to her on a primal level. The anticipation for that was... intoxicating.

So, she hoped her King wouldn't mind, because this was the most fun she'd ever had!

The only thing better than this would be if she was going to fight too, but unfortunately, for there was no winning against the being he was tangled with.

What a fearsome being...


A tipsy, magical girl Satan wrapped an arm around me.

Her favor.

But what surprised me the most was that... she's turning me down, not because I wasn't kind enough or compassionate enough, but because I wasn't powerful enough for her.

Maybe this 'New Satan' wasn't as 'Un-Satanly' as dad thought.

"I just might," I said, unable to keep the corners of my mouth from twitching up.

The weight of all the knowledge of [Intermediate Item Construction], [Bronze Creation], [Structural Analysis], [King of All Trades] and all the others sat heavy in my head... Along with two Gold Tickets and one silver burning a hole in my thoughts, ready to add to that a grand library. Every chance to earn those big tickets like Diamonds, Legendaries, Mythics and even Divines. It'll all be mine.

An absolutely Satanic demonic power flooded like the camp.

The temperature submerged, the fire choked to a dim candlelight, and it was like the air itself was flash-cooled into arctic ice-water. And in the near darkness, twin blue eyes stared right at me. And while I was still stunned thanks to my Byakugan, fists reached for me and gripped the lapels of my suit jacket, closing the fabric around my torso like I was a fish caught in a tightening net, and she pressed herself right against me. Almost lip-to-lip with a Satan, her soft, curvaceous body the only warm thing in a hell of cold.

"Don't you mean..." Her breath ghosted lightly across my lips, revealing how a Satan Leviathan sounded like when she was hungry: a voice like a rolling purr alongside the dull groan of metal warping. "... What would I have done to you if you were Ultimate-class and you'd asked for something more than a kiss? That's what you wanted to know, right, Mal-kun~?"

And like the devil with a death wish that I was, I nodded yes. And the Satan smiled.

"Well~"

She pulled back, and the air was light again.

"... Magical Girl Levia-tan☆ doesn't know the answer either!" she said with a cute wink and a cheery smile. "A pure maiden's heart just isn't ready for those kinds of things, don't you know? And it looks you aren't either☆"

'Ultimate-class' was the unsaid reminder.

"F-fair enough," I coughed, still trying to get my bearings after that blast of a Satan's demonic power, and I knew for a fact that it hadn't even been at full power. No, that was just a taste of what the higher end of Ultimate-class was like.

Meanwhile, the dark-haired magical girl just shifted in her seat, smiling innocently as she went back to finishing her glass.

That's when I noticed that... the groaning metal I heard earlier wasn't just my imagination. As Serafall scooted back a little, she revealed the section of solid bronze bench she'd been straddling just then, the metal now crushed to the narrow width of her tight thigh gap. Just a perfect mold of her thighs' shape left in the amber-brown metal, like it was made of plaster rather than solid, demonic-power-reinforced metal. It was as tangible a sign as any to stop lusting after girls who not only had a lot more demonic power than me, but could also crush metal with thigh-strength alone.

Any sane man would heed that warning.

But I was no man, I was a Devil of House Nebiros! Satan would understand me lusting after a girl (who was also a Satan) as long as I don't forget my unholy duty of inbreeding with my sisters!

Serafall's empty tumbler clicked onto the bronze beside her, and she turned to me.

"There is one thing I want to ask you about." Her tone was serious, blue eyes intent behind her black strands, "When I was joking around with you, I noticed something."

"You did?" I said with a half-chuckle, and a raised brow, "What would that be?"

But the Satan didn't answer right away. Her gaze going past my shoulder instead, and stayed fixed there.

...

"Mal-kun... who's that hiding inside your cabin?"
- Rias Gremory's Number One Hater End -​

Chapter 6: The Empty Quiver of Satan


[Gremory Castle - Underworld]


"From Leviathan-sama, It's urgent..."
​

Rias' ears perked at the sound of Grayfia's whisper.

She peeked across the dining table—across the lacquered, Oriental meats glistening under the blue-fire candelabras—to where her Sirzechs-onii-sama sat on his dining throne. He'd been kind enough to stay, offering her those small reassuring smiles everytime their eyes met to help her through the slow mind-numbing torture of her remedial etiquette lesson.

This time, their eyes didn't meet.

His were turned to the side. To where Grayfia-nee-sama leaned in at his shoulder. Her ribboned braid of silver hair slipped forward, brushing his polished pauldron, and her deep-ruby lips nearly blended with his mane of crimson hair as she whispered into his ear.

Rias made sure to look innocent, just pushing a slice of honey-glazed peacock from one side of her plate to the other as she strained her ears.


"House Nebiros is more active than previously thought..."
​

The knife almost slipped through Rias' fingers at the sound of that cursed name.

It would have been too soft for anyone else to hear. Almost too soft even for her. But she knew her Grayfia-nee-sama's whispers; this happened often enough that she'd made it a game for herself over the years: listening in whenever this happened, and piecing together what she couldn't hear from the movement of the older woman's lips. It had been just something to entertain herself over dinner. Now, thanks to etiquette lessons, it might be the first piece of news she's gotten since starting her lesson.

Rias gracefully glanced up the smooth length of the grand marble table.

Her parents had noticed nothing. Their voices carried easily through the banqueting hall, with all its candle-lit chandeliers and fine artpieces. Her mother and her father looked perfectly content in their fine white dress and maroon suit—having used her remedial etiquette lessons as an excuse to dress themselves as itchingly formal as she was—as they trading gossip of scandalous moments among the 72 Pillars... her own ongoing scandal not included of course.


"... working on a black project since before the Civil War—"
​

"Rias," her father glanced her way, bright blue eyes peering past the curtain of his flowing crimson hair, and Rias paused in her eavesdropping to return a patented Gremory Heiress Smile, giving the appearance that she was behaving in a manner acceptable to an heiress of a 72 Pillar Clan.

"Yes, honoured father?" She replied in her most graceful tone.

"Your nekoshou, Koneko, I trust that she's doing well?"

"She is, honoured father," Rias answered, and she wouldn't have spoken another word if remedial etiquette lessons didn't compel her to be more... 'helpful.' She kept smiling, "The doctors said that Koneko would have no lasting ailments, though her memory is spotty concerning the night's events. By her own words, it's clear that she experienced a vivid... recollection of her time with House Naberius."

"Oh, poor Koneko..." Her mother murmured. "I do hope she's feeling better now."

Rias stopped herself from visibly grinding her teeth. It made her blood boil, having to pretend that Koneko's ordeal was no one's fault. "She is, okaa-sama. Akeno-san and Yuuto-san are aiding in her recovery to her full emotional faculties. Supplying her with ample company here in Gremory Castle seems to be doing splendidly for her."

And by 'company', she meant 'kept far away from the Nebiros bastard.' Speaking of which...


"—Leviathan-sama can only discuss it in person, but she was almost—"
​

"Will your peerage be staying for the duration?" Her father interrupted her eavesdropping again.

Rias had already known the answer before she got here.

They still had to return to Kuoh Town without her. The place could not be left unattended when so much depended on her peerage. Their roster of human clients would accept no substitutes or replacements, the stray devils had been popping up more often as of late, and above all, there was Gasper, who needed looking after more than anything. Her cute, cross-dressing dhampir still reacted very poorly to anyone outside her peerage using the shoving stick, Sona's peerage included. They had only recently gotten him accustomed to Kiba being the one to shove food into his room, and even then, Gasper still occasionally made the knight piece into a victim of his time-stopping panic attacks. Baby steps, so it was important that they don't lose momentum now. And that's why...

"...As much as I would prefer they stayed, honoured father, I cannot keep my peerage from their other duties."

But the 'I'm the one who's grounded, not them. It wouldn't be fair to them.' part was left unsaid.

"A sensible decision, Rias," Her father nodded in return, a slight bearded smile appearing on his face before he went back to conversing with her mother. And Rias immediately went back to straining her ears as hard as she could, hoping she didn't miss much.


"My Satan Lucifer... she has seen it fit to declare a Modified Empty Quiver."
​

Rias tried not to panic.

Because although she didn't know what those words meant, she saw what they did to her onii-sama: Sirzechs Lucifer had stopped breathing. He sat there in his dining throne frozen so perfectly still that Rias would have thought he was a life-like wax statue of himself. His eyes wide and pupils shrank to needlepoints. It was an expression that she had never seen him make before!

Then Grayfia's second whisper followed.


"… she says we need to convene. Now."
​

The words unfroze him. His shocked expression replaced by one that she was more used to seeing: the hardened expression of the Crimson Satan. Blue eyes sharp and jaw all tense, it was the leader of the Underworld staring down a calamity he was about to stop, or an enemy he was about to destroy.

Their eyes met just beyond the flickering flame of a candelabra, and in between flickers, that harsh expression disappeared.

Her smiling Sirzechs-onii-sama had returned.

He nodded to her with a reassuring smile. Carefully, he set his fork and knife down onto his porcelain plate, as though the Underworld had turned a hundred times more fragile since his last bite, and offered their parents a composed, almost carefree smile.

"Kindly excuse me," he said lightly, already rising with a metallic click of his armor. "It's been a pleasure, but I really must be going. Matters of state are a jealous thing. Till next time… Otou-sama, Okaa-sama, Rias-chan."

"Thank you for joining your parents for dinner again, Sirzechs-kun," their mother said with a smile. Beside her, their father nodded quietly, more than accustomed to his early departures by now.

Rias doubted either of them had heard what she heard, or saw what she saw. They simply smiled as her brother bowed graciously and turned for the door, and neither did they even notice that his footsteps resounded faster than normal, or that Grayfia kept pace behind him, whispering more things into his ear that she could not hear anymore. Or how, as he swept down the hall with his cape billowing behind him, the candles flickered as if trembling from even the brush of his demonic power.

The door shut behind him, but the feeling of dread still lingered for a bit.

Rias looked down to the food on her plate. Well, if she hadn't lost her appetite before from remedial etiquette, then she definitely lost it now.

Something big had just happened beyond the walls of Gremory Castle, and it was big enough to make a Satan urgently leave to personally handle it. She was sure her onii-sama could deal with whatever it was. But at the very least, she can take comfort in the fact that whatever had happened just now, whatever had ruined her onii-sama's day, she could sleep easily knowing that the world has proven Rias Gremory right once again...

...because this was absolutely, one-hundred-percent that Nebiros bastard's fault this time.


(A few minutes ago) [Mal's Homestead]


"Mal-kun... who's that hiding inside your cabin?"
​

The question made me stone-cold sober.

I knew exactly whose one-of-a-kind, chimera-ant-catgirl familiar was hiding in my log cabin: Mine.

I'd told Neferpitou to hole up in the cabin, and if things turned sour, get me the hell out of here. Away from the Satan who likely thought my usefulness had run out and all that. But that was when I still doubted Serafall's intentions; that doubt lasted right up until she cracked open the whiskey, told me her entire plan and threatened to kill me with her Satan-class thighs. 'Cept as luck would have it, that burst of Serafall's demonic power must have spooked Pitou, who likely came close to bursting straight through those log walls and swiping me clear out of the Satan's arms... and thighs.

She must peeked her head out to check. Just for a heartbeat, maybe less. With demonic power like that, I would too.

But that was enough. The Satan Leviathan must have caught a glimpse of her shadow. And now, this magical girl in pink and white sitting right beside me was looking me in the eye and waiting for an answer.

Trying to lie didn't even come up in my head.

"It's fine!" I called out over my shoulder, "Come on out and introduce yourself!"

Quiet like a ghost, the cabin door opened, and the snowy-haired catgirl stepped into the shadowed, moonlit forest. The fire tinted my familiar's blue coat orange more and more as she stepped closer. Her face was expressionless and her movement without the cat-like grace that I was used to—dried leaves cracked loudly under her footsteps. Neferpitou's way of showing she meant no harm, I suppose. She stopped right before our seat and knelt on one knee, right by the fire.

Pitou bowed her head low, her snowy white tail settling on grass as she spoke.

"I am Neferpitou, a mere humble Familiar to my King. It is an honour to meet you, Satan Leviathan, King of Kings."

A raven-black eyebrow rose on Serafall's pretty face. "It's... very unusual to have a nekomata willingly take a familiar contract with a devil, especially one that's— Yes, she's much stronger than the average nekomata, isn't she?"

Pitou said nothing under the Satan's stare.

"Rating Games have restrictions on powerful familiars, she'll need to be registered if she's to participate in any matches at all. However... that can come later," Serafall looked over to me, "Mal-kun, as the Satan of Foreign Relations, I have to ask: Is she, or has at any point been, affiliated with any of the Youkai factions? Especially the Kyoto or Kanto ones."

"C-Course not!" I smiled, eager to clarify that I wasn't causing a diplomatic incident myself by kidnapping Koneko's cousin or something to make into my familiar. "In fact, she's not youkai at all! Tell her, Pitou."

Pitou kept her head bowed as she spoke. "I am a wholly different being from what one would refer to as 'Nekomata' and merely possess a passing resemblance to them. Nothing more."

"Not a Nekomata?" Serafall looked over to Pitou's white cat tail, "What are you then?"

"I am a chimera ant, O' Satan Leviathan."

The campfire popped.

"Chi-" Serafall lost her voice mid-word, eyes wide like my catgirl had grown another head, then as if I had grown one too. "A chimera? I guess I shouldn't be surprised... but still! Mal-kun, when were you going to tell me anytime soon that you made one into your familiar?"

That last question even came with a pointed stare levelled straight at me.

"Truth is I only came by her not too long ago," I answered honestly. Couldn't do much else when I didn't even know what I did wrong! "She wasn't with me then when we first crossed paths over at the Griffon Grand Hotel. And after that, there didn't seem like the right hour to bring it up, especially not after getting tangled up with a nekomata losing her marbles. No sense in stirring up even more rumours and speculations about my character than Rias Gremory already had! I reckoned I could at least wait till after the investigation cooled people down before I show her off. Hell, I didn't think I'd end up getting a familiar so soon. I can tell you that!"

All the sharpness in Serafall's eyes softened into confusion. "She was... handed to you after you arrived and met Rias? And you didn't know in advance?"

"Pretty much," I said back without hesitation. I'd made peace with how the Chaos Gacha gives what it gives; all I could do was be grateful for the good rolls that I get. "And I'm glad to have gotten Neferpitou here... 'cause she's a hell of a familiar! Barely a year old and more loyal than any devil of a hundred years too!"

"I emerged merely four months ago, my King." Pitou intoned, head still bowed. "But I am ready to dedicate all that I am to you for as long as I draw breath."

I smiled at Serafall. "See? Incredibly loyal!"

"Familiars invariably are, but few are admittedly capable of saying it out loud," the Satan said, lips still pressed into a thin line, as if she was chewing on a thought. She glanced at the catgirl for a moment, but eventually settled on me. Her voice softening, almost hesitant, maybe even shy. "Mal-kun... if you don't mind me asking, do you think your family was involved with her being here?"

I blinked, then laughed at the thought. "Nope, I'm pretty darn sure they didn't send Pitou here! I mean sure, she'd be hard to pick out from Nebiros Valley if you didn't know her! I even know a couple back home with the same shape of ears like hers, one of them almost the same shade of white too! But let me tell you: Neferpitou hasn't got a drop of Nebiros DNA in her, she's 100% pure Chimera-ant-catgirl, so to speak. But... she's family now in my books."

"M-mrrrm, I am not worthy," The catgirl purred as I gave her an affectionate pat on the head, ruffling her fluffy white hair and scratching the back of her cat ears.

"Kinda wish everyone home were half as easy to deal with as this one, in fact. Satan knows I'd have a lot less trouble back home." I winced slightly. "Though... speaking of trouble, I might get into some for bringing a non-devil into the house. We're not supposed to bring anything inside, unless it's cattle to be butchered, but... I'm sure he'd make an exception for my familiar!"

"C-cattle? Nevermind, Mal-kun, you report straight to the director of your whole House?"

"I wouldn't call dad the 'director' of Nebiros valley..." I scratched my chin as I thought about it.

Dad didn't just have the loudest voice in town, but he had a real knack for making people listen to him too. Whether it was cajoling stubborn devils or brokering agreements between the town hotheads (and we had many)... he was a natural orator of a rooster-devil. Or maybe, it's because he'd gotten everyone in the valley used to following his voice since he'd been doing that cockadoodledoo call first thing at 'morning' for the past few hundred years without fail.

'Just doing my civic duty for the community!' Dad would say. 'Getting lazy, no-account devils outta their beds, that is! Ain't no time for lollygaggin', dillydallyin', or shilly-shallyin' around in dreamland when there's Satanic deeds needing doing!' And he would say it often too as he was mighty proud of what he did.

"... But I guess he does sorta calls the shots around town? No one else does."

"So, you're the heir of House Nebiros?" came Serafall's whisper. Blue eyes glittering like very interested gems under the firelight.

"Now hold on," I protested, "My great-grandpapi might be Zaoroma Nebiros, but—"

"The Zaoroma Nebiros?"

"Yes?" I was gonna say that the entire town descends from him. I was on the line of inheritance for sure, but more than half the town had to pass up the opportunity first! But for that same reason, curiosity stopped me short too. "You... wouldn't know where he is, would you? Seeing you're a Satan and all."

"No...?" Serafall said, looking just as surprised and confused as I was. "No one's seen Zaoroma Nebiros since the Civil war! We didn't even know House Nebiros still existed!"

"Ah, no one in town knows where he is neither."

"I see..." She murmured, looking deep in thought before smiling back at me. "But as expected of one of the Six Houses of Lucifer, even without your Head of House, it sounds like your family keeps Nebiros Valley in an admirable state of affairs."

"That's right! It's hard work, but we manage!" I smiled, "I finished setting up this new system just last year in fact, a mighty clever one too if I do say so myself! Haven't had anything slip past it in a while neither! We just—"

That eager smile dropped from my face as I realised what I had almost come close to saying.

How I was gonna tell the Satan Leviathan all about that new paddock fencing system I spent all of last year building, and all about the cattle I herd through them, about the devil-coyotes and devil-hogs that I chase off, about the devil-cotton and devil-sugar and devil-opium that I helped grow myself, about my sisters in their dusty train yard and their neverending excitement for the trains that stop in it, about the town's top three favourite watering holes... and about how the Satan-loving folk of Nebiros all had their place in town, doing their humble professions.

Yes, I stopped myself from telling her how we were all just small-devil-town devils.

Us. House Nebiros. One of the Six Houses of Lucifer.

Because there wasn't a devilish soul in the valley who didn't know where our family stood: Yes sir, House Nebiros were one of the Lucifer Six! Servants of Satan above most, and every Nebiros there was damn proud of it! So...

... What was one of the Six Houses of Lucifer doing out in the middle of nowhere in the Underworld?

"You just…?" Serafall's soft voice prompted, her blue eyes shining in the night.

And it was at that moment, sitting there in the crackling, orange glow of the campfire with the Satan Leviathan waiting for a straight answer that I realised that I didn't have one to give her. Like having a ningen president ask some washed-up citizen why their once-wealthy family who lived at the top-rung of the social ladder was now holed up in a weather-beaten cabin in the wilderness past the end of the map.

"We just..."

And for the first time since I've left home, I felt my face burn in shame.

I didn't think much of it until now. Why dad never let a supper pass without talking about the standing of our House and over our lack of ningens.

Because as much as it stung to admit it, House Nebiros has fallen far from grace like dad said. Nebiros Valley might be expansive, but all we had were fields and prairies, and creeks and woods, and the satanic kin of Nebiros to inhabit them. Not like the rest of the 72 Pillar Houses who had cities with skyscrapers and stadiums and tens of thousands of peasantry families answering to them. Yeah, we see them 72 Pillar House cities a lot on TV, and while no soul in Nebiros Valley ever much longed to turn our quiet stretch of countryside into some bustling city (not even me), it still gnawed at a devil's pride all the same.

House Nebiros was from the Lucifer Six, one of the six families who served Satan almighty directly dammit! Folks like our family shouldn't be living humbler than half the Underworld!

... But that's exactly what House Nebiros did.

We lived humbly with not much to our name anymore. All we had were old stories of how we used to be great... and not even proper stories at that, just scraps passed around over casual conversation around dusty porches and quiet campfires. Half-remembered pride for an even less-remembered history. All of it circling back to the same question that no one in town could bear to ask one another.


How could we have let House Nebiros fall so far?
​

"W-we just keep to ourselves out there in the valley," I said, looking away and rubbing the back of my neck. "I mostly do routine work myself, keeping a close eye on everything! I-it's a handful maintaining the domain of a Lucifer Six house, I can tell you that!"

It wasn't much of an answer, barely even an awkward excuse. But it was all I got on me.

"Oh, it's okay if you can't say much!" Serafall said quickly with a smile. "Far be it from me to pry into the affairs of House Nebiros! I'm just glad you're here now, Mal-kun, you've been a great help!"

"Thanks..." I said, glancing up at the glittering night sky, part-covered the forest canopy. "I'm just finding my place in the Underworld..."

It'd been simple when I left Nebiros Town: Just keep taking the fight upwards. Get close enough to grab those perfect, gleaming, feathered varmints looking down from their gold-white thrones... and drag them down and make them squawk. Just as Satan Intended. But them angels can wait their turn to be blasted to heck, because I realise now that there's a more important fight to be won right here... and that was the fight for the family name.

My Lucifer blood burned in my veins at the prospect.

House Nebiros, the only House of the Lucifer Six still standing, and I'm gonna make that mean something again. The Six Houses of Lucifer were feared for a reason once, and I'm gonna be the reason why it will again. I'm gonna pick up the House Nebiros name from the dirt, give it a good ole polish, and hang it back where it belongs: straight at the top where everyone can see it and where they can shake in their boots at the sight of it again.

"Mrr~rrm," Neferpitou purred under my hand.

Because if there was any single devil among all the Satanic kin of House Nebiros who could pull it off, it would be me, and the pure chaotic horsefeathers that I could pull. Even now, a Silver and two Gold rested in the back of my mind, ready to be cashed in the moment Serafall left. But there was one last thing I needed to know...

I looked up to meet Satan in the eye, the twin-tailed girl in pink giggled. "You have that look in your eye again, Mal-kun. Something on your mind?"

"I do." I nodded, "Hypothetically, would the 72 Pillars and the Underworld at large accept a Lucifer Six house back?"

Blue eyes widened in surprised delight, and Serafall's entire face broke into a radiant smile.

"OH, ABSOLUTELY☆! I—"

!

The world snapped like a video skipped forward.

The forest was now upside-down, and so was my cabin, hanging down like long, woody beards from a chin of grass and dirt. A blink later, and I recognised that I was the one hanging upside down, slung over my catgirl's slender back like a sack of potatoes. My face pressed right against her pert ass cheek as she stood in a cat-like half-crouch, her white tail swishing in front of my eyes.

"Pitou, what in blazes?!"

The catgirl's tense voice came. "My King, her hand moved to pluck your liver."

I twisted as much as my undignified position allowed, craning for a better look. "What?"

"Pfft, hehehe☆! It's alright, Pitou-chan. I just wanted to shake Mal-kun's hand, not his liver."

From my upside-down vantage between Pitou's tight orange shorts, I caught sight of Serafall standing up from the bronze bench, and said catgirl's thighs tightened right before my eyes. Pitou was ready to bolt at one wrong move. Serafall must have noticed too, because for a flicker of the campfire, that easy grin cracked and real panic slid in those blue eyes. Her smile turned nervous and she stopped in her tracks, worried that Pitou might just vanish with me slung over her shoulder like stolen goods. Me too, I didn't fancy reaching mach speed upside down and leading with my rear end.

Except before I could defuse the situation myself, Serafall got to talking.

"I promise, I just got a little too excited and moved a little too fast!" She aimed her apology at my face that was dangling upside down between catgirl thighs. "Sorry for spooking your familiar like this☆~!"

That made Neferpitou wait long enough for me to respond.

" 's alright," I managed after a second and gave my catgirl's hip a pat, "You can set me back on my feet now, Pitou. It was just a false alarm."

She did, and immediately bowed. "My King, my deepest apologies for—!"

"It's all right, you silly cat," My fingers ran across her scalp gently. "You were just looking out for me."

I didn't hold it against Neferpitou for reacting this way. Couldn't really. We were both outsiders to this Earth in our own different ways. I came all the way from the wild countryside beyond the edges of most Underworld maps; she came all the way from her grimdark, blood-and-scheming dystopia and was now here in this romcom boob anime. It wasn't one lick surprising that she misinterpreted what was supposed to be a funny visual gag as an attempt to disembowel me.

Still, a misunderstanding was a misunderstanding. So, fixing my hat, I stepped over to the Satan watching with curious, blue eyes and...

"Sorry about Pitou being jumpy. You were saying?"

...I offered my hand with a smile that said 'let's just forget that happened.'

"Hehehe☆!" She laughed as she took my hand, and we shook. "I just wanted to say that as Satan☆Leviathan that the 72 Pillars would never—not in a million years—turn away a House. Extra Demon or 72 Pillar or otherwise! But especially one as respected as House Nebiros! You, your House, and any other House out there will always have a place in devil society! Magical Girl Levia-tan☆ Guarantee!"

"That's a great relief to hear, Serafall!" I smiled after shaking off my surprise. "Much obliged!"

"You're always welcome, Mal-kun!" Serafall said, beaming before her eyes flicked upward a heartbeat later, catching on the glow of the full moon hanging in the starry sky. "Oh, it's that time already?! It's been really fun, Mal-kun, but the Underworld needs its magical girl☆! She can only skip out on work for so long, you know? Hehehe☆~!"

Two golds and a silver smoldered in my soul, impatient to be ignited.

"Of course!" I replied with a nod, smiling despite myself. "Don't let me keep you."

"Take care, Mal-kun," The twin-tailed Satan waved back. Her smile lit up with pink light as a glowing magical circle spun to life beneath her ribboned shoes. Her pink miniskirt fluttering lightly in a soft breeze. She glanced over her shoulder, "Oh, and one last thing... Introduce yourself to Sona-tan properly, would you?"

"Meet the sis. Got it," I said with a nod and a smile to reassure her, "We'll play nice this time. Might even bring her a fresh fruit basket and everything."

"She'd appreciate it a lot!" She beamed a brilliant smile, and with a twirl of her star-tipped wand and a shout, "Well, see you soon, Mal-kun! Magical Girl Levia-tan☆ away~!"

The Satan disappeared in a flash of pink light, and I collapsed back onto the bronze bench.

"Are you alright, my King?" Neferpitou was beside me in a blink.

"Yeah..." I said, taking my hat off just so that I could run my hand tiredly through my hair, "Part of me was worried that she wouldn't buy my lack of explanation for House Nebiros. Honestly, I wouldn't either. So, I'm glad it all worked out somehow!"

"I humbly apologise for being spotted!" She bowed her head again. "Her Demonic Power flared to alarming levels and I had shifted my position to better come to your aid. I had mistakenly thought my In would be enough to hide my presence from her, but her eyes were sharper than I had anticipated. Far sharper than any ningen I had encountered before. [Falbium's Command Center - Underworld]

There had been a thousand different projects to develop a hundred different curses, a hundred different poisons and a hundred different plagues. Others were completed, and promptly unleashed upon the ranks of the Angels and the Fallen. If Malal Nebiros was in possession of one of the more virulent anti-Fallen magical plagues described in those documents...

There hadn't been any shortage of those grand projects. Wonder weapons meant to win the Great War in a single move. Chimeras were one of them, belonging to an umbrella project meant to address Devilkind's low fertility rates. Or more specifically, their increasing shortage of powerful warriors. And in their now ancient reports to Lord Lucifuge, House Nebiros promised the Old Satans an army that could be raised regardless of Devilkind's population. In other words...

If blood was currency in war, then House Nebiros had promised them infinite riches.

An endless horde of powerful demonic beings, distilled from the flesh of magical creatures and reborn with demonic power, ready to fight and die for the Underworld. For the Old Satans. For each devil alive, there would be ten of these powerful creatures ready to die for them. Blindingly loyal and unflinchingly obedient. An army to overwhelm the angels and the fallen. Taking Heaven and Earth, inch by blood-strained inch, drowning their enemies in a tide of bodies, winning the Old Satans the Great War once and for all.

... But it never came to pass. The project was cancelled by House Nebiros themselves, hundreds of years before the Civil War. No Chimera was ever reported to be produced for the war effort, and no chimera was even seen.

Until now.

Until this picture that now filled the entire wall of a Satan's command centre. Claws and fangs bared at the camera, staring with blood red eyes. A chimera who just happened to also be the familiar of the first Nebiros seen in five hundred years.

House Lucifuge was the Original Satan's chosen blade. An entire devil lineage forged into a weapon, honed by hides of broken enemies and oiled in their gore, the instrument by which His wrath was made manifest as they unleashed tides of slaughter upon His foes.

Drenched in the blood of the uncounted, riding into battle to carry a singular command to His enemies: 'Die screaming.'

Serafall actually respected them herself, back then.

And yet, near the end of the Civil War—when the embers had only started to die—there the Lord Lucifuge lay broken, in a medical tent in the ruins of Valafar, his body failing. His final wish, spoken to Sirzechs Gremory, his enemy... and son-in-law.

So it was decreed. And beneath the watch of all the Lords of the 72 Pillars and grieving armies of Lucifuge, the bloodline was extinguished. Banners burned. Blades and armours, tried in battles beyond counting, were cast into the crucibles. Their domain demolished to the last stone, their proud population scattered to domains across the Underworld.

The greatest of the Lucifer Six was willingly sacrificed on the altar of peace.

It was an acceptable tribute.

Devilkind, ravaged by the Civil War... weak and vulnerable, had 'disarmed' even further. Rolled over and showed their belly. And from that act of humility, the concord between Devil, Angel, and Fallen was strengthened. Grayfia's father disbanded House Lucifuge to prove that the Four Satans would no longer rule through fear and overwhelming force. The maid, the strongest Queen in the entire Underworld, flinched. Strikes that would have vaporised high-class devils and six-winged angels would not have made her bat an eyelash, but being called by her own name made her like she had been slapped.
