'Grayfia Gremory' was denied her happiness and her peace. Because, even after being destroyed, they still needed her House' strength. Its name was enough to give their enemies pause. And though House Lucifuge may be just a shadow of its former self, even the looming shadow of Satan's right hand was something to be feared, to be respected. To show that Devilkind wasn't defeated, wasn't to be taken advantage of.

If there would ever be true peace, then it would be as equals. And for that, they needed their strength back. All that they could regain. House Nebiros, more than most.

But if nothing else... Serafall understood.

Grayfia's grief-addled mother wastes away in a torpor, silently mourning for her father and her House. Her younger brother, missing for decades, possibly having thrown himself to the Old Satans faction or worse. Her House paid for its 'sins.' Meanwhile, here was another Lucifer Six House and they're getting special treatment.

Grayfia made to turn away, but stopped halfway to whisper. "We don't need them."

"We do. We really do."

Another shadow approaching, towering over where Serafall lay in her massage chair as it reached down and took her bottle away. "Serafall," Sirzechs-chan waved her bottle with a sigh, "we think you've had enough to drink."

"Fine~"

Looks like the maid was tagging her husband in.

"So, your proposed solution is to accept them and all their bioweapons, Serafall?" Sirzechs said, rather than ask. "I thought we were close to an actual peace talk? Just within the decade?"

"That's true. If only that was our only problem..." She grunted. "We could always negotiate our way to another peace talk, however many decades that would take. But what we can't negotiate our way out of is the Old Satan faction getting a hold of House Nebiros and all the research that they apparently hadn't stopped working on, because we drove them into their arms. You all know what that would lead to."

And Falbi's voice, their Satanic Head of Military Affairs, was the one to speak the words, echoing through the command center over the speakers.


"A Second Civil War."
​

There would be no going back from that for Devilkind. The 'first' one wiped out half the houses in the 72 Pillars and brought them to their knees; a second civil war would drive Devilkind to extinction. And the Old Satan faction were just the type of maniacs who would happily burn the whole Underworld down if it meant they could rule over the ashes and fling it into their Michael's and Azazel's eyes like pocket sand.

"But for contingency's sake, if we were to make the attempt..." their Crimson Lucifer considered. "... do we know anything else that could help in neutralizing House Nebiros?"

"Zaorama Nebiros is still alive, but is not in Nebiros Valley, and has not been for some time." She shared to them, "The Lord Nebiros has already gone off to start his next project. So, any hostile action against their base in Nebiros Valley won't take out their entire house. It took us five hundred years just to get even this first footprint of his. But we might cut it down to half and spend only two-hundred-fifty years for the next base, who knows☆~?"

Sirzechs-chan and Grayfia exchanged visibly worried looks over that the Original Lucifer's mad scientist was still on the loose.

Serafall winked playful at them. "Welcome to Magical Girl Levia-tan☆'s wonderful world of trying to find a way to deal with strange beings you can't kill no matter how much you want to~!"

"Wait..." Falbi's voice crackling over the speakers, participating much to everyone's surprise in the conversation of his own accord, "If the Lord Nebiros is absent, then who is leading the so-called Nebiros Valley?"

"His grandson, Mal-kun's father, is the acting Lord Nebiros of the site, and that makes Mal-kun himself... effectively the heir."

"But he's not officially?" Sirzechs-chan asked.

"He's definitely involved in the family business in all the ways an heir does. So, I told Sona-chan to receive him like he is. At this point, we really cannot trust anyone else to do it," Serafall said, before looking over to the former Astaroth and the former Glasya-Labolas, "Unless Latia-chan or Zephyrdor-chan are willing to accommodate him instead?"

"She's not ready for such a responsibility." / "He has the wrong temperament for diplomatic work."

"Then all our hopes are with Sona-tan now," Serafall shrugged, her massage chair's black leather squeaking under her shoulders. "But I genuinely think it will take a while. Mal-kun's pretty open and wears his heart on his sleeve, but he's still secretive about his family. I asked him what House Nebiros has been doing in Nebiros Valley, and you know how he reacted? Pure terror. He was terrified of me finding out, enough that all he could do was awkwardly deflect. 'Living quiet,' he said. Mal-kun's too honest... he can't really lie."

"Nebiros Valley must be a massive laboratory-fortress."

"What makes you say that, Aju-kun?" Serafall shifted to where she can just barely catch a glimpse of that wind-swept mop of green hair as he spoke.

"The scale needed to not just conduct this amount of research, but to also keep it secure against both internal and external threats, and against...." Ajuka stepped into view, face lit up by the screen as he stared up at it. At the image of Pitou-chan. "... against living weapons of this power, we're looking at something akin to a vast prison complex."

"Ajuka..." Falbi yawned through the speakers, "What exactly would those defenses entail?"

"A fortress to surpass those of any Pillar House's. The thickest walls, both magically and structurally reinforced. The most secure bulkhead doors meant to isolate crowds of high-class chimeras from each other. And interlocking spheres of security of... varying degrees of lethality. All reinforced by redundant systems that are crucial to any lab. Perhaps triple or quadruple redundancy, especially one of such monstrous size. Moreover it would boast a defense mechanism similar to that of many beast tamer Houses: Even if its walls were compromised, any invading force would need to contend with every hostile organism, every viral/chemical agent and otherwise hazardous materials the laboratory was containing."

Ajuka's long, green cape swayed as he turned around, looking the Crimson Lucifer in the eye.

"I assume that I do not need to explain how calamitous it would be if all of it were suddenly released out into the Underworld?"

"No," Sirzechs-chan just laughed it off, "But to be clear, Nebiros Valley is impregnable?"



"Girls! Have you seen your brother today?"

"Uh... onii-chan is still out working on the fence again today, attaching the hogwire! Probably? We dunno."

"Mm! He's activating the oink-reppelling force field! Zuuuuuu~"

"Well, there's a devil-hog on the loose right now in the devil-sugar! And I say, I say, what in tarnation's the point of building a fence to keep a hog out when we've already got one stomping around in? I told that boy—yes sir, I told him—'Son, cover that gap in the east corner first, that's where they sneak in!' But nooo, boy's likely out there sippin' iced tea and hammerin' wire in the shady part out north again, meanwhile a porker's treating the cane field like an all-you-can-eat buffet!"

"I'll handle it, sugar. Let me just whip up some dipping sauce."

​

"Very impregnable." nodded Ajuka.

Serafall scoffed. "It still surprises how often we miss these massive, sprawling fortified compounds just sitting in the open."

"Well that 'contingency plan' is at a dead end," Sirzechs sighed, massaging his temples and sneaking another glance at the pair of slitted red eyes staring down at them from the screen, "... so let's consider the more immediate matter at hand: His nekoshou-chimera familiar."

Serafall raised an interested eyebrow. "We're assuming that House Nebiros was involved in the Nekoshou Incident now?"

"Well, the pieces do fit..." Aju-kun supported Sirzechs-chan's hypothesis. "Both Nekoshou sisters were the wards of House Naberius, once a vassal house of House Nebiros. And House Naberius did have an MO of experimenting on and chemically-enhancing their peerages... of which the older Nekoshou was part of. The series of events are as follows: The Nekoshou incident occurs, House Naberius disappears without a trace, taking their research with them. And now the one Nebiros we've met reveals that he has a nekomata familiar. One that just so happens to somewhat resemble the nekoshou that reacted so strongly to his the sigil of House Nebiros. That's far too many coincidences."

"Also, Mal-kun also only got Pitou-chan after that incident with Rias-chan," She reminded them, "Obviously as a direct response. We already know that the rest of House Nebiros has been watching. Now we know that they're responding."

"And what are they trying to say by sending this Neferpitou?"

"I have no idea☆!" She chirped, "House Nebiros knows that if they wanted to talk-talk, they'd just send a chimera through the front door with an invitation and we'd all be dressed up over dinner right now. But nope, we get these mysterious signals instead."

"Ajuka," Sirzechs-chan slapped their friend on the back, "you're the scientist and used to thinking like one. Is it a threat?"

Aju-kun looked at him like he was stupid. "Your logic is flawed, but I suppose the conclusion still has merit. Hmmm..." he hummed in consideration, cupping his chin as he stared at Pitou-chan's picture, "I believe this is an exhibition. House Nebiros is letting their work speak for them, but at the same time, they've expressed their support of their heir by entrusting him with the fruits of their research. He may have run away, but they're on his side. However... I suppose it is ultimately still a show of force, or at least, of competence. If I am correct, we'll be seeing more of their projects revealed in time... I am unsure if I'm looking forward to that."

"So going forward, we assume that House Nebiros have assembled all their toys☆?" Serafall laughed.

"We should assume the worst. So, yes."

"That sounds fantastic☆!" Serafall laughed, enjoying the worried look that Sirzechs-chan and Grayfia shared, "I wonder what kind of surprises Mal-kun has next!"

Serafall Leviathan always loved surprises.


[Mal's Homestead]


"My King, whenever you're ready."
​

I had no business sparring with my cat.

This was a fool's errand! There was no hope of me winning in a physical match against her. I'd have better luck trying to lasso lightning bolts than win a straight match with her. Even if it was a spar, there'd be no other way this'd end than with me tasting fine Japanese grass.

But that... was what the Malal Nebiros of one hour ago would have said.

"Just one kata this time," I smiled at the catgirl, standing across the clearing. My hands raised, and my boots crunching on dirt as I steadied in my martial arts stance that felt like I'd been doing it for more than ten years, because [King of All Trades] also knew kung-fu. And for a few seconds, neither of us made a sound. Just two statues in martial arts stances in the dark forest. A gentle breeze swept past my camp, sending her snowy white hair swaying, grass rustling around her shoes.

And somewhere inside my cabin, a clock ticked once.

I moved.

Quick as I was able, I dashed to her. Closed the distance. Eyes on my target.

Five strikes with my fists, one after the other. All of them rocketing at her face. Her slitted red eyes didn't even follow them, hands just batting them away like flimsy branches in her way. My boot shot up from the ground like a snake aiming for her chin. Her palm kept it from getting even a third of the way there.

We stopped. The kata was done.

Then the sonic booms followed. Waves of air exploding outward, overturning some pebbles, and rustling the tree branches above something fierce. Six of them, one for each punch that I threw. And one for the kick.

The clock ticked a second time, and the catgirl smiled.

"Mrrm. You moved faster and struck harder than some Hunters!" She said with a bow, white tail swaying behind her.

"I did? That's good to hear!" I laughed, bowing in turn. "Thank you for the spar, Pitou."

"It is always an honour to aid you, my King." She said, "Oh, and your growth was... hrrmm... eight times larger. The same as the one from the book, my King."

"Good to know!"


[Muscular] |Elite Ability|
MHA - You can freely increase the density and mass of muscle fibres in your body, massively increasing your explosive power and durability. If you increase the fibres more than they can fit in your skin, they will cover the outside of your body, wrapping around you like a second set of muscles. The more muscle fibres you have the faster you will burn through your stamina.

...
​

My last Gold Ticket burned got me that.

I raised my arm again, and right before my eyes, I covered my forearm in muscle. Literally covered it in muscles, like red ribbons winding up and down my arm.

The guy who had this ability in My Hero Academia...

His name had been Goto, if I recall correctly. He was also called "Muscular," the namesake of this ability. Or 'Quirk' in his world. He'd cover his whole body in these bulging red muscles like these ones that covered my arm. Then he'd use 'em to tear through solid concrete like wet cardboard, shrug off bullets, and laugh off impacts that would turn a normal ningen body into red paste on the gravel. Then he'd laugh harder as he turned the other guy into that same red smear.

Oppressive speed and overwhelming force. No one could say that he didn't have those.

Muscle fibers bulging past the skin. Layered and dense beyond anything a ningen can naturally have, beyond even what bears could have. Except, Old Goto just reinforced everything everywhere because he didn't understand what needed reinforcing and how.

It was a damn waste, couldn't lie. But he was stronger than everyone else, and that's all that mattered to him.

Goto had been just that kind of man.

Like so many people with their money, he didn't want to master his Quirk, he just wanted to indulge in it. Feeling his enemies bones give under his punches. Squeeze skulls like oranges in his hand. Pull a spine apart like a french fry. Crumple people up into a ball of leaking flesh and splintered bone like a ball of paper. And overall, just revelling in the carnage that overwhelming physical strength could give.

It was a hobby that I could respect, even if it came from a ningen. But that didn't change the fact that he'd been standing on a mountain of potential, and all he did was jump up and down on it, enjoying life.

That's the curse of an uncurious mind, I'd say. No sense of natural wonder.

But if someone else had that Quirk... If it had been in the hands of someone who understood biomechanics. All the way how physical force transferred from muscle to joint to bone and back again. How a slight shift in fiber alignment could multiply force without increasing mass? How to angle them muscle fibers along optimal pull lines? How to optimize the contraction sequences?

That man would have been five times faster and ten times stronger than Goto on his best day. And if he wasn't a man at all, but a devil? Well, even better, devil muscle was stronger and denser than ningen muscle after all. And that devil would be the one who had oppressive speed and overwhelming force.

I shrugged my black jacket on, and fixed my dark hat... all under my clingy catgirl patient stare.

"Well, Pitou, let's get to foraging for that fruit basket." I smiled at her, ruffling her downy soft hair under my palm.

"Mrr~rrm!" The catgirl nodded enthusiastically, and lifted an arm to point, "There is a bush full of ripe cranberries on the opposite side of that hill, my King. Over there."

With my [Byakugan], I glanced in the direction she was pointing at. "The one beside the leaning tree with the bird's nest with five eggs?"

"Mm!"

"Good find! We'll head over there first!" I laughed. "And before I forget...Ichabod! Stand guard, will you? We'll be back in a few."

Glowing yellow eyes stared unblinkingly. The mouth didn't move, of course. Just a solid peace of metal without articulation. However... there was one addition that allowed Old Ichabod another way to express himself.

"Blades working?" I asked the construct.

Immediately, ghostly blue blades extending out from under Ichabod's gleaming amber-brown 'gauntlets' like wrist blades. The Carian Slicer modules looked like they were working fine, and looked sharp enough to cut diamonds.

"Good man." I smiled, patting his arm as Pitou and I headed out.

And on the way, we passed by [Bronze Creation] greatest creation yet: A massive metal frame that could have fit me. Hoops where muscle could hook and pull on, just an exoskeleton. And even... This was only just the prototype, the beginning.

I smiled.

And in the dark, of a deep forest where no human was meant to go, a devil and his ilk grew stronger as the nights passed.

Just as Satan intended.


(The day after) [Gremory Castle - Underworld]

Rias Gremory sat by the window, sighing like the princess that she was forcibly cosplaying as.

A full-length powder-blue dress with long, slender silk gloves that reached past her elbows. She noticed that she wearing more powder blue dresses lately. Her mother's personal preference for her. It really gave her that fairy tale princess feel, her mother said, especially when she looked longingly out the open window with her red hair in the gentle breeze.

It was becoming one of her new favourite pastimes, she was sure.

Chin resting on her gloved palm, she looked out over the estate, trying to make her eyesight pierce through the outer perimeter walls, through the capital city of the Gremory Domain, and to the train station where her peerage was embarking the train bound for Kuoh Town.

Rias was sad to see her peerage off, but it was for everyone's good.

Except hers, of course.

She blew a strand of loose red hair from her face.

She could already feel herself losing her grip on sanity under the weight of all the remedial etiquette that she had to suffer through. The endless book readings over the bestselling novels of the 1600s were already grating even with Akeno accompanying her, and the continuous suite of dance lessons were demanding enough with her own knight piece coordinating with her. But worst of all, if she had to spend another hour, swaying and bobbing on the family camel's back around the family property to practice her camel-riding one more time...

A set of taps on the solid oak of her door echoed out in her room, and immediately she sat prim and princess-like, turning her head ever so slightly so that the window light caught her face like a painting.

"You may come in," She said in her most regal voice.

"Lady Rias." The maid entered and bowed before her, offering a golden platter on which sat a letter, "Your correspondence for the day."

Her painted lips smiled, this time genuine as she accepted the letter. Anything from the outside world was welcome.

"Thank you, you are dismissed."

The parchment had a nice fragrance of fresh fruits, and a grassy scent of Japanese pine that reminded her of Kuoh actually. Or maybe she was more homesick than she thought and she was imagining it all. That was also possible, maybe even likely.

But she could at least take comfort that sending them back was the right—

The letter almost slipped through her trembling fingers as she read the curt but world-ending message. She stumbled to her feet, almost tripping on her powder blue dress as she called out.

"Maids! Someone get me Sirzechs-onii-sama!"


Dear Rias Gremory,

Akeno Himejima. Koneko Toujou. Yuuto Kiba. Gasper Vladi.

With Love,
Malal Nebiros

- The Empty Quiver of Satan End -​

AN:

Finally, we're gaining forward momentum again!... Enough politics and talking, let's get to the gacha, the power ups, the violence, and the Rias bullying.

Again, the power floor is lowered... Sirzechs can't just casually solo the entirety of Heaven like canon would suggest. That would be beyond my ability to write for this kind of story.

Even if Mal rolled a familiar like a... Gardevoir or a Pillar Man or a Fate character, the reaction would have still been the same. The local family of mad scientists succeeded in making their own Frankenstein's monster/artificial creature. Honestly, it would have worked best with Resident Evil's Nemesis rather than HunterxHunter's Neferpitou. But a cat is fine too.

The Chaos Gacha really really wants me to build the... Tequila Gundam. It's even a shade of bronze brown.

Kudos to @andreykl for predicting what I planned... not the first time someone's made an accurate prediction, but it is the first prediction to come true.

#3,825
Chapter 7: Esoteric Cowboy Magicks

In which... Sona engages in the most challenging battle of wits of her life (Not with Mal, of course.) Mal gets handed immense magical power that would have all the kin of Nebiros bow before him. And Rias' peerage, and by extension Rias herself, gets strike two with Magical Girl Levia-tan☆.



"I believe the cat won't be able to control herself any longer, she'll do something reckless soon and that'll be our chance..."

"Of course."

"And be sure to take Heracles along with you, you've heard how bored he is."

​

[Kuoh Town Botanical Park]

The sun shone warm above the park, over vibrant beds of tulips in riotous bloom.

Sona Sitri couldn't deny it was a beautiful day for an arms deal.

On the park bench, she checked the leather briefcase for the third time in so many minutes. Gleaming brass buckles unclasped just enough for her to peek inside, and the dark shape inside pulsed with demonic power. She had been assured that it was inert enough, and wouldn't spontaneously explode in her possession. However, she had handled a few in the past, and she knew that statement was suspect. Regardless, she clasped it back shut and sat back with her pocket book on children's pedagogy, her secret weapon for focusing her mind and steadying her nerves. She was going to need them in the next scant minutes.

Figures continued to rush in the distance, past the rows of plum-blossom pinks and canola yellows and past the maple trees: her peerage concluding their final checks.

No alarms had been sounded thus far. Though she almost wished they had, something minor to prove that their patrols were working properly, rather than suffering through the constant dread that the perimeter was already compromised and they simply hadn't noticed the intruders. But as it stood...

"Sona-kaichou," A tall shadow stood over her, darkening the open pages of her book. "The perimeter is secure, and I have verified the illusion spells on all entrances to be functioning. We won't have any civilians wandering in."

... As it stood, it was too late to reconsider her opening move now.

The book snapped shut in her palm, and Sona stood up from the bench. "May I assume the driver's last report also held no irregularity, Tsubaki?"

"Yes. They are in transit and on schedule. Our guest will be arriving shortly."

Sona glanced at the taller girl, and checked her head to toe.

Long dark-hair, freshly trimmed and freshly conditioned. Square-framed teal glasses, not a speck. Kuoh Academy blouse and magenta skirt, without a crease out of place. Brown dress shoes, parade worthy polish. There was no question that this was the correct attire for her and her peerage. They needed to tie themselves to this territory— A mode of dress to act as a non-verbal reminder of their station and their authority here in Kuoh Town.

Sona straightened her own uniform.

"Let's take no chances," she said, handing the leather briefcase to Tsubaki, "Have Reya monitor their approach, and ensure that there are no complications or surprises."

"As you say," Tsubaki replied, a communication spell circle already spinning in her palm.

This will not end like Rias' attempt did.

Her Serafall-onee-sama had already done the difficult work: snatched their reputation out from the flames, pared off the burnt pieces of Rias' blunder into a vaguely diplomatic shape, and bought them a second chance to play the gracious host. Now, the move was Sona's and her objectives were clear. Receive the Lucifer Six heir properly for his extended stay in Kuoh Town.

Convince him, and by extension his fearsome House, that they needed the strength and resources of the Seventy-Two.

Her eyes went to Reya who kept watch by the park's main roadside entrance. Her bishop piece's chin high and her arm bandaged all the way from her wrist up into her sleeve, and all the way until it peeked above her collar, behind a curtain of brown hair. And Sona knew that, all across the park grounds, the rest of her peerage also held their posts with equal injured discipline. Limping proudly with their bruises and fractures, neatly dressed in bandages and makeup.

Yes, what a magnificent advertisement.

"Sona-kaichou, this is them," Tsubaki whispered as a black luxury sedan rolled to a halt before them. A razor-clean silhouette of glossy black finish and polished chrome loaned by one of the Sitri-associated human companies.

The uniformed chauffeur, another loan, nodded his silent greeting before opening the passenger door.

She, Tsubaki, and Reya all bowed low.

"Prince Nebiros," Sona said, "I humbly welcome you to Kuoh Town proper."

She wasn't her onee-sama; she still belonged to House Sitri. Their princely rank among the Seventy-Two mattered little in regards to the Six Houses of Lucifer whose seat sat far above theirs. Proper respect for the traditional hierarchy must be shown, particularly when reintegration was the goal. And if House Sitri just happened to be the first amongst the Seventy-Two to give the sole surviving Lucifer Six house their due and benefit from the rebridged relations? Well, her onee-sama won't lose sleep over it. Not with this guest.

"Feels mighty awkward being called a prince," The Lucifer Six heir said warmly, stepping out the car and onto sun-baked pavement. "Just call me Mal."

"Very well, Mal-dono." Sona straightened to meet him face-to-face.

He was dressed the same, she observed, still in that black-suited cowboy ensemble. Thumbs hooked on his belt, showing off that same oversized belt buckle bearing the House Nebiros sigil that gleamed under the morning sun. And past the brim of his dark cowboy hat, their eyes met and he smiled.

"Howdy," he said with a tip of his hat.

The last time he'd uttered that specific word a few days ago, everything went horribly wrong for them. But much to Sona's relief, there was no brass stroller with him. No folded up golem ready to break her peerage's bones under giant metal fists. It appeared that he had truly come in peace... If only that sentiment lasted longer.

"Now, I hope you don't mind," He began with that same deceptively friendly smile. "but I brought my familiar this time around."

Her heart immediately rose to hammer in her ears as she said, "W-We do not mind, Mal-dono."

She could almost hear Tsubaki's rising heart rate too. Because unlike before, Serafall had briefed them thoroughly and very seriously. They knew precisely what his 'familiar' was, and what it meant for the Underworld and for every plane of existence.

And out the car's dark interior, like an apex predator stalking out of its den, House Nebiros' greatest known creation emerged.

A white-haired nekomata stepped out under the proud eyes of her master. Cat ears flicked in the breeze and a white tail swished behind a blue coat. An attentive, open expression on her pale face. She wasn't smiling, not quite, but there was a brightness behind her eyes as if every piece of the world held her interest.

And unfortunately, that included them.

Blood red eyes flicked to Tsubaki, then to Reya, then to four other places out of sight, places that just happened to coincide with her other peerage members' posts, before finally settling on her... and staying there. All with that open-eyed, mildly interested expression.

"Greetings, Princess Sitri," the snowy-haired nekoshou bowed politely. "I am Neferpitou, a mere humble Familiar to my King."

Chimera.

They were not new to the biomedical magisciences after all. Rare, but not unheard of. Whether through forbidden esoteric rituals, deliberate acts of butchery or even freak accidents involving Sacred Gear, they invariably existed as cautionary tales rather than as any real threats. Their creation often involved the sacrifice of a low-class devil and some magical creatures to polymerize a gurgling, temperamental and scarcely-middle-class beast that possessed barely enough thought to follow its master's orders, if at all. They were pitiful creatures, born of flawed processes which operated upon incomplete understanding of magical theories on the subject matter... All to produce something that was worth less than the sum of their parts.

But not this chimera.

"Ain't she the prettiest thing?!" the devil-in-black chuckled, scratching the abomination behind its ear as if it was a regular cat.

"Mrrmm~ My King..."

Because House Nebiros did not make such amateurish mistakes or suffer from such embarrassing failures. Instead, they proved once again that in matters of biomedical magisciences they were without peer... by producing loyal, obedient, and incredibly dangerous chimeras.

"Yes... a-a pleasure to meet your acquaintance, Neferpitou-san," Sona bowed in return, holding back what she really wanted to say.

A formerly Naberius-aligned nekoshou traumatized by events she could scarcely remember, and a catgirl chimera appearing the night after their offense against a Lucifer Six heir. She understood what her sister meant by 'coincidences.' The pieces fit so perfectly together that it was painful.

But that didn't matter, especially not now.

Accusations of horrific genetic experimentations had no place here. Things have changed too much after House Nebiros' clawed hand reached out from the uncharted wastelands of the Underworld to tip the scales of diplomacy too far in their favour. The vanguard of their vast artificial army front and center for the world to see. Standing right before her, in fact.

This reaction was admittedly their fault to begin with.

Her Serafall-onee-sama had surmised that someone within their House, perhaps the Lord Nebiros himself, did not appreciate the attempt to essentially vassalize their runaway heir. It had been incredibly foolish for them to even try in hindsight: Assigning someone of Mal's station to the humiliatingly subservient position of a lowly peerage member to a 72 Pillar House heiress, sister of a New Satan or no. Had they known that he was the heir, they wouldn't have— No, there was no use for that thinking anymore; they could only hope to set things right now.

Past the violet rim of her glasses, Sona glanced at the leather briefcase in Tsubaki's hand. She merely needed to choose the right moment... In fact, she was going to bring it about herself.

So, Sona smiled, and made her move.

"I welcome you both to Kuoh Town." She said, and with a graceful sweep of her hand, indicated to the lush, blooming park beyond. "Serafall-onee-sama mentioned your affection for nature, so I arranged for the entire park to be reserved for us. Would you honour me with a walk?"

"Your sister sure knows what I like," The devil-in-black let out a short laugh. "Can't deny that I do prefer open skies over ceilings anyday. Let's walk and talk."

"Excellent," She smiled, and began walking.

Their 'casual' and 'innocent' walk in the park began like that— with her taking the lead and asserting her 'ownership' of the territory as the host of this exchange. Their respective attendants fell in step with them: Tsubaki trailing by her side, and the chimera by his.

Nothing but the breeze rustling through the park spoke for the first, quiet minute.

Then, as planned, they came across one of her pieces.

Momo appeared beyond the spread of tulip beds, the white-haired girl stark against the greenery, especially with the blue medical sling that cradled her still recovering arm. Her bishop piece did not appear to notice them passing as she reinforced the perimeter. Underlings obediently fulfilling her commands and tending to her very secure and very prosperous territory in full view of guests... it was practically word-for-word from the hospitality manuals penned in the Underworld. And Sona Sitri was nothing if not studious.

Sona personally did not enjoy reducing her peerage to purely performative roles, but needs must. She needed to impart the impression that Kuoh Town was sufficiently well-managed. And thus, the path they'll take will have them come across her other peerage members as well.

She was prepared to move on when...

"What's that spell she's casting?" Malal asked, and she followed his eyes to spot a magic circle spinning in Momo's hand, and the air rippled around her like a mirage.

"Merely the Falsefront illusion spell." she supplied, watching with him, "In my opinion, a severely underappreciated spell. I personally have found it indispensable in my duties as the day-to-day administrator to Kuoh Town. Some devils prefer hypnosis spells to manage the non-supernatural portions of the human population. But I've found that with even minimal forward planning, the illusory walls and gates and other objects are vastly more efficient."

"Doesn't sound like a bad thing to have…" Malal rubbed his chin contemplatively.

"It is, and I highly recommend it," Sona said and found herself slipping into the lecture despite herself. "Falsefront is often overlooked for its lack of offensive effects in Rating Games. But it has immense value in manipulating enemy movement, misdirecting their energy and attention, and delaying their command decisions at crucial moments. I can say without embellishment that a single, well-placed illusion can win a match."

"Yep, I figured as much," His eyes remained on Momo's spellwork. "Wouldn't mind learnin' that spell myself. And by the way you say it, I'd wager you've taught that lesson before."

"Only to those willing to learn it properly," Sona shared a knowing glance with Tsubaki. It was a rudimentary spell among the 72 Pillar Houses; a Lucifer Six house would have certainly taught their heir the bare basics. Unless...

Another of Serafall's theories was proven true, it seemed.

House Nebiros had clearly invested in shaping and specializing their heirs for the 'family profession,' and left baseline combat training as an afterthought. The 72 Pillars, by contrast, expected every noble child—from the most minor of noble families to the scions of the Seventy-Two pillars—to uphold a somewhat competent standard of combat readiness if only to win in Rating games. No devil family would turn away their chance to advance beyond their station. But House Nebiros, an outsider to modern devil society, did not participate in Rating Games. And as a consequence— here their heir stood, lacking what modern devil society would consider basic knowledge.

If she didn't know better, she'd have thought Malal Nebiros to be one of those occasional country bumpkins that would wander into the city from the remote edges of devil territories in search of fame and fortune.

But Sona knew he was anything but.

His black suit swung open slightly from a passing breeze, and she caught the glint of the rows of cutting-edge magical artificery tucked inside.

The irony of the heir to the most scientifically-minded House among the Lucifer Six being so brilliant and sophisticated that it had turned around that he resembled a country bumpkin... Sona politely resolved to never bring that comparison up. However, the topic of Rating Game magicks did present the opening that she was waiting for.

She turned to him. "This does bring us to a matter I was instructed to raise during your stay."

"From Serafall?" His eyebrows raised in interest beneath the shadow of his cowboy hat.

"From House Sitri, actually," She smiled diplomatically.

"Well, I'm listening."

Sona looked the other devil in the eye. "Malal Nebiros, given the regrettable confusion that accompanied your arrival, House Sitri thought it proper that our first formal gesture should be more substantial than a warm welcome and some pleasant scenery. A work of magic, offered in good faith, seemed more suitable. Especially considering your interest in practical spellcraft. So, on behalf of House Sitri, I would like to present House Nebiros with a gift."

She didn't need to gesture to her Tsubaki, there was no need. The taller girl stepped forward on cue, the leather briefcase now in both hands. Demonic power oozed out as the clasps clicked open before Sona reached into the opening... and pulled out the source of the demonic power.

"A book?" Malal said as he laid eyes on the thing in her hands.

Bound in thick red leather and trimmed with polished brass, it contained a stack of fine vellum pages that was yellowed with age.

"A spell tome," Sona corrected, and she felt relief as his eyes immediately lit up with interest. "This particular tome holds the spell for the creation of a dimensional pocket. Forgive my ignorance on the topic, it is an advanced spell and I know little of the finer details. But I do however understand that this spell is a progenitor to what Ajuka-sama used for the development of the Rating Game's field."

By the end of her modest explanation, the devil-in-black genuinely amazed by it, just as Serafall said he would be.

Her smile was small and formal as she stepped forward, red tome in hand. "I offer this object of scholarship from my House to yours. A modest contribution to the hope of cooperation between our Houses..." she offered the hefty tome for him to accept, "... if you'd accept it."

Malal tilted his cowboy hat back and let the sun fall full upon his smile. "I do humbly accept House Sitri's most generous gift!"

Sona kept her mouth shut behind a polite and diplomatic smile.

It was not from House Sitri's archives.

This spell tome had never rested beneath Sitri wards, nor was it passed down through generations of Sitri hands. No, it had actually been in the possession of the Four Satans. A trophy lying on some gold pile in Lucifaad's vast vaults of war spoils, untouched from the era when Devilkind still waged war against the Angels, against the Fallen, against other pantheons... And, when bored of that, against each other. In plainer terms, it was not House Sitri's to give, but the Four Satans had deemed it more palatable if a noble House of the Seventy-Two presented it. An apology/tribute from House Sitri was easy to accept, but a barefaced bribe from the Four Satans to coax House Nebiros back into the fold would have been far less elegant.

Her father was only overjoyed to have House Sitri benefit as a diplomatic middleman.

"Lord Sitri also wishes to extend his formal assurances," Sona recited, just as she practiced this morning, "that House Nebiros will find its presence here… greeted with warm hospitality among the Seventy-Two, for as long as it so chooses to avail it."

"That's mighty kind of him 'specially with this gift. Tell him I said thanks!"

"No, thank you." She smiled in return.

It was indeed a generous gift/bribe. Perhaps too generous, but then again, that does sound like her Serafall-onee-sama.

"But a word of caution, Nebiros-dono..." She said, not yet letting go of the tome as she looked him in the eye, "This spell tome is from Pre-Crucifixion Era and thus written entirely in Infernal."

"Infernal. Got it, I'll... uh be careful opening it," He accepted the tome with a look of confusion.

Seeing that reaction, Sona felt stupid for saying such a warning now. Of course a child of Nebiros like him would likely have read several if not dozens of different tomes written in it by now. She wouldn't be surprised if his House's archives were written in nothing but the ancient script of Devilkind. After all, Infernal only demanded immense caution from those not of pure devil blood. Or as Old Satan Apologists would say, those...

Her eyes went to her peerage, reincarnated humans all.

... those not of 'their kind.'

There was no helping it with Infernal, the language was rooted to the nature of Demonic Power itself.

It was because Demonic Power had never belonged wholly to reason. Spells borne of Demonic Power were brought about by vivid imagination. And teaching them was akin to artists teaching artists. Hand on hand, gently guiding them through the brush strokes, through the instincts, and the swirl of emotions and the forests of meanings.

For that reason, Demonic power laughed contemptuously in the face of the safe, formulaic, and above all, laughably magicless rulership of mortal language.

Write 'water' in a thousand human tongues and what had been meant? A still pond reflecting the stars? A raging rapid near-boiling? A clear liquid inside a volumetric cylinder? Was this 'water' briny with seasalt or as pure as freshly fallen snow on one's tongue? One word, a thousand waters and a thousand-thousand ways to cast a wholly different spell. Attempting to add more words only quadrupled the confusion. How still was still? How raging was raging? How briny was briny? Ad nauseum. The eyes fail and the mind wanders as different upbringings pollute the essence of its true meaning.

Properly written Infernal—ritualistically steeped in so much demonic power that it oozed malevolence—'solved' that problem.

It had done so for millenia, just not gently.

The primordial devil script forced the reader to imagine along the grooves of the writer's will. To read Infernal was to let another mind reach its fingers behind one's eyes and twist your inner world by force.

Infernal to a pure-blooded devil was to have one's mind, consciousness and soul all expanded as pure concepts were driven into them like nails on wood.

A magician's grimoire detailing the rudimentary Waterblast spell may require familiarity with evocation arcana, background knowledge in fluid mechanics, and weeks or even months of practice. Yet, within a single reading, an Infernal Tome could impart the knowledge of that same spell with perfect clarity to a dyslexic, dim-witted wild child raised by wolves.

Of all the ways to gain great power, none was a faster or easier path as Infernal Tomes.

At least, if one was a pure blooded devil.

The experience of reading Infernal Tomes by anyone else was far less pleasant. The mere sight of it was a corrupting influence to their being. A strong-willed human mundane could perhaps absorb the knowledge of a basic spell from an Infernal Tome, leaving a permanent pulsating scar on his sanity. Magicians could read the entire book if they girded themselves with all the proper precautions, but even then, they only did so with varying degrees of perilous risk of reducing themselves to cackling madmen. Only Reincarnated Devils or Pact Magicians with their devil patrons could truly read Infernal... but the risk of spontaneous or gradual mental unraveling was never completely eliminated if the current studies were to be trusted.

She'd even heard rumors of devils who descended from bloodlines 'tainted' with non-devil blood suffering the same fate as well.

Mere rumours, of course, but if it was true...

... It would be poetic in a way. When even a drop of human blood availed one a chance to be born with a Sacred Gear, that very same drop also denied them the ability to read Infernal Tomes without fatal risk.... There was a symmetry to it, as if it had been planned. The Blessings of Heaven and the Curses of Hell upon the mortal. The former was divine intervention granted without ceremony to chosen few, the latter was a dangerous gamble that anyone can take of their own free will.

It was the classic Faustian bargain. Granting power as effortlessly as it extracted its price. A spell learned without need for direct instruction, requisite knowledge or even basic literacy... at a risk.

Very quintessentially devil.

That was what made Infernal Tomes so dangerous and so very valuable for mortals.

And there was no end to the number of people who would take such a deal. Magicians, reincarnated devils, devils of diluted bloodlines, and even mundane humans willing to risk it all for a scrap of power... So, it came as no surprise then that—for the public's own safety—Infernal Tomes were tightly controlled both within and beyond the Underworld. Fantastically illegal for those whose family names were not found carved into the foundations of Lucifaad by the Original Satan Himself.

And naturally, there were Six carved closest to His name.

But even if Infernal Tomes wasn't a controlled item, few noble houses would ever trade one away. There were simply too few of them left: Those sparse faithful copies and those precious surviving originals. Making them was already a half-lost art, and those who might have revived it were half-lost as well. House Paimon was nearly extinct. House Ronové was gone entirely in the flames of the Civil War.

Sona looked at the tome in Malal's hands enviously.

House Sitri possessed its own treasured Infernal Tomes, written by her ancestors' hands and locked away until the heiress, namely herself, was strong enough to wield the power sealed within their pages. Her house would sooner fight to extinction than surrender those (As many have before). And even the lesser tomes, they were reluctant to trade away.

Sona understood that reluctance perfectly.

She'd been reluctant to hand this one over herself, and it wasn't even hers!

At least... She was able to sneak a peek inside—just a few pages to ensure the spell tome was presentable, of course—and as she had turned the pages, for one orgasmic instant, she had almost grasped it: the spell to incarnate one's own personal plane of existence.

Then the blinding headache had slammed into her, and she had to cease her 'peeking.' Clearly, even as a Pure-blood devil, she was not yet at the level required to comprehend this spell, perhaps not in one day.

She could not imagine how arduous it would be to read the thing in full!

Regardless, the arms deal/bribery/apology was done, and she need only to make the closing remarks. So, fixing her purple glasses, Sona smiled. "I trust that this Infernal Tome is to your satisfaction, Nebiros-san?"

Her guest did not answer. He only stood there with his head bowed in the sunny day, wholly absorbed in the tome gripped in his hands.

"Please, take your time, Nebiros-dono." Sone said with a small indulgent laugh. She could hardly fault him for it; she had worn much the same look when her Serafall-onee-sama first placed the tome in her possession. If anything, his silent concentration reassured her more than any polite compliment could have. It meant the choice of gifts was very effective, her sister would be pleased to hear that.

A brief glance between her and Tsubaki, and the taller girl returned it with a faint smile and a nod.

Yes, this was going very well.

Birds chirped around them as a happy minute ticked by, then followed by two more. The third one was markedly less happy. And by the fifth minute, a rising feeling of concern began to build up in the back of her throat. The time did not bother her... but the fact that he not once moved a single muscle across those many minutes did.

Sona cleared her throat, and tried again. "I trust that this artifact is to your satisfaction, Malal-san?"

Still no response. The cowboy in black was seemingly frozen where he stood on the park footpath. Stuck in the same pose, his hands still gripping the tome's leather edges in the same places and his face stuck in an unreadable expression as he stared into the cover of the tome.

"... Mal?" She leaned down to peek under the brim of his cowboy hat, finding that he wasn't even blinking, expression locked in intense, unmoving concentration. "Is everything- Are you alright?"

And when no answer came, panic struck full force and Sona's mind immediately raced through the possible causes of why her guest was suddenly petrified. Was it an adverse magical reaction? Was there an assassin that had stung him with a paralytic without anyone noticing? Had the tome been switched for a boobytrapped one? Or was it—

"My King?"

His nekoshou-chimera—the one that Sona had all but forgotten was even there—stepped up to her frozen master with a naked look of concern on her pale face. Her red eyes shifting from his unresponsive expression, then down to the tome in his hands, then up... to Tsubaki and her.

"What..." came the razor sharp whisper, "...did your tome do to him?"

And suddenly, Sona was very keenly aware that not only did they have a very dangerous and very protective chimera in their midst, but that they also did not have anything that could have any hope of stopping said chimera should it turn hostile.

"No. No, that's impossible. I mean— We didn't do this!" She forced herself to breathe. How does one explain such complexities to a familiar? That they were innocent in all this?

But to Sona's surprise and horror, she didn't need to.

Blood red eyes searched her own violet ones. "... You knew and anticipated that this 'Infernal Tome' would enforce an effect," the nekoshou-chimera correctly surmised, her eyes focusing even more on them. "The effects, their trigger conditions and the deactivation clauses. You will explain them to me."

"W-what?"

Serafall had warned her about the chimera's strength, its speed, its reflexes; she had not warned her about the mind inside it.

Her sister had spoken as though they were facing an unusually obedient and powerful familiar, nothing more: just a creature capable of simple problem-solving, crude adaptation, low animal cunning passed off as forward planning, even the assumption of a humanoid shape, all the ordinary useful skills that familiars performed when properly trained. All of them—herself, her sister and possibly the rest of the Satans—had expected this Neferpitou to be that, perhaps smarter than the common familiar, and certainly more than the bestial chimeras they were accustomed to.

Nothing about her had suggested otherwise! Through the entire meeting she had worn the same airy expression of faint, cucumber-water interest... listening, blinking, drifting behind her own eyes with the blank expression of some well-behaved and harmlessly detached familiar.

Until now.

Now they were face to face with a creature capable of making an observation, drawing from it a rational deduction, then proceeding with a precise and methodical attempt to reverse-engineer an answer to an abstract problem.

And her preliminary conclusion: They had the information she sought.

An immense pressure pressed down on them as Neferpitou stepped forward,clawed hands flexing and unflexing, and red eyes still locked to them. "Effects, trigger conditions, deactivation clauses. Now."

"Tsubaki, don't." Sona warned through gritted teeth, not needing to glance away from the chimera. She knew her queen piece had been readying for combat that they could not win, judging from the strange magical aura pressing down on them. She frowned. "Neferpitou-san, Infernal Tomes are—"

"Sorry, my mind went ridin' off without me." said a suddenly unfrozen Malal Nebiros, clearing his head with a little shake.

"My King!"

The pressure vanished with her. One instant the air had been crumbling beneath that pressure; the next it was gone, and the chimera was by Malal, pressing her face into his side like a cat.

"Hey, Pitou," He laid a hand on the nekoshou-chimera's back and rubbed slow circles, the Infernal Tome resting in his other palm. "Worried you, huh? I'm fine, clingy thing. Just drifted a spell, that's all. Did I miss somethin'?"

Neferpitou's eyes lifted to Sona. "Mr~rm..."

"Nothing requiring your concern, Mal," Sona cut in, stepping smoothly over whatever answer the creature might have given. "We were merely feeling restless over the extended silence. You are... Are you feeling alright?"

"Sure am. Never better, I'd say!" Malal's grin widened, looking completely untroubled despite his strange bout of petrification a moment ago. He patted the tome. "It was just me uh... getting a feel of this here Infernal Tome!"

"I trust that this artifact is to your satisfaction?" Sona complimented. Perhaps it had been a ritualistic trance for divination? She'd heard of abilities such as that. "I understand that even a tome's cover alone is... informative, to the properly trained eye. A devil with your impressive insight of artificery must have drawn more from than most."

Her eyes went to the part of his black suit jacket where rows of his 'Spell Totems' lay hidden under. It was bold, perhaps even rude, to pry into another House's private research, but the Satans would appreciate any answers, thus Sona had to make the attempt.

"It's a mighty fine gift! Beats my last birthday by a country mile!"

"Then I'm pleased it has come into appreciative hands," Sona moved on before he took offense. Attempt failed. Malal remained guarded about his research, as expected; pushing for more at this point would have been impatient, and 'impatient and aggressive' was not her way. The Satans will have their answers to this specific question another day. But they'll at least be pleased to hear that the tome had succeeded in its function as a bribe/apology.

Sona levelled a polite smile at their guest. "Would you like some tea, Mal?"

"Iced with a lemon wedge for me!" Malal laughed and scratched beneath Neferpitou's chin. "How about you, clingy cat? You want somethin' fancy too?"

"Mr~rm," The snowy-haired chimera purred beneath his hand. "I have everything I need, my King."

Sona smiled indulgently. "There is a local bakeshop that makes cakes from honey made fresh in this same town," She said, not looking away as she gestured to her queen piece to prepare the refreshments. "Tsubaki, if you will..."

The taller girl bowed once before marching away.

Anything to stall for a bit longer.

Because now, they had a more immediate, nekoshou-shaped concern; and her sister, the Satan, would be very interested in whatever could be learned of the chimera's limits. The difference was not small: A powerful beast could be hunted, but if that beast was in actuality another hunter in disguise, one who was stronger, faster and smarter than those that hunted her... Yes, they needed to know. Pets and familiars were tested with tricks and orders. But for testing a reasoning mind... Well, she had just the thing.

Her gaze drifted to the center of the park, where all the stone paths and swaying flowerbeds seemed to converge on. Sona's gaze drifted toward the center of the park, where all the stone paths and swaying flowerbeds seemed to converge on. There the park gazebo stood, a graceful pyramidal roof held aloft by slim wooden pillars, open to every passing breeze. And in the center of its shade, there 'coincidentally' was...

A chess table.

And for the first time in their meeting, Sona smiled her first dishonest smile.

"There is one indulgence I would ask..." she said, guiding their path toward the gazebo as though the idea came to her just now. Their footsteps sounded softly on the wooden floorboards. "While refreshments are brought, perhaps you would grant me a game?"

Malal glanced at the board, then scratched his jaw with a wince. "I ain't much for chess, but I reckon I can lose polite enough."

"Forgive me, Mal. But I did not mean a game with you," Sona smiled politely, resting a hand on the chess' black piece side before levelling a stare at the chimera. "I meant a game with her."

Neferpitou blinked up at them, airy and vacant once more, all soft eyes and empty grace.

Malal's hand stilled on the chimera's back. He looked down at Neferpitou, back to Sona, and raised one eyebrow under his hat. "You want to play chess..." he said, "....with my cat?"

"Yes," Sona replied. She had planned to have a match or three with the Nebiros heir to gauge his temperament, personality and his intelligence. Because while her sister was 'blessed' with the ability to make semi-accurate estimation of people just from first impressions, Sona Sitri on the other hand could do that with Chess. And today, she had just the target. "That is if she is familiar with the rules?"

Malal looked down to the nekoshou-chimera in his arm. "Do you, girl?"

Neferpitou did not answer immediately. The delay was small enough to be missed by anyone who wasn't actively looking, so Sona certainly did not miss it. The chimera's red eyes lowered to the chessboard, and something focused right behind the airy blankness. Like a shark beneath the surface.

"... I do."

Sona Sitri sat down with a smile behind the ranks of black pieces, opening her arms wide with well-earned arrogance as she welcomed her opponent to her domain.

"Prove it."

(Meanwhile) [Gremory Castle]

"C'mon, pick up, Sona..." Rias Gremory muttered as she paced.

But the call-spell stayed unanswered, ringing continuously just beyond the sleeve of her blue, camel-print pajamas. She paced some more.

The purple silk curtains of her bedroom behind her breathed inward with the breeze, brushing over her red hair and stirring up the faint scent of freshly-waxed wood furniture and the bowl of sugared citrus she had been too irritated to eat. And beside it on her writing desk, crumpled up parchment of unsatisfyingly-written letters scattered across the wood.

"Rias-sama."

"Tsubaki, finally!" Rias straightened, then immediately remembered that she's in her camel-print PJs and did away with the effort. "Tell Sona to stop ignoring my call-spells and answer me already!"

On the other side of the spell, Tsubaki's holographic image hovered above the magic circle: long black hair falling around her face, spectacles catching the warm yellowish light. And Rias knew immediately that it was that bakeshop that Sona always favoured in Kuoh Town. The display cases showing a buttery lineup of baked goods—the tarts and turnovers, and the cakes and croissants—were a dead giveaway. Tsubaki even looked to be sitting in the same illusion-hidden booth that the Student Council often used when they needed privacy from the human residents!

Tsubaki stirred honey into her steaming cup of tea. "Sona-kaichou is presently engaged in an important meeting and cannot be disturbed, so I must ask that you cease your attempts for the time being. She will contact you when the meeting has concluded."

"A meeting..." Rias went very still. "It's with the Nebiros bastard, isn't it?"

"... I cannot disclose Sona-kaichou's private appointments."

"So that's a yes," Rias deadpanned. "and you're there at Renée's... because she sent you to fetch tea and those little honey cakes she likes to give to every diplomatic guest to show off that beekeeping project she started. Serafall-sama hasn't told her that the Nebiros bastard had started sending me threatening letters, has she?"

"Nebiros-dono threatened your life?"

"Worse, Tsubaki. He threatened my peerage." Rias half-shouted, snatching the letter to wave at Tsubaki's hologram. "See? It has all their names on it, including Gasper! And nobody knows about Gasper unless I let them, not even his clients know his real name! He's been spying on us, Tsubaki, you have to tell Sona!"

Tsubaki's eyes went to the single-line of the letter. "We will take that under advisement."

"Don't just advise; tell her! Another House snooped around in House Gremory territory AND spied on both me and Sona! This is serious business!"

"I'll raise your concerns with Sona after the meeting."

"My 'concerns' isn't the problem, but him knowing things he shouldn't is! Where did she even take him for this meeting?"

"Rias-sama... After the last time, you were specifically warned by Serafall-sama against interfering."

"Last time," Rias snorted. As if last time had been entirely her fault! And as if the Nebiros heir had not walked in with that brass murder-statue and without knowing the history of his House's trespasses against her Koneko-chan! "I'm not asking because I intend to do anything stupid— I'm not interested in getting grounded for even longer. But Kuoh Town is still my territory. You can tell Sona that I asked, if it makes you feel better. Please?"

"I cannot assist you."

"Fine," Rias rolled her eyes and threw herself onto the edge of her bed. The mattress gave beneath her with a soft crinkle of fresh blankets. "It's not in Kuoh Academy, is it?"

Tsubaki said nothing, only sipping on her honeyed tea.

"Of course, it wouldn't be in Kuoh Academy," Rias stared at her bedroom ceiling, thinking deeply. "After last time, that would have been insulting to everyone involved. Also, etiquette for hosting fellow nobles demands that it must be private, but somewhere still inside the territory... Not at Renée's or else she'd be there with you right now. No, that bakeshop is too small anyway, and Sona would want to show off a little... I'd say the beekeeping farm, but that'd be too rustic and behind-the-scenes for a formal first meeting. She'd want somewhere with more presentation. And if this wasn't after a fight had broken out, I'd have said the theatre, but it's too close to the population center of Kuoh town—"

Rias sat up on her bed, eye wide and staring at Tsubaki's hologram.

"The botanical gardens."

"... You did not hear that from me." The other girl smiled at her.

"I know Kuoh Town and I know Sona! And now, etiquette too, thanks to remedial classes." Rias nodded proudly. She knew that the former Shinra clan member was nothing if not respectful of people actually figuring things out themselves.

"Then please allow me to be direct in turn," Tsubaki's voice sharpened and she looked at her through teal glasses. "Serafall-sama and Sona-kaichou has impressed upon us the importance of this diplomatic meeting. We of the student council will not hesitate to stop any attempts at interference. Even if or when it is to come from another 72 Pillar House."

Tsubaki was onto her now, Rias knew. Not that it'll change the call that'll come after this one.

"I'm not the enemy," She said with a sigh, "I'm only saying that Nebiros came because he has his own motives and reasons. And after that letter, we both know he's no longer pretending he doesn't have knives poised under the table."

Tsubaki's porcelain teacup clicked as she set it down.

"Serafall-sama knows that, and made certain that we do as well. That's why we're working to neutralize the threat he poses. Diplomatically and non-violently. Sona-kaichou herself was aware of the risks and wouldn't be doing this if she didn't believe we would succeed."

"You think she can handle him on her own?"

"She already is," Tsubaki said without hesitation, much to Rias' surprise. "When I left them, Lady Sona had him exactly where she was strongest. She has the situation, the terms, and him well in hand."

[Kuoh Town Botanical Park]

All eyes were glued to the board. To the game.

"Well, I'll be..." I muttered, reached up under my cowboy hat to scratch my head in awe. The board sat quiet in my side-on view of it. Black pieces and white pieces, scattered from that short, mean war... and a king all penned up at the edge of the board, nowhere left to run like some outlaw at the edge of a cliff, staring down the business-end of a sheriff's rifle and every deputy in the county.

Checked and Matered!

I laughed at the sorry sap whose king had just been cornered. "Miss, that was some tall talk for somebody who just lost to my cat!"


Feat Achieved! — House-Pet Level Chessmaster
Humiliate Sona Sitri by having your pet beat her in a fair game of chess.
+1 Gold Gacha Ticket

...
​

Serafall's little sis just buried her burning face in her palms. Her violet eyes peeked between her slender fingers, glued on the board where her king done got trapped. Her elbows rested on the table, and she looked like she couldn't decide between melting right off her chair into a puddle of shame, or keep staring at the board and replay the last twenty moves in her head to find where it all went wrong for her.

And to add salt on the wound, across from her, said snowy-haired cat just sat straight in her blue coat, looking like she didn't even sweat once all throughout that bloody match.

"You're really good at this, Pitou!" I just laughed, proudly slapping my cat over her back. "You made that look easy!"

"I have only seen several chess games," she said, "but many Gungi games."

"Ain't that literally a whole different boardgame?"

"Mr~rm... Yes, Sire," Pitou leaned into my palm. "But they operate under the same principle: Area denial and tempo control to acquire a position where unequal concessions can be forced. However, chess is the vastly more simple game of the two."

Sona's face snapped up with a glare ready. "Simple?!" She slammed both palms on the table, rattling the pieces. Violet eyes blazed behind her glasses, her cheeks flushed scarlet. "How dare you call chess simple?"

Pitou did not so much as twitch a cat ear.

Sona jabbed a finger at her. "You cannot wander into my territory, under the auspices of a diplomatic meeting, use an insulting irregular chess opening in our very first match, and then simultaneously declare—to my face—that I am an amateur and that chess is simple because of some allegedly superior game no civilized academy teaches! This has been the rudest match I have ever played, and I have played one against Sairaorg! But he, at least, did not insult me while yawning and picking his ears through that entire, short game! And more importantly, I demand a rematch!"

"What do you say, Pitou?" I looked down at my cat, still fighting the stupid grin pulling at my mouth. "You reckon you got another game in you?"

Neferpitou didn't even glance my way, just met Sona's stare head-on and said, "As many victories as you desire, my King."

The park gazebo just went quiet, 'cept for the breeze.

"You..." Sona's eyes narrowed into dangerous slits as she sat back down. "Set your pieces. I am going to make you eat those words."

I leaned back in my chair and let the grin win. Nothing like a little friendly competition between my cat and my future sister-in-law. It was nice seeing this side of Sona. She'd been all formal titles, careful posture, and graceful gestures all morning. But here, she looked almost normal!

Well.

As normal as you could look frothing at the mouth and angrily playing a grudge match with a cat girl.

Marble clicked on marble as the lady and the cat moved their pieces across the board. Pawn, pawn, pawn. Sona adjusted her glasses... then knight, bishop, queen, pawn, knight takes Sona's bishop. Then Sona took her eyes off the pieces to glare at my cat, exhaled through her nose, then went right back to ruminating the board... just like the first game.

That was how I knew Pitou was already winning.

But fun as the match was to watch—and as much as [Nature's Blessing] had a pleasant, flowery-tasting buzz of energy for me here—I had more interesting things to chew on.

The Infernal Tome rested heavy on my lap, and [Structural Analysis] fired again. A hefty stack of red, magma-salamander leather with brass-gold trim written in vellum made from unicorn hide. Didn't even need to know how much those went for in Underworld markets to know that this was expensive to make, and that was before we were tallying up what was written inside.

This damned thing sure would have been an expensive way to scorch my own brains clean out of my skull!

It took every scrap I knew from [King of All Trades] and [Intermediate Item Creation] to make heads or tails. But I had to admit... it was worth the trouble. Because now I had a new trick holstered.

My magic circle—the one with the eight-headed sigil of House Nebiros—spun across the red book's cover like a silver spur catching lamplight. Then it opened wide and swallowed the whole tome. One second it was there, and the next, it was gone. Dropped clean into nowhere.

Sona didn't even notice me cast the spell, too busy shooting real angry looks at Neferpitou's knight piece that forked her rook and bishop.

More pockets were always welcome on the trail, especially the spatial kind. This one wasn't anything real fancy, just a rough little bubble of fortified realspace staked out in the Dimensional Gap, like a barrel full of treasure bobbing up and down a lake. It cost a mean chunk of Demonic Power to cast, even to hold something as small as that book, but it meant having a secure place to stash my gear and that sort of usefulness was worth every drop.

Shame I couldn't make the most of it.

Just to double check... I fished the Infernal Tome out of my Dimensional Pocket again, checked it over, tossed it right back in, and frowned at just how much Demonic Power that drank up. It'd take years to build up the reserves to do that casually. Longer still before I could toss anything living in there, especially something that can struggle. Couldn't imagine how long it'd take to build a large enough reserve to build a whole fighting arena, the kind they used for Rating Games!

Lucky that I didn't mind cheating.

A Golden Ticket sat pretty at the back of my mind, ready to hand over a hearty expansion to my maximum reserves and also a big prize.

My eyes went to Sona who was shooting real angry looks at Neferpitou's knight piece. It'd be mighty risky to roll right now, but gosh darnit if this Gold Ticket didn't make me feel impatient! These Gold Tickets were bringing out the worst out of me!

Agh, to hell with it!

I'll risk a familiar appearing out of nowhere! And if it had to be a familiar... Satan, please let it finally be a horse!

I burned the ticket.


[Verdance] |Elite Ability|
You have formidable chlorokinetic abilities. Allowing you to create, communicate with and control plants in your sensory range. You are able to create plants out of the soil and boost any existing plant to supernatural levels, making wood harder than steel, bring down leaves sharper than razors, impale people with roots that erupt from the ground, or simply grow a berry bush for a quick snack. But you need a plant in your range or grow one to use your abilities on them.

...
​

...

My [Byakugan] was on. My 'sensory range' covered a massive chunk of the whole town. And within this space...

Is... is this how them gods feel? To hold the whole damn world in one hand?

I was like someone born with no arms suddenly waking with a million green hands, every one of them hungry for use. Every leaf reaching up for the warm sun, every blade of grass shivering like whiskers in the wind, and every root pushing through soil in the pitch black of the underground for water and nutrients. All this time, I'd been looking at the world through [Byakugan], nothing had been hidden, but everything was out of reach. Not anymore.

I reached out with [Verdance].

Across town, in the parlor of a traditional Japanese house, a bonsai tree snapped free of the solid steel restraints. The old man beside it froze with his teacup halfway to his mouth, eyes round as silver dollars. Out by my cabin in the nature reserve, a beetle was chewing through a maple tree's leaf. The leaf curled shut around it, there was a wet crunch, and the little pest became paste.

And on the park path, just a few dozen yards behind me—

"Ow!" Sona's blue haired rook piece rubbed her head, glaring at the ripe apple that had fallen on it. Her partner, a brunette with the twintails and striped leggings, just giggled beside her and picked up the fruit. And through it... the warmth of her hand and the softness of her fingers.

I felt it all.

The Green was mine to command as if it was my own hand. But it was so much more than that.

Through [Nature's Child], I drew strength quicker the closer I stood to nature. And I wasn't just 'close' to the nature of Kuoh Town, I was one with it. All of it. All at once.

That hefty chunk of Demonic Power that I spent playing with the Dimensional Pocket, I regained in a blink. Even now, my power swelled and overflowed. And so long as I stayed in wooded country like this, my Demonic Power was functionally infinite, like a lake in a hurricane filling up faster than I could drain it.

I could cast spells all day, I could fight all day... And with [Stim], as long as something didn't kill me in one shot, I'd regenerate like one of them Phenex! I could even make spell totems all day long... Heck, I could make as many Ichabods as I want without running out of juice anymore! I could... I could...

The potential never ends.

I hadn't had a hand this good since I got [Intermediate Item Creation] and [Bronze Creation]! If that was a Pair, then [Nature's Child] and [Verdance] and [Byakugan] were Three-of-a-Kind!

Every plant was now my power source and weapon.

Every tree lining the roadside, every flower crammed into some window planter, every potted fern sulking in the corner of a living room, every mushroom growing in the damp dark corners of—

My mind skidded to a dead halt, and I frowned fierce in my chair.

Wait a cotton-picking minute... Fungi weren't plants! If my [King of All Trades] had left me with even one scrap of learning worth keeping, it was that mushrooms, yeast, mold, and the rest of 'em belonged to a whole separate kingdom from plants and animals! And by every modern biology textbook, my plant magic shouldn't have been able to touch them at all! Unless, of course... Magic didn't give one solitary damn about modern ningen taxonomy.

And sitting there, turning the thought over in my head, I realized... That was the kind of attitude that I could get behind!

To heck with them ningen classifications of the natural world!

A devil-cowboy like me might not be much of a spell researcher, but Satan as my witness, me and my [King of All Trades] were gonna invent and cast whatever magic I'll damn well please! And no taxonomist was gonna keep me from it!

I was gonna research the heck out of these spells! And there sure were a lot of 'em spells, just waiting to be made true!

Just off the top of my head, I could think of a few that would revolutionise Nebiros Valley! Spells like Banish Tumbleweed or Lesser Marijuana Field or Summon Dire Beer. Shoot, my folks were gonna be mighty proud of me with just those alone! Just from how much devil-drugs I can grow on a dime, but the real 'holy grail' was the spell that every kin of our proud could only dream of: Conjure Ranch Dressing.

If I can crack the mystery of that spell, if I could inscribe it in an Infernal Tome like the Sona gave me, everyone in Nebiros Town would crown me Satan faster than I could say 'Unlimited Ranch Refills!'

They'll make a monument in my honour! Malal Nebiros, the greatest farmhand amongst the Underworld— No, the greatest farmhand who ever lived!

I sat back, resting my hat over my eyes with a chuckle.

And in a cabin in the woods under the glowing eyes of bronze golem, branches picked up chalk, began writing on a chalkboard... chasing after magical secrets that no other devil had ever pursued before.

(A while later)

"Refreshments, Sona-kaichou."

Tea arrived precisely when Sona was losing her fifth game.

"Thank you, Tsubaki," She murmured, still staring at the increasingly dire state of the chessboard.

Her queen piece—the one that hadn't been sacrificed to stop this damned chimera's advance through the right flank of the chessboard—set down a second table for tea service, draped with restaurant-quality white tablecloth. And over it, Tsubaki's long black hair swayed like a curtain of black silk as she served them tea from Renée's. Setting down a white teapot painted with Sitri-blue irises, a bowl of sugar crystals, a small glass pot of amber honey, and a tiered plate of honey cakes delicately dusted white with powdered sugar.

Sona reached... for her rook, and the corners of the catgirl's mouth twitched.

"Your king is still in check, Lady Sitri," Neferpitou 'helpfully' reminded.

Sona's fingers remained on the rook. "I am aware of that," she said, staring at the board without truly seeing it. "I'm merely reevaluating my position. Don't rush me."

"Your rook has no legal moves."

"I said I know," Sona replied, annoyed as a steaming porcelain cup was set down beside her pile of fallen pieces.

Another cup was set down beside her opponent's infuriatingly smaller pile. But as Tsubaki navigated around the table, Sona caught sight of a... tall glass of iced tea with lemon? Her brows furrowed. What kind of uncultured, country bumpkin would want iced tea—

A cold pit dropped in Sona's stomach.

And with a wince, she moved her violet eyes, almost unwillingly, from the tea tray to the corner of the park gazebo that she had been consistently overlooking all this time.

Malal Nebiros sat leaned back in his chair, arms folded across the black of his suit. His black cowboy hat had tipped forward over half his face, hiding his eyes under it. He looked, for one terrifying instant, as though he had fallen asleep. Or worse, as though he had been awake for some time and had simply decided that there was nothing here worth interrupting.

Ooo, she was going to be in trouble for this.

She had been sent here to accommodate him. Make House Nebiros feel received, valued, and kept within reach until her sister could finish the true work. The rare Infernal Tome had simply been a 'reset button' as they say, actually making diplomatic progress beyond that had been up to her. And what did she do? She'd made some polite noises then... promptly forgot all about the heir from a Lucifer Six House to go play chess with his bodyguard. For four whole games (All with four losses, which was humiliating but not currently the point), she had completely ignored him.

With a wince, Sona tipped her king over with two fingers.

The white-haired, faux nekoshou nodded slightly, accepting her fifth victory. "Mr~rm. Another match?"

"Not right now," Sona said.

She... she can fix this. There was still time to fix it. Unlike with Rias' catastrophic blunder, this wasn't permanent. Just... a misstep.

He was willing, or rather forced, to cooperate: Staying put while he was investigated by whoever her Serafall-onee-sama had assigned to his case. But that won't last, and they had until after his Rating Game to convince him otherwise. The main body of the convincing would, of course, be up to her sister and what concessions a Satan can give to Malal and his House, but the groundwork for polite discourse started here.

Fixing her violet glasses, Sona's eyes rested on the tall glass of lemon iced tea.

Yes! This is where she could start: with making Lucifer Six feel at ease in Kuoh Town. Because it was always hard to imagine a reassuring future of cooperation when the immediate present was already fraught with uncertainty... and neglectful hosts.

"Lord Nebiros," Sona said, her voice far calmer than she felt. "Forgive the delay, but tea has arrived."
