"Much obliged!" Malal sat up immediately, either awake or quick to react as he fixed his hat with a cheerful smile. He accepted the tall glass, and with a long sip, he hummed in approval beneath the brim of his black hat.

Sona allowed herself exactly one breath, relieved that he wasn't visibly angry at her diplomatic faux pax.

"Sona-kaichou," Tsubaki began quietly, leaning close to her shoulder. "While retrieving the refreshments, I received a call from—"

"Later," Sona whispered back.

The taller girl hesitated momentarily, then bowed and withdrew to the tea service.

She already knew who has been incessantly trying to contact her, and would also be catastrophic if mentioned in front of present company. Hospitality first, her panicking friend later. Rias' calls, unless it was about her peerage about to charge through the hedges, could wait until after the meeting.

So, with a soft smile, Sona turned back to Malal.

"I apologise for the conspicuousness of our security measures. Kuoh Town sits at a rather busy crossroad. Tokyo, the capital of this human country, is merely a stone's throw to the northeast, and our town holds the largest station that pierces the veil between the Underworld and the human world. Commuting devils, pact magicians, and diplomatic guests, all eventually pass through Kuoh Town for one reason or another. Needless to say, we are by necessity quite proactive with dealing with any stray devil that dares trespass here."

Secure and prosperous. Two things that any noble would gladly boast their domain to be.

... And Sona had to pretend that Kuoh Town was hers to boast about. In reality? This domain was Rias' by grant and bloodright, administered under the banner of House Gremory. Sona merely held the reins in Rias' absence. Temporarily, of course, while her friend had since been recalled to her family's demesne for discipline. It just seemed unwise to remind her guest that the lawful mistress of the town had recently attempted to have him decapitated.

That would be the last thing she needed right now.

"Proactive? Is that what you call it?" Malal Nebiros chuckled, eyes shifting under the shadow of his hat to share a bemused look with his chimera.

Sona was very aware that they knew something she didn't, but she couldn't do anything but continue to smile pleasantly. "Indeed, while you remain here, that watch includes you. Any hand raised against a guest under my jurisdiction will be treated as a hand raised against the order of this territory."

"And I appreciate the thought, Miss Sitri," He nodded. "Just seems funny saying that with all these stray devils on the loose."

"You've... encountered Stray Devils?" was her surprised reply.

"Encountered ain't the word for it... I'd say 'discovered' suits it better." He shrugged, "Closest we got to an 'encounter' with one of 'em was when it skittered through my neck of the woods. He was smart enough to keep his distance, that one was. And I was otherwise preoccupied building my cabin back then, so picking a fight was the last thing on my list. After that, no one else wandered that close, so we've just been watching 'em mutate all around town. There's one that had six legs just the other night. It's down to four now, but it learned manholes."

Sona could feel the conversation back-firing in real time.

Not because of the spike in stray devils in Kuoh Town. No, with Rias and her peerage currently absent, no one was able to perform the usual sweep of Stray Devil eliminations. So, an increase in Stray Devils in the area was to be expected, but not enough for them to be this noticeable! Finding them was difficult enough as it was, accidentally running into them was an astronomical chance. So, either there had been a mass defection of devils in the Underworld that she hadn't heard of or...

"... you're able to locate them?" Sona whispered to him.

"Sure do," He said with an easygoing smile and a wink. "You can say that I got some of the sharpest pairs of eyes in House Nebiros!"

"I... see," Sona's eyes drifted, despite herself, to the other side of the chess table.

The white-haired nekoshou-chimera sat quietly beside the board, wearing that same airheaded look of low intelligence, as if she had not beaten Sona at chess fiv- four times in a row.

House Nebiros had shown them one chimera, brazenly placed it right in front of them like they were brandishing a sword. And despite having told it straight to their faces that there were more hidden away, they had made the mistake of assuming that this Neferpitou was the only one deployed here in the human world.

How many, then?

Just how many spies did House Nebiros already have in Kuoh Town, if their heir could monitor stray devil movements this closely? Were the others stationed throughout the territory as powerful as this one? As intelligent? As good at chess? Serafall had said the chimera she encountered possessed stealth capabilities refined enough to elude even a Satan's senses for a time, so was the creature sitting across from Sona might not even be that same chimera? Would they have any reliable way to tell them apart?

Her sister was not going to like how couldn't answer any of those questions.

Sona returned her gaze to Malal with great care. "I... we would appreciate it," she said, keeping her voice even, "if you would forward such information to us, Nebiros-dono."

"I would," Malal said. His smile tilted beneath the shadow of his hat. "Provided you let me join in on the fun."

Sona went still. "So, you wish for hunting rights in this domain..."

The question sounded calmer than it felt. Old law had sharp teeth, and Malal Nebiros had just brushed his fingers over one of them.

Kuoh Town belonged to Lord Gremory. Its roads, its resources, its humans, and its stray devils. And by his license, Sona held the steward's seal here—the youngest in six hundred years entrusted with a realm of this size in the human world, but that wasn't important right now—every stray devil slain within these borders was supposed to be slain under the Gremory owner's direct authority or hers. A right guaranteed to them by the Satans. Perhaps the Lord Bael could make that trespass, but no one else from the Seventy-Two. However...

She sipped her tea, looked past the porcelain rim to the devil-in-black smiling across from her.

... Malal was not of the Seventy-Two. He was of the Six Houses of Lucifer.

In the older books, a Lucifer Six heir would not have needed to ask before destroying any stray devil they found. 'Deserters' was what they had been called rather than stray devils, since these laws predate even the modern peerage system by several centuries. But even in those times, having deserters of another House find refuge in your realm was... problematic to say the least. Lord Gremory or whoever he left in charge of the realm—namely a Rias Gremory and a Sona Sitri—would have been the one made to answer to Malal.

After all, the Six Houses of Lucifer were often called upon to discipline 'disobedient' Houses. With House Lucifuge's punishments the most well known for being the most... spectacular.

That was the trouble with dead laws. No one bothered to prune them until someone who was supposed to be extinct strolled in through the gates with his shiny, oversized belt buckle of authority.

Sona folded her hands neatly in her lap. "Are you requesting my authorization as Steward of Kuoh Town, or are you asserting a privilege of House Nebiros?"

The distinction was thin, and mostly decided if she was going to need an Underworld lawyer for this.

"Which of 'em means that we can go do it our damn selves?" The devil-in-black said in a half-laugh. Then he jabbed a thumb to point past his shoulder, pointing beyond the manicured colours of the botanical park, and toward the rougher, greener and hillier rise of Kuoh Town's rural edge. "There's four of 'em tucked up that way, just round the bend. Two of 'em are looking extra ugly, and I reckon the last two aren't far off themselves. We can mosey on over, collect their scalps, and make it back before the first fly finds these honey cakes. All this sitting around is making me itch."

The first one then.

Recognising her authority and allowing himself to act within it... she was not expecting that, but she did appreciate the gesture.

"Thank you for your consideration," Sona said, stalling with a sip of her teacup.

Just a little hunt between heirs then. A more violent and bloody social affair, but a social affair nonetheless. She had only gone to... two her entire life? And those were for hunting wildlife, not Stray Devils. This would certainly be more dangerous, though not recklessly so. In fact, it would be insulting to suggest that a 72 Pillar heiress, a Lucifer Six heir, a peerage, and a war-chimera combined wouldn't be able to handle a Stray Devil.

"However..." She began with an appropriately regretful sigh, "I cannot knowingly place a guest under my protection in danger. Regardless of how unlikely that danger may be."

Malal chuckled as if she had just told a joke, "And if those stray devils just suddenly turn up dead?"

Sona's fingers tightened once against the handle of her teacup. He's reminding her that he had an army inside Kuoh, and leaving it to her if she wants a demonstration of what they could do right underneath their noses. This chimera army could accomplish a lot—more than Rias and her peerage could—of that there was no doubt. She had already admitted that they couldn't find the Stray Devils in their territory, so how much more with the army of nigh-undetectable chimeras that could hunt them at a snap of his fingers?

"I do not doubt they would, Nebiros-dono..." Sona said softly. Her eyes swept across the swaying treeline, where the shadowed center of the tree, and she wondered how many nekoshou-chimeras were watching from the branches at this very moment.

Then it struck her. Like a chess strategy she had never witnessed before until now.

... Why was she against this?

Wasn't this precisely what her Serafall-onee-sama wanted?

In any other scenario, a Lucifer Six heir revealing hidden claws inside her borders could hardly be mistaken for anything other than a threat. Yet... Malal hadn't revealed them to threaten her. No, he had not only given up the advantage of surprise that would otherwise have been fatal for them, but he offered the use of his House's creations in service to the Underworld's safety, working with her authority rather than in defiance of it.

That was what the Four Satans wanted, wasn't it?

To have the Lucifer Six house invested in the interests of the Underworld? Their interests intertwining closer and closer until they find themselves reintegrated? To have House Nebiros' chimeras acting within their authority rather than challenging it?

Could it be just this easy? Dare she believe it was?

It could still be a trap... Or it could simply be House Nebiros being what they have always been known for: obtuse, inscrutable, unnervingly horrifying in their methods, and yet still unquestionably aligned with devilkind. It all depended on whether the devil in front of her could be trusted—

Yes, Sona Sitri trusted her elder sister's judgment. In matters this serious, at least.

If Serafall, for whatever reason, believed House Nebiros could be brought back into the fold through Malal, then Sona would not be the one to flinch when the first real opportunity presented itself.

"Very well, Mal," she said.

He sat up, a wide grin under the shadow of his hat. "So I'm deputized? With the right to arrest?!"

"Yes," Sona smiled along. "You may eliminate stray devils found in the territory of Kuoh Town, and my office will confirm and record your success. Though, that is not the same as a general right to administer justice in this territory, nor to the right to the humans. This is as much authority as I can formally grant you as Steward of this territory, and I suppose that we will be working much more closely from now on."

"That sounds like a fancy yes!" Malal grinned wide, scooting his chair closer. "So, do we shake on it or something? Or is our word good enou— Ah, I guess it is."

"Yes, however, I'd prefer for us to formalize it by putting the agreement to ink," She turned her head slightly, violet glasses catching a pale flash of garden light. "Tsubaki."

The taller girl was ready with the writing instruments. Vellum, House Sitri's blue sealing wax, and Sona's favoured blue pearl fountain pen, arranging them beside the tea service as neatly as if they were simply another course. The absurd normalcy of it steadied her. Sona had expected that she would be expected to produce something along the lines of a formal, written apology. But never had she hoped that it would be this productive instead.

Sona looked to the blank vellum laid out in front of her... and carefully took up the fountain pen, her pale fingers wrapped around the swirling blue and white pattern on the pen's glossy surface.

For once, the board seemed to be moving in her favor.

She steadied her breath, then pressed the golden pen nib onto fine calfskin vellum. The words formed with graceful curves of ink. Sona knew them by heart, so similar were they to the license that Lord Gremory had granted to her. And she'd spent long enough staring contemplatively at that precious framed declaration of her status to recite it.

She also insisted on the formality of getting it in writing then.

A single line in and the gravity of what she had done and what she was doing struck her. House Sitri... aligned with the first real Lucifer Six house. Her parents and especially her sister were going to be over the moon. A tiny part of her whispered that this could pay immense dividends for herself down the line too. This... this could even be the political leverage she needed to start her own Rating Game Academy!

Tremors of excitement threatened to ruin her penmanship, but Sona kept her hand steady.

"Kaichou…" Reya, her bishop piece who was supposed to be on watch, murmured near her shoulder.

Sona did not look up, eyes focused on gliding the polished nib of her pen over the vellum in precise, controlled strokes as she whispered. "Later."

"But—"

"Not now," she said, barely above a breath.

Across from her, a chair creaked, and Malal took a long sip of iced tea, his black cowboy hat tipped up just enough to watch her write. "You oughta hear her out," he said, his eyes glanced up from the parchment and to her. "Reckon it's about that train that rolled into town a few minutes ago. Same one Miss Gremory's peerage rode in on."

The fountain pen froze, and Sona did as well. Her eyes peering incredulously over her glasses' violet rims. "What?"

"They're headed this way now!" Malal's mouth curved with open amusement, and then he actually laughed. "And they don't look none too happy."

"It's as he says, Sona-kaichou," Tsubaki said quickly, the taller girl sounding apologetic, "I'm sorry. This was what I tried to inform you. Rias called earlier and she was acting strangely, asking where you were. I told her you were not to be disturbed, but she intuited this location, and—"

"We must act fast," Sona stood up, her chair sliding back with a sharp scrape against the wood. She offered an apologetic look to her guest. "I'm terribly sorry for the interruption, Nebiros-dono. If you will excuse me for one moment, I must prevent—"

"Too late," Malal scoffed, still smiling as he gestured with his glass of iced tea past her. "That's them right there."

Tense, as if expecting a punch, Sona turned around, almost praying for everyone's eyes to be lying to them. But there, at the far end of the park, a pair of figures advanced down the sunbaked concrete path between the beds of tulips. Yuuto Kiba and Akeno Himejima... Both proudly wearing the Kuoh Academy uniform as well as proudly wearing the firm expressions of people who were absolutely determined to ruin a Sitri heiress' day today.

Rias—or at least her peerage—had once again appeared to snatch defeat from the jaws of victory.

Sona clenched her teeth tight to keep herself from cursing. Because of course they had arrived precisely now, just as the meeting had begun to resemble success.

"Mal," she said, already stepping away from the table, "please stay put."

"Sure thing!" He said before tossing something small to his familiar, "Hey, Pitou, this is yours."

"I am not worthy... but I will treasure it forever, my King."

Sona marched out the gazebo and under the noon sun to meet the duo, greeting them with her coldest stare. "Akeno, Yuuto. What in Satan's name are you doing here?!" she hissed in a whisper. "Serafall told you, all of you, not to interfere!"

"We apologise for the interruption!"

The Queen and Knight pieces chorused with their synchronized bow, Akeno's long dark ponytail even brushing on the concrete.

Sona massaged the bridge of her nose. At least... they hadn't forgotten all their manners, but they hadn't remembered enough of it to not interrupt her diplomatic meeting in the first place!

"Sona-sama, we are not here to fight," Akeno politely emphasized as she straightened, chin lifted and violet eyes looking into hers. "We are here only to ensure that Rias-buchou's correspondence reaches Nebiros-sama without tampering."

"A... letter?" Sona parroted dully, staring at the bustier girl wondering if the nutrients had actually rushed from her brain. "You interrupted this diplomatic meeting... for a letter. Couldn't have waited until after it concluded?"

Akeno's expression did not change, but the blond knight coughed beside her. "Buchou... " He began. "... she believed the matter was urgent enough."

"Rias is grounded."

"But remains the heiress of House Gremory and Lady of Kuoh Town," Akeno replied, technically correct with caveats studding her statement. "And she wished for you to know, before you made any... agreements, that Nebiros-sama threatened her by letter."

"And I sure did!"

Sona closed her eyes—Of course he had heard—then glanced back.

Malal remained exactly where she had told him to stay. He sat tipped back in his chair, iced tea in hand. "I threatened to take one of her peerage members..." he raised his glass in salute, "... by winning our Rating Game!"

Akeno just nodded, letting him speak for himself.

Sona reached up to pinch the bridge of her nose. "Akeno-san... That is the publicly declared wager of the match." she said, opening her eyes again with immense effort. "Everyone in the Underworld knows that!"

"It was phrased aggressively enough to concern her," Akeno reached into her pochette and produced a sealed envelope between two fingers. "And Buchou has replied in kind."

Sona looked at the seal, then at Akeno. Then—

"Who is that yokai standing beside him?" Yuuto spoke up, his blue eyes glancing past her and toward the gazebo. The faint, curious 'Mr~rm?' was far more worrying to her than it was to the blond knight.

That's right... Neither Rias nor her peerage has yet been informed of the nekoshou-chimera. She would need to have a discussion with Serafall about that. But in this immediate situation...

"That is none of your concern, Yuuto-san," She said with a cold stare.

"But—"

"Drop. It."

Rias' knight piece pressed his lips together, tearing his eyes away from the chimera.

At that moment, Sona felt pride for Rias. She was proud of her friend... for not being catastrophically stupid enough to send her nekoshou rook piece along with them, otherwise... Well, her reaction to just House Nebiros' sigil started this mess. She could not imagine how Koneko would react being face to face with what they strongly suspected was her mutated, mass-produced clone. If only she could have also felt proud that Rias allowed her diplomatic meeting with the Nebiros heir to go uninterrupted as well!

"Akeno," Sona turned her attention to Rias' Queen piece. "Give me the letter. I'll hand it to him myself."

"With all due respect, Sona-sama, Buchou ordered that it be delivered directly to Milord Nebiros."

Sona countered with her own politeness. "With respect," she began, "Rias is not authorized to interrupt an active diplomatic meeting with a Lucifer Six House heir. He can receive her letter from my hands now, or he can receive it from your hands later. You may choose what's more important, but you are not taking one step closer to that gazebo."

Akeno hesitated, her violet eyes glancing around. And Sona knew that Rias' Queen piece was looking at her peerage who had all gathered to enforce their King's will. With a soft exhale, she bowed, extending the letter to her. Rias's seal in bright red wax resting on the vellum, pressed hard enough that the crest had nearly cracked through the paper.

"I entrust it to you, Sona-sama."

Sona accepted it without a word. Instead, she turned, walked back under the gazebo's shade, and placed the envelope in Malal's waiting hand. "There," She turned to Akeno. "Delivered without tampering."

"Much obliged." Malal accepted the letter between two fingers. And for one narrow, hopeful instant, Sona thought he might simply tuck it away and allow the matter to die with what little dignity remained of her diplomatic meeting...

And then the envelope spontaneously burst into flames.

Rias's red seal blackened. The paper curled inward, bright orange licking over the folded parchment until whatever angry words Rias had committed to ink became ash between Malal's fingers.

"I'm sure it had some real nice words from Miss Gremory," Malal stood up, boots stepping forward casually on wood. "Unfortunately, I don't listen to sore losers and their ramblin'."

Sona's smile survived by training alone. Akeno flinched, and Yuuto snapped.

"You bastard!"

Malal only smiled at the reaction. "But where, oh where, are my manners?" The devil-in-black chuckled as he patted the last of the ash off his sleeve. His thumbs hooked on either side of his shiny, oversized belt buckle as if to draw attention to Nebiros sigil engraved on it.

His dark cowboy hat tilted forward as he nodded.

"Howdy, I'm Malal Nebiros." he said with a grin, "Great Grandson to Zaorama Nebiros. But you can start callin' me your King."

Both Rias' Queen and Knight piece startled, wide-eyed, at the sheer arrogance of the statement.

"One of you lucky sons of a gun is gonna end up callin' me that anyway. And I reckon you'd all best start getting comfortable saying it now..." He grinned down at the two of them, "'specially since I already have my eye on who I want."

Kiba went very still. And Akeno wore a genuinely worried expression on her face as well. And for a moment, no one in the park moved. It was then that Sona understood, with perfect and helpless clarity, that there wasn't going to be any peace in Kuoh Town any time soon. In fact, her quiet little human town had just become a town on the edge of their own mini-devil civil war.

But in that second, Sona herself had to wonder: Malal Nebiros had already decided who he wanted to take, but...

Her eyes glanced at the blonde knight piece and the Queen piece.

... which one was it?

[Kuoh Academy - Occult Research Clubhouse]

Koneko sat against the door, the shoving stick resting across her lap.

"So, you... feel it?" the girly voice said, muffled by the thick door where seals engraved into the dark wood. "That's so cool... They say only the most sensitive internet psychics with the most followers can sense it."

"Mm," Koneko gripped the stick tighter. "It's... lingering over the whole school... reaching all the way here."

"Uweh, that sounds pretty scary."

She frowned, pressing her cat ears to the top of her head. "I only notice it if I focus on it.."

"If you focus, huh?" The plasticy crinkle of a konbeni rice bowl being unwrapped came through the door. "Some psychics say that when they focus enough, they see the shape of a bug, but others see a cat. What do you think, Koneko-chan?"

"Mm..." she tried, just a little to focus on the evil-feeling energy, and she shivered, "I don't know, but... I think it's both... Mmm, Gasper?"

"Hmm?"

"Do you... know where it's coming from?"

"They say it's been coming from the woods in the hills." Gasper said through a mouthful of rice.

"... Then we shouldn't go there. Ever."

"Don't worry, Koneko-chan, it'll probably go away soon," Gasper said back, a smile in his voice as he spoke through the door, "We're devils now too. It can't be too scary, right?"


​

- Esoteric Cowboy Magicks End -
