#4,415
Chapter 8: Big Bronze and Cat Complications

In which... Butterflies prevents someone's tragedy from happening. Mal becomes Sheriff Cuddle Cowboy of Kuoh Town. And a few poor souls come to the wrong neighborhood.

[Outskirts of Kuoh Town]

With a smile, I seated a pair of spell totems into my double-barrelled shotgun.

One totem to tell 'em that I mean business, and the other for anyone with poor hearing. The inscribed containers of magical reckoning whispered across the mirror-smooth bronze, like the sound of silver dollars rubbing against each other, whispering their excitement for what was gonna happen.

"It's a darn good night for a shootout!" I laughed, angling my dark cowboy hat to look at the place better.

The moonlight shone down on tonight's killhouse: One of those Japanese hillside homes where the top floor sat road-level, and the rest of the house was built stepping down the hill slope. 'Course, this one was abandoned like one of those horror movies. A house of rotting wood and deep shadows, half-hidden behind the overgrown brush. Cracked stone steps dotted with lichen, flanked by broken potted plants spilling their rooty, soily guts onto the path. And the house's pointed thatch roof loomed above like a raised axe.

The front door itself was nowhere to be seen, leaving just an open void leading inside, and...

... It felt like the house was breathing.

That was the only way to call it. The air rolled out the open doorway. All warm, and wet and sour... like the breath of something big lying in the dark with its mouth open. Musty with old rainwater, and the faint iron smell of nails gone bad in soaked wood. Even ningens could tell that this place had been left abandoned and left to rot until the world remembered to clean up after itself...

"Perfect place for a couple of moving targets to settle in!"

With a twist of my wrist, my double-barrel snapped straight and shut, the moon glowing from deep within the gun's muzzles just as much as it did in the sky overhead.

I stepped over the threshold—into the house's shadow—licking my lips as the shotgun warmed in my hands. Its twin barrels growing hot with all the demonic power I pumped into its creation... just like any devil with Lucifer blood did at the promise of violence. Just like mine.

"Malal!" Sona Sitri stepped up behind me, violet eyes looking at the house suspiciously.

"Hmm?" I stopped, already a few steps inside the house's dark, and turned back toward the girls still gathered outside, all seven of them framed by the doorway. Their pretty faces had gone tight with worry... which was the best place I could focus on, because I was more aware that I could see through their Kuoh Academy uniforms when I'm looking at them head on like this. "Something a matter?"

"Yes," Serafall (the short-haired, glasses, b-cup edition) said, her violet eyes flicking at the darkness behind me, "Please don't rush ahead like this. We have yet to devise the optimal strategy. Are you certain there's only four stray devils?"

"As sure as if I went in there and eyeballed them myself!" I smiled, just glad that she hadn't asked how I knew these things. My [Byakugan]'s telescopic, 360-degree x-ray vision was too big and too valuable of an ace up my sleeve to just announce to people. But... I don't mind flaunting that I just 'knew' things. Like where all the targets were. I pointed deeper into the house, my black sleeve extended out and blending with the shadows, "One of 'em is in the dining room just down the hall, and other three scattered on the bottom floor. There, there and there."

The short-haired girl pushed her violet glasses up, "And their mutations?"

"Two of 'em are pretty far along, one's just started breaking out of his skin, and the last hasn't."

"That's..." Sona paused, her mouth tightening as if stopping herself from saying, "...That's far too many to handle cleanly. Rias-san and her peerage normally handled these duties. According to her reports, they seldom engaged more than two at once. Attempting to secure four stray devils in a single operation is... cause for concern."

A few of the girls traded looks. The redhead thumbed the handguard of her katana. Sona's queen piece, Tsubaki, glanced left and right as if expecting the dark woods to reach for them any second. Only the one with the shoulder-length teal blue hair, Tsubasa, looked ready to step up and spit in a stray devil's eye.

I liked her already.

Her dark blue eyes met mine as she spoke up. "This is our first Stray Devil Elimination, Nebiros-dono."

"Tsubasa..." Sona sighed.

"So?" I leaned on the doorway as if I owned the place. The bronze shotgun resting easy in the crook of my arm, "All you gotta do is stick close to me. First mission, four strays down. Hell of a start, if you ask me."

Sona turned that careful, proper look on me. "That confidence is exactly why we should proceed carefully, Malal."

"Careful?" I parroted. And despite my 360-degree, telescopic vision, I made a show of looking past her, down the path swallowed by trees, then back into the warm-musty black throat of the house. "Careful of what?"

"An ambush for one."

"There ain't an ambush out here," I said, before gesturing deeper into the house with my shotgun muzzle. "The first critter's that way."

Far behind me, in the dark, the malformed stray with its magic circle caused a table to telekinetically move across the room. The long scrape of wood across wood echoing out all the way out the door.

A couple of the girls startled a little at the sound.

"See?" I smiled at them. "Friendly. Already saying hello. So, let's head inside and say hello right back!"

That did not settle their nerves any.

Sona glanced back behind her, to the seven girls in that Kuoh uniform, "We need to hold here and form a proper plan."

There were nods all around, and my fingers tapping impatiently on the smooth bronze of my magic shotgun, "You know... you and your peerage can stay here and strategize yourselves a plan if you want. I can just head on in by myself."

Sona did a double-take, her whole gaggle of six girls did in fact. A mildly insulted one too. All of 'em wearing frowns made of pinched pride as they narrowed their pretty eyes at the cowboy who implied they weren't brave enough or tough enough for this.

Said cowboy just grinned back at them.

"We're coming with you," Sona replied with a frigid look.

"That's the spirit!" I said, already turning around to go deeper into the house, a grin on my face, and my hands gripping excited on the gun that I was about to use on a real, live target. "Besides, they're strays. Ain't no match for the Underworld's finest!"

I strolled nice and slow, making it easy for them to catch up. And behind me, the floorboards creaked as the girls followed, seven pairs of girls' school shoes crossing the threshold from dirt to rotting wood.

"Tomoe, Tsubasa, both of you are the rearguard," Sona said as she followed after me, "Ruruko, cover Reya. Momo and Tsubaki, on my target."

"Yes, Sona-kaichou," they said in a chorus, followed by a quiet shuffling as they switched formation.

The dark folded quick around their little formation. Sona, Tsubaki, and Momo just behind me. And the katana girl supporting the lone brave girl in the group. Like a bravery sandwich. There were still a bit of nerves, but they kept it together alright.

The humid hall ran narrow ahead, paneled in wood with lingering scraps of wallpaper where the damp hadn't rotted it off. And mushrooms grew from the corners where the floor met the walls. The ceiling beams were bruised black with water and rot. I could feel every soft place in the house through the moss, the roots, the mushrooms nosing through the corners.

I chuckled at it all.

Bad place for them to stake their hideout in the middle of my turf.

"Oh, careful on the left," I said, stepping over a stagnant, black puddle that had gathered where the floor sagged some like a shallow bowl. "The floorboards there are drinking water. They'll crack underfoot, and trust me, ladies, you don't want to meet whatever's been nesting in that little space between floors."

Behind me, the girls slowed their steps.

With [Verdance], every tree, every piece of moss and lichen was a weapon I could use. Roots to turn into spears, vines into living ropes, leaves into blades. All of it was mine to command. And with [Byakugan], I had my eyes on every sorry stray inside. They had nowhere to hide. Boy did it feel good having a whole damn deck of aces up my sleeve! Could only imagine what I'll be able to do once I hit ten abilities and get my third Ability slot... and that was only one ability away now. And I bet I'll get a ticket for hunting and killing my first stray!

A door on our right stood just slightly ajar, showing nothing but a sliver of black beyond. As we passed it, an empty tin of tea inside rolled across the floor with a slow, hollow scrape, then knocked softly against the other side of the wall.

The whole line behind me tightened.

"And never mind that sound," I said, letting the shotgun rest easy in my hands. "I reckon it's trying to lure somebody in. Floor's weak in there; rotten clean through. Step in the middle, and you'll drop straight to the lower level before you've had time to scream proper. Just like it wants."

One of the girls whispered, "It?"

"Yep. This one right here."

"What?!" Their voices chased me down the hall, but I was already around the corner, tipping my hat to the thing waiting in the dining room.

"Howdy, varmint! Underworld's best come to do some damage!"

The dining room opened up in a smear of rotten furniture and mouldering countertops. And in the back of it stood the thing that had been scraping tables and rolling tins: a pale, desiccated corpse with six arms, long like dead branches, spread open like an outstretched spider. Its fingers scratching on the ceiling, the walls, and the floor at the same time. Dried blood striped its chin.

It turned its fanged, too-wide smile toward me.

"AnothEr brAve soUL Come to—"

I pulled the trigger.

White-blue light swallowed the sentence.

The Sorceries of the Raya Lucaria Academy, even when shot through a gun, didn't thunder or boom. Instead, the sparking white-blue literally cut through the musty air with the singing keen of a hundred knives being drawn. Dozens of spectral blue daggers sheared through air, meat, bone, and wood in the blink of an eye. The stray's torso vanished along with its upper legs... and most of the wall behind it too, blasted out into the night in a burst of splinters and dust. And beyond the ragged hole, Kuoh Town glittered all pretty beyond the dark treeline.

Then the wet pieces hit the floor.

Black ichor dripped from meaty stumps and into the gaps of the floorboards. Six over-stretched arms twitched their fingers. The beast's long-haired head rolled near my boots, jaw still working around the words it never got to finish.

I lowered the misting barrels—blue glitters lingering in the mist—and looked down at the ugly mug bleeding black all over the floorboards.

"Boy," I shook my head, "what were you thinking, pulling the horror-movie act on us devils? Did you think we were a buncha ningens?!"

"Mal!"

Sona and her peerage came running up behind me, magic circles burning in their hands. Tsubaki swept her spellwork across the corners. Momo dropped to one knee, palm pressed to the warped boards, blue light crawling out beneath her fingers. Behind them, Tomoe had her katana drawn and glinting like silver, eyes locked on the twitching arms like she expected them to crawl back together.

"Clear," Tsubaki said, though she didn't sound happy about it.

Sona's eyes went from to the head, to my gun and then to me. "In one spell?"

"Carian Phalanx-shot," I told them, snapping the shotgun open. The spent totems misting in their chambers. "Both barrels. Wasn't sure how strong he was, so I took no chances. Only needed one, turns out."

Sona stared past me at the hole in the wall, then at the pieces twitching across the dining room floor. "Next time," she said, very carefully, "If you have accurate intelligence of the enemy's position, kindly notify us as well."

"Will do!" I smiled.


Feat Achieved! — Self-Defense, Your Honor
Kill someone for the first time (regardless of whether you consider them real people)
+1 Silver Gacha Ticket

...
​

Didn't even get to think about burning the ticket before the real 'fun' started.

A wood-splintering crash came from far below. A full-bodied impact that shook stagnant water off the ceiling beams in putrid splashes and sent the broken wood clacking in their frames. Because somewhere beneath our feet, an old flaking door had exploded into splinters as a half-horse, half-ape stray devil forced its way through.

"Broke through the stair doors," I said, already sliding fresh spell totems into the chambers.

Sona's head snapped an unbelieving stare at me. Wide-eyed and incredulous like a gambler watching his own ace crawl out of another man's sleeve.

"Charging up the stairs," I said. The shotgun clicked straight and shut.

More crashes came, wood snapping each time, closer with each one. The wood under my boots tremoring and the black puddles in the sagging corners trembled in rings.

"Momo, Reya!" Sona finally barked, Sitri-blue magic circle flaring in one hand. "Barrier on the stair landing! Funnel it into Tomoe and Tsubasa's—"

"Rearing to jump through the floor."

"W-what?" She choked on that command, and whipped her face to me.

I lowered the shotgun until the muzzles pointed at the boards just inches from my boot.

The horse-ape thing below us gathered itself just a floor below, a hooved-fist gripping a mean flanged mace as it stared up at the ceiling. And through gaps between rotting wood, over the sights of my shotgun, our eyes met. The whole floor took one deep breath.

Then I pulled the trigger.

For one split second, the full moon lit inside the dining room.

Pure white punched downward in a gleaming white cone of energy, tearing through the floorboards and straight at that poor bastard's ugly face. The blast didn't sound like blades this time. It sounded even cleaner than that, half-real, like the sky itself had cracked a silver bell over the house and let the note fall straight through.

The floor vanished. Wind slapped dust and splinters back into our faces. And on the floor right in front of me... A gap, wide and round as a manhole, had opened at my feet, wooden edges glittering faintly with white sparkles where the spell had punched through the damp wood.

Sona and I leaned over it.

Below that was a larger hole torn through the second floor, big enough that you could drop a bed through it without hitting the edges. Below that, in the ground floor, was an even bigger hole. And right through that, smack dab at the rocky side of the hill, was the crater where the stray had been flattened like roadkill until it burst red paste.

"Was rearing to jump through the floor," I scoffed and cracked the misting shotgun open again.

Sona just looked at me quiet. Like she couldn't decide whether to thank me, or throw me through the next floor herself.

Rennala's Full Moon was the cherished prize of an unbroken line of astrologers of the Raya Lucaria Academy, the realisation of their awe before the moon's majesty. This was a spell meant to incarnate that pale, perfect orb: whole, magnificent, and every bit worthy of their eternal devotion.

So naturally, the first thing I did after learning this spell was figure out how to stuff the moon into my shotgun.

Launched properly, the spell expanded like a silver flower, blooming on impact in a clean circular radius of magical moon explosion. Launched through my magic shotgun however, Rennala's Full Moon-shot came out too fast for all that elegant, ceremonial hooey. The blast stretched forward instead, dragging the moonburst into a cone of white annihilation.

I reckon this wasn't what Queen Rennala intended...

But it suited me just fine!

"Right. That leaves two," I snapped the shotgun shut with two fresh totems loaded.

"Leaves two where?" Sona's mouth tightened at once. Behind her, her peerage looked nonplussed some. Momo and Reya had their barriers half-formed, Tomoe's hands were still raised for a counterspell she no longer had anything to counter, and Tsubasa looked like she was rapidly recalculating every assumption she had ever made about stray devil eliminations, gun etiquette, and me personally.

"Ground floor. Just before the crater. They're standing out in the open over..." I said, waving my hand in the air to my left. "... this way, facing down the hill. These are the least mutated ones."

"So, they're still sane?"

"Plenty of devils lose their minds before they even go stray, but I get what you mean... Maybe just one of 'em though? The other one's not looking so good."

"Since you're undoubtedly going to charge ahead again, please wait for the rest of us before you engage them in combat."

"Fine by me!" I grinned, with a little hop into the hole.

The fall took me past the first floor in a rush of dust and torn boards, then past the blasted second floor where the mooncone smacked into a very unlucky horse-ape bastard. Once I got to the ground floor, my wings stopped my descent, and lightly, I stepped onto the moldy living room floor, shotgun already pointed.

There were two of them. Crouched on the floor.

The first must've been just a middle school boy when it was reincarnated. Small like any fourth-grader kid. It still had a regular face and regular head with regular black hair, but... the right sight of its body was a different story. It was still wearing a torn school uniform, but it couldn't hide the spreading meat of the change. One arm had stretched out to longer than his own length, the fingers fused into three hooked talons, and patches of dark feathers had burst down its neck and shoulders. And on its back, it looked like an animal trapped in a skinsuit, trying to claw its way out of rubber skin. It was either saddest or the most disgusting thing... Or both, depending on how charitable one felt like.

The other one was a girl. Couldn't have been much older than me or Sona.

The real surprise of the day was how she hadn't started mutating at all. Not one feather or horn... she looked even more of a regular devil than my folks! Pretty thing too: Long deep green hair, a face that could win a pageant, and a blue china dress that didn't hide her curves. She was looking fierce, but exhausted too. Eyes were bloodshot.

I tipped my dark cowboy hat.

"Howdy."

The sharpened steel point of her red-tasseled spear pointed my way. "Go!" she hissed. Not to me, but to the half-mutated one. "Now. To that place we talked about, and don't look back!"

The mangled thing whimpered. "It hurts. My hand—"

"I said go! I'll hold them off!"

It hesitated for a spell... then behind me, the Sitri heiress landed a lot more gracefully than I had, because she had class. And a heartbeat later, the rest of her peerage descended, spreading out behind us in the ruined living room with their magic already up. Magic circles every colour of the rainbow.

Then it bolted.

The half-formed kid hurled itself off the balcony on all fours, trees swaying as it hopped from branch to branch, putting as much distance from this here house as fast as possible.. or at least, he would for anyone that couldn't see through walls a mile away. And trying to further block my view was the china dress girl. She stepped fully into my line of fire and leveled her spear at my us.

Sona's gaze cut past the rogue devil to the open balcony with the forest view. "Malal, we can't let the other one get away!"

"Don't worry, it's being handled."

Sona's eyes snapped to me. "Handled how?"

"You'll see in a sec," I smiled, just as I used [Verdance] to move a branch out of the way mid-jump, sending the half-formed stray down to the forest floor. "Yep, just... don't worry about him anymore."

"You'll have to get through me first!" The green-haired girl's spear swung from me to Sona, then across the girls behind her. Daring for us to try and move. She misunderstood, probably thinking that me and Sona were meaning to chase after the mutated one... not knowing there was something worse in the forest.

A breeze blew past, smelling of iron, and she appeared.

Just one moment, empty space beside me, and the next, there was someone standing in it. Thin, girlish frame in a blue coat and orange shorts. Wavy white hair framing a face that was almost sweet. And in one of her blood-soaked hands...

A familiar half-mutated boy's head, blood-drooling mouth hanging open, one eye missing from a bloody socket.

"Good girl! Catching the stray quick!" I praised, tussling her snow-white hair.

"Mr~rm," Neferpitou purred, blinking up at me slowly. "I was only doing as you commanded, my King."

"No..." came a small murmur. The stray devil girl's spear dipped, and so did the expression on her face. Wasn't terror at my cat holding her friend's head. But she did look horrified some, just not for her own life... the green-haired girl lurched one step forward, a shaking hand reaching for the head as if she'd forgotten that I was still standing there with a loaded gun.

Then she screamed.

"TATSU!"

Too raw and high and wailing to be a battle cry. Must be the name of the kid in Neferpitou's hand. The spear dipped in the hyperventilating girl's hands, but then snapped back up, trembling now. Her fingers tightened around the spear until the knuckles went pale.

My grip on my bronze shotgun tightened just a smidge too. This stray devil girl was gonna charge at us, I could feel it, could see it in her eyes. She was gonna avenge her friend, or die trying. But instead...

"The other stray devils...They were your fellow peerage members, weren't they?"

... Sona spoke up.

The stray devil's mouth twisted. Her eyes shone with hate, but the hate had too much grief under it to stand straight.

"Yes, and you killed them all."

A lovely little silence followed that.

Sona's peerage looked ashamed of that for some reason. Had no clue why though, it ain't like they're the ones who pulled the trigger! But that did get me thinking about that six-armed corpse that done got shredded by my Carian Phalanx-shot, and the horse-ape thing flattened by Rennala's Full Moon-shot just a few paces from where we were standing.

Not that I feel bad about killing them, no sir!

I wasn't some ningen with a bleeding heart; my mom raised me better than that! Nope, I just had to wonder what kind of Pieces they were. Hopefully Rook Pieces, 'cause that means my gun was working really well! Speaking of which... This stray devil girl who had yet to start growing fur or extra limbs. If she lasted this long, she's bound to have some potential, right? Maybe made of sterner stuff to test my Carian Phalanx-shot on!

Sona and I met eyes, and I 'subtly' gestured with my bronze gun.

But the short-haired sister of Serafall just shook her head, and with a deep breath, she took one step forward to the stray. A strange, determined look in her violet eyes... then her soft, lyrical voice—like a calmer, younger Serafall—echoed out the rickety house.

"When a devil is declared a Stray Devil by their King, the Peerage's Evil Piece loses the living structure meant to regulate it."

"Don't lecture me!" the stray devil girl snapped, baring her teeth.

"Not to you," Sona said, her voice firm. "To them."

The six girls behind her stiffened as their King glanced back, taking the time to meet each of their eyes.

"Even after nearly five hundred years, there is still so much we do not understand about the Peerage's Evil Piece. Unspoken rules, undiscovered quirks, and hidden mechanisms. The Satan Beelzebub, the Evil Piece's inventor, has always insisted that we uncover its truths ourselves, and in deciphering his greatest creation, we have made shamefully little progress."

She adjusted her violet glasses with a quiet sigh, then looked back to the stray devil.

"But this much is known. The Evil Pieces binds us together as King and Peerage, as power and structure, as will and restraint. Even the strength granted to a Pawn Piece can elevate a normal human beyond anything they could dream of becoming, and power that great must be held in balance by hierarchy. If that bond is ever severed improperly…"

Sona's gaze moved to the dead thing dripping blood from Neferpitou's hand, and her peerage followed it.

One of the girls swallowed hard.

"The mind is the first thing to fail," Sona continued, quieter now. "Hairline fractures form across the psyche. Higher cognition begins to unravel, and the baser instincts rise to fill the cracks: Rage, Greed, Paranoia... It all worsens rapidly from that point, because devils are creatures of will and imagination, our bodies answer the mind's desires, regardless of whether those desires are consciously made... or even sane."

The stray devil's expression flickered, but Sona did not look away.

"Soon after, Stray Devils begin to mutate... becoming whatever their broken minds have dreamed of. Whatever their nightmares have been feeding them," She paused, letting the words settle into the room. "The process... It is irreversible."

"No, that's not true! We..." The stray devil's silver eyes went to the head in Neferpitou's hand, and looked away. "... We heard there was a way out! In the human world, somewhere in the mountain peaks of Sichuan, there is a hidden master who dedicated his entire life to devil exorcism. We pieced the rumor together ourselves. The Evil Pieces CAN be removed!"

She shouted it loud enough to almost convince herself, but not Sona.

Serafall's little sister didn't change her expression. Didn't look like surprise, though something that resembled pity passed behind the violet glass of her lenses. And it was that look of almost pity that she levelled at the stray devil for a long moment, before she said...

"Your King was cruel to you, wasn't he?"

"What?" The stray devil froze.

"You were mistreated," Sona continued, as if she was retelling a sad story she'd heard a thousand times. "Neglected at first, then humiliated, then punished more often. And without ever outright stating it, your King made it clear, in a hundred little ways, that your presence in the peerage had become unwelcome. And that's why, eventually, you ran away."

The stray devil's mouth opened and closed.

"How..." The girl's voice cracked around the word. She stared at Sona with wounded disbelief. "How could you possibly know that?"

Sona did not answer at once. Her eyes moved over the girl's stance, the way she kept her weight low despite the ruin in her body, the way her fingers twitched whenever she looked toward the fallen beasts. Not claws first. Not spellwork first. Balance first. Footwork first. Someone had taught her to survive before anyone had taught her to kill.

"Before I tell you," Sona said, "what were you before you were recruited?"

The china girl squeezed her eyes tight, as if gritting through from a bruise. "I... I was a ferrygirl in Guanxi."

One of Sona's peerage girls blinked. "A ferrygirl?"

"My family carried people through the reed waterways. Farmers with their sacks of rice, couriers with their parcels, and even travelling monks who would bless my boat with protective parittas." The stray devil's mouth moved like she almost remembered how to smile, then forgot halfway through. "My father taught me to drive off wild spirit beasts with an oar. Only later did I understand that what he taught me was spear law. How to move gracefully in water without a ripple and strike through the swiftest currents. He said a river daughter who could not guard her own boat would one day lose everything aboard it."

Sona's expression softened by the smallest measure. "And then?"

"I... ran away to enter a martial arts tournament," the green-haired girl said. Her silver eyes dropped. "I was hoping to win the prize money to..."

"To?"

Her next words came out quieter than the others."To afford a good academy for my little brother. He was... too clever. Father used to joke that the heavens had misplaced a scholar in a boatman's house. He deserved better than the muddy banks of a ferryman's life, but..." the girl continued, "a spirit beast rampaged through my town while I was away training. I heard about it three months after. By the time I returned... they had already buried the entire town, and the blood in my ruined home had long dried."

Her hands clenched.

"The beast had escaped, and I knew I would not be strong enough to defeat it. At least, not with anything my father had taught me. So when a mysterious stranger with inscrutable power offered to make a pact with me, I accepted. I thought it was a hidden master looking for disciples, but..."

"It was a devil looking for peerage members," Sona finished. "Your former King."

"So how?" the china girl whispered. "How do you know what happened after?"

"Because the Evil Piece, once accepted, cannot be unaccepted," the Satan's younger sister said plainly. "A King has only fifteen Ranks within their Peerage. Once a devil accepts the Evil Piece, that place is filled, and there are very few clean ways to empty it. Trading for an 'unused' piece is the only legitimate way we know of. Death is... not always as legitimate: Embarrassing training accidents, poorly supervised hunts in dangerous territory... and Stray Devil Elimination missions they are too inexperienced to survive."

Behind her, one of the girls went pale, and Sona exhaled, fixing her glasses.

"But those accidents invite questions about the King's competence. A devil going stray, however... That is understandable. Merely, a tragic failure of character, how could anyone know? So, the unwanted Pieces are pressured: Neglected, humiliated, then punished disproportionately. Anything to drive them toward anger, then call that anger proof of their instability."

The stray devil's lips parted, but nothing came out.

"And when they are close to breaking," Sona continued, "They hear a rumor: About a wise hermit in Sichuan, or mystic in Old Anatolia, or a saint living quietly in rural Brittany. Some faraway mountain, steppe, or valley... the details change, but the promise is always the same."

Her voice lowered.

"A way to remove the Evil Piece."

"No..." the green-haired girl half-whispered and half-exhaled, close to tears. And also half-realizing where the Sitri heiress was going with this.

"Yes," Sona said, "And that is almost always the final push they need to go astray. They flee into the human world, chasing the fiction their King had baited them with. And when the mutations finally claim them, when a trail of dead humans appears and the elimination order is issued, their King reveals their 'history of rebellious and erratic behavior,' and everyone is made to understand that the Stray Devil was always the problem. Putting them down is an act of mercy. So, their now-former King mourns in public, saves face among their peers, and quietly petitions for a replacement Evil Piece with which to recruit or reincarnate someone new."

The girl in the china dress stared at Sona as if she had reached into her chest and named the shape of the wound there.

Then the truth entered the stray devil all at once.

Her spear clattered to the floor, then followed by her knees. "So we were thrown away..." she said. Her hollow voice echoed off the ruined, rotting walls, "They followed me. They trusted me. They were hungry, and afraid, and changing, and they still followed me because I... I..."

Her claws dug into her palms until fresh blood slid between her fingers.

"I told them we would find it!"

Wood cracked under fist, the pitter-patter of tears coming soon after.

"But..." Blooded palms found her spear again, and she rose to her feet. A fierce, tear-streaked expression on her face. "If there is no way to be free of the Evil Piece. If the only paths left to me are to crawl through the dirt until my own name rots out of my skull, or death... then I will choose while I still understand what choosing means. I do not want the world to see what I would become."

She took one step back and lifted her spear, readied it and herself.

"My name is 衛策," she said. "Viser."

Her spear gleamed at us.

"Let whatever is left of me die with dignity. In honorable combat."


(Later) [Kuoh Academy]


[Rain] |Uncommon Ability|
Allows you to make it rain from the skies, the more energy you expend the larger the scale of the rain. Slightly boosts water-based abilities and slightly weakens fire-based ones.

...
​

"Darn, not even a rare one," I clicked my tongue. The office chair creaking as I sat back. "Well, ain't that just fine. Three for three, and not one of 'em has been any sort of useful!"

Three silver tickets.

That was all I had to show for clearing out that stray devils house from foundation to rafters. Sure, I wasn't fool enough to spit on good Tickets, but I'd been hoping the rolls would come up kinder. Not so much. [Rain] was near useless in a fight against anyone worth fighting, and I don't even have water-based abilities to boost with it neither! I reckon the only use I'll get from this is make it easier to water the fields back home, not like we devils didn't have water spells for that already. But... I ought to be thankful that I had three whole Ability Slots now. I just wish it had been from a more useful ability...

The one that came before was at least mildly useful.


[Edna Mode] |Rare Skill|
Incredibles - You are not a simple fashionista baby, you are THE fashionista. You are a master of designing clothes and costumes; if someone needs a costume, they come to YOU. Your skill in creating costumes can be compared to a Tinker even because you are just THAT good.

...
​

But the first roll?

The first roll had been worse!


[Cuddle Emperor] |Rare Trait|
Your existence is one that "Cuddles Greatly." Your hugs and snuggles are incredible, being able to comfort even broken souls. Being cuddled by your cuddles feel like being wrapped by the softest, warmest blanket, and they are even therapeutic, helping stabilise the mental state and comfort whoever you are targeting.

...
​

"Sona will be here soon, you clingy cat..." I whispered.

"Mrr~rrm..." Neferpitou purred as she sat crosswise on my lap, arms in blue sleeves wrapped around my neck. And hearing me say that, her face just nuzzled deeper the crook of it, her white hair tickled my cheek. "... just five more minutes, my King."

Well, it sure was working at least.

I gave her one last scratch behind the ear, wishing I could relax like she was. The last time I was in Kuoh Academy, they tried to take my head off. Closest I got to death right there.

But this student council room was comfy enough.

Gloomy, even with the rich blue curtains pulled back and tied in with gold tassels. It was more like a dining room than an office, with a deep blue carpet on fine wood and a dark oaken table for twelve. Bookcases lined the walls with leather-bound books, and it even had a chandelier overhead. Reminded me a lot of Glasya-Labolas. And it sure was nice to see some proper interior design instead of those modern ningen offices with them wheeled office chairs and their endless plastic everywhere! Not much in the way of fine art or just gold like you'd expect from devil design, and it made this room very modest by devil standards.

Could scarcely fault a devil for not thinking this was the command center of a Satan's little sister!

But I suppose that was the point: blending in with the ningens, so not a nosy parent or faculty could sniff anything strange.

But would it kill 'em to add a potted plant or two? Maybe even a cactus sitting snug in a mug? I could barely feel a thing here! After spending so much time in them woods this whole place felt deadened: Not a piece of living wood to turn into root-spears or leaf-blades with [Verdance], and not a single, living plant to draw energy with using [Nature's Child].

At least the rest of Kuoh Academy was a lot better. Grassy strips were laid out all around campus for bushes and pines to sit in, not to mention the woods out back near Rias' building. In fact, I might just—

The door clicked open.

Sohna stepped into the Student Council Room, Her violet eyes moved over me first. Then to Neferpitou, all cradled against the black of my suit jacket. And behind the faint glint of her violet glasses, one eyebrow rose.

"I see you've made yourself comfortable."

"We sure did," I smiled, before patting the chimera-ant on my lap. "Alright, girl, break's over for us."

"Mr~rm..." Neferpitou whined, but with a flick of a cat ear, she slowly disentangled herself from me. Her red eyes aimed a dirty look at the bespectacled Sitri girl before she stood behind me with all the poise and manners of a royal guard.

"Cats," I shrugged to Sona with a smile. "Now, what did you want to talk about?"

She cleared her throat, shifting to straightening her posture in her Kuoh Academy Uniform. "I was just in a call with Serafall-onee-sama and—"

"Serafall called?" I sat up straight, "Is she doing alright?"

"I... yes, she looked well," She blinked, then continued in a more formal tone. "Serafall wanted to convey her formal acknowledgements and congratulations for your new office as... Bailiff to the Steward. Your contribution to Kuoh Town's peace and security last night was greatly appreciated."

"Well you can tell her that there's more of that 'peace and security' comin' once I really get started!" I chuckled, "I'm always happy to help the Satan Leviathan!"

"She'll be pleased to hear that," Sona smiled a small smile.

"Yep," I said, leaning back with a relieved smile, "It's mighty fine news for me too!"

It would've been a crying shame if my little spat with Rias had barred me from Kuoh Town— the motherlode of all Ticket-earning opportunities this side of ningen country. But not no more! Now I had the formal backing of a Satan that guaranteed that I could stay right where I pleased. Hell, I even had more than just that: I also had all the leave a devil could ask for to ride up and down Kuoh Town, claiming bounty on Stray Devil scalps and picking clean whatever opportunity caught my eye. And the best part? Rias or any other Seventy-Two couldn't do a damned thing about it but sit there chewing nails while I do!

And who said working for Satan didn't pay well!

"Serafall also wishes to remind you," Sona began, returning to that careful official tone of hers, "Rias' Peerage will still be active with their own Stray Devil Elimination duties, and she hopes that there will be no quarrels over quarry or grounds."

"So, it's first come first serve?" I raised an eyebrow, "You know that I'm gonna beat them to the punch every time, right?"

That ticked her some, just the corner of her mouth tightening. But instead of saying anything, she walked over to the far wall of the student council room to where golden sunlight streamed through clear windows. She stared out, sun shining on her face, and hands clasped behind her as she looked at Rias' building that stood beyond the gymnasium building.

"Rias's Peerage..." She said with a small proud smile, "they're far from incompetent, Malal. I wouldn't underestimate them."

I barked out a laugh. "I'd hope not! I'm claiming one of 'em once all this is over. No, what I'm saying is—" I paused, before smiling wider. "You know what? How about I just stop jawing and show y'all exactly what I mean in the next few days? Won't we, Pitou?"

Neferpitou gave a soft, approving sound behind me.

Sona's gaze flicked past my shoulder, to my cat, then returned to me. "So long as there is no bloodshed, realised or attempted, between authorized parties."

"Does this mean that we can go over there and talk to 'em?"

"It..." She froze, just for a breath. "... is rude to enter a lady's personal quarters without her say so, Nebiros-dono."

I nodded. "Fair enough. Do I get a badge of office to wave in their faces when I run into them on the street, then? Something like a sheriff's star?" I asked, reaching into my coat. And before she could answer, I pulled out a gleaming bronze badge and held it up between two fingers. "Because I already made one!"

SHERIFF
KUOH TOWN

For a moment, Sona only stared at me. Then she adjusted her violet glasses slow, as if she was thinking about it some. And... merciful girl that she was, she let me have it!

"There... is no formal badge of office," she said with a sigh. "So I suppose you may wear it."

"It's official?! Fantastic!" I barked a laugh and pinned the bronze star to my chest. Then I tossed the second badge over my shoulder. "Pitou, here's yours!"

Behind me, Neferpitou's ear flicked. One slim, clawed hand caught the badge without trouble, pinning it to her chest in one smooth movement. "Thank you, my King. It is an honour to serve in this position."

Sona's gaze moved from me, to the badge on my chest, to Neferpitou, then back to me. "You are giving a symbol of office... to your familiar as well?" she said, each word placed with care, The look she gave me over the rims of her violet glasses told me she knew exactly what I had done, and worse, that I knew she knew. "For the record, the Steward's office expects a degree of seriousness from you."

"Oh, I am serious." I smoothed the front of my black coat and let the bronze star catch the daylight. "You'll find last night wasn't luck. I am, in fact, very good at wrangling stray devils. Even on my own."

"I…" Sona paused, and a faint blush touched her cheeks.

We both knew how much of last night's work had ended up in my and Pitou's hands. And how little of it ended between the seven of them.

"In regards to last night's events, there is one other matter I would like to discuss. Serafall-onee-sama—" She stopped, just enough to continue in a softer tone, "No, I would like to thank you for helping me. My Peerage and I would have been ill-prepared to handle four Stray Devils at once. Who knows how many humans they would have preyed upon if not for your intel, and how many injuries we would have sustained in the attempt."

"And you're always welcome." I grinned as bright as my new Sheriff's Star. "If you ever need backup again, just holler and I'll come running."

Sona smiled back in return. "I appreciate the offer. I have a feeling that—"

"Huh."

"—Is something wrong?"

"Oh, Rias' catgirl is just eyeballing us." I said turning in my seat to smile and wave out the window.

"Koneko is what?!" Sona rushed to the window.

There Koneko stood across the academy grounds, framed by one of the polished windows of the old schoolhouse. The catgirl looked small and stiff behind the glass, her hands were clenched white around the curtain she was peeking behind like a kid would hide behind her mother's skirts. A pair of golden eyes staring wide-eyed in a slack-jawed look of horror at the chimera-ant behind me. And Neferpitou... she turned her head just enough to acknowledge her. Her red eyes settled on hers, unblinking, while the rest of her remained perfectly still.

Across the field, Koneko vanished from the window.

I lowered my hand. Still wasn't sure what made her so scared of me.

"Malal," Sona said, turning from the window with a long sigh, "you have been an authorized officer of Kuoh Town for less than a day. Do not begin your tenure by tormenting one of Rias's Peerage."

"Can't help it," I shrugged. "We still have a Rating Game to fight. Taunting is fair game."

"I understand. But kindly keep it off my campus, I will not tolerate disorder in—"

That was when it happened.

"Sona," I said.

She stopped. Maybe it was my voice, or maybe it was the way Neferpitou's red-eyes were practically shining in anticipation.

I myself had gone stiff in my chair, smile gone, eyes staring right at the wall in front of me, but was in fact, looking far, far away to the right. Someone had stepped into the edge of my [Byakugan]— No, not stepped. She was zipping through the woods behind Rias's building fast enough that she was leaving a storm of leaves in her wake. Black hair on black kimono with two black tails. Whoever she was, she moved quicker than a horse at full gallop and carried enough magic power to light up like the sun in my eyes.

"Is Rias expecting visitors?"

Sona's brows drew together above her violet glasses. "It is daytime; the portion of the day that I'm responsible for. Rias would have informed me if her Peerage was receiving any visitors. Why do you ask?"

"Because someone just came in through the back window of her building."

"What?"

"Another catgirl," I said, watching the shape slink into the room where Koneko was snacking. "She's talking to Koneko... who's not looking too happy to see her. And—"

A chop to the back of the neck, and Koneko folded into her arms like a marionette with its strings cut.

I was standing before the thought finished forming, bronze shotgun in my hand, black cowboy hat pressed to my Lucifer silver hair.

"She's knocked Koneko out. Pitou—"

"Circle around and attack from the rear, my King?"

"You got it." I reloaded with spell-totems for a potential hostage situation.

My bronze shotgun clicked shut, waking Sona from her panic. "Wait," she squawked. "We don't know—"

"Gather up your posse," I said, already moving. "We'll try to herd her your way. And watch out: This one's fast."

I hit the window boot-first.

Glass burst outward in a bright, singing spray. My boots caught the sill for half a heartbeat, and [Muscular] activated. Ten legs worth of muscle fibers suddenly occupied my two legs. Bulging my calves and thighs under my dark pants. If I was a normal ningen, this would have been enough to challenge even middle-class devils in a competition of raw strength...

But when it was a devil muscle of a pure-blooded devil?

I kicked off the wall hard enough to make the air crack around me, shooting through the sunlit air like a bullet. The Student Council Room shot away behind me, Sona's sharp voice lost under the rush of wind. Neferpitou shot off at an angle, even quicker than me, cutting the long way around to reach Rias's building from the rear. For me, I took the straightest shot to the commotion.

The span of two football fields and I shot across it in a blink of an eye.

Rias's private schoolhouse rushed up, and I hit one of its 2nd floor windows shoulder-first, rolling through the broken opening in a glitter of falling glass. My boots skidded over expensive carpet, and my shotgun was raised and ready...

But Koneko's room was already empty.

"Front door." I clicked my tongue, immediately seeing where they were heading.

I ran. My boots hammered through Rias's private halls, over fine rugs and past flower arrangements that didn't deserve the kind of morning they were getting. I caught up, almost catching a clear line of sight of the catnapper as she turned the corner on the ground floor, just a shadow darting out of view.

Neferpitou turned the corner, and we met at the front entrance where this whole ugly business with Rias had started, her white tail lashing once behind her.

We nodded, and burst out into daylight together.

The kidnapper had made the open field. She stopped with Koneko limp over one shoulder, one hand braced around the catgirl's legs, the other already gathering magic. She was tall, sharp-built, and dark-haired, with enough of Koneko in her face to make the blood relations plain, and enough anger in her posture to make the difference plainer. It only got angrier when she caught sight of my cat.

Then the sky glittered.

A dome of crystalline magic snapped into place around the dirt yard, sealing the space from sky like a glass bowl trapping a spider against the floor. The barrier rang once, a clear bell like clinking wine glasses.

Sona's Peerage took their positions outside the dome. Their arms raised and their demonic power flaring in coordinated unison. The crystal shell pulsed again, thicker this time, looking even more solid behind the prism-like rainbow refractions. And Sona stood at the center of them, shoulder-length black hair still neat despite the chaos, violet eyes hard behind violet glasses.

The catgirl in the black kimono shifted Koneko higher on her shoulder, just as Neferpitou and I came walking up behind her.

My bronze shotgun leveled with her back.

"Well now," I said, walking up. "Looks like you took the scenic route right into a box canyon."

The catgirl turned around, facing me proper. But her golden eyes... just glossed over me, dismissed me, but stopped as it landed on Neferpitou beside me. Her pretty face paled. "You... You wear her face wrong." she whispered to my cat. Anger, bitterness, regret... The only thing that wasn't in her voice was sounding happy to see Pitou. "You little stolen thing. Even your chakra feels unnatural. An abomination like you shouldn't be using senjutsu, nya."

"Easy there..." I shifted the shotgun, the sun glinting off its polished amber-bronze. "What's your name?"

The catgirl's eyes finally flicked to me, and now there was hatred enough for everybody. "And you keep it beside you? Would you still do so if were able to feel what we feel?"

I didn't take the bait of answering her questions. I just kept the shotgun, my trigger finger and my voice all calm. For now.

"Who are you? And why're you after Rias' Rook Piece?"

"I'm Kuroka, and I'm Koneko's sister."

That quieted the field. And Koneko made a faint sound, a whimper.

The older catgirl's face cracked for half a heartbeat. Then her eyes went back to Neferpitou, and the softness died. "I'm only doing what I should have done years ago, nya," she said. "Rias, the Satans, everyone... you've all failed her. Brought that thing here to her."

My finger pressed every so slightly against the shotgun's trigger. "Lady, I don't know what the hell you're on about, but you've got nowhere to run. So, why don't you just set the catgirl down nice and easy?"

I wasn't stupid enough to think she'd listen.

Under my black suit jacket, [Muscular] prepared. Layers of muscle gripping upon itself, each layer doubling, even tripling my strength making it stronger than five devils... stronger than ten, than twenty, than fifty.

'Course it came at a cost, and I could feel it already. This much raw power drank up energy faster than a devil-horse riding through a desert. But... It didn't matter. [Nature's Child] let me draw energy from every plant connected to me, [Verdance] connected me to every plant in my sensory range... and [Byakugan] widened that same sensory range to everything in a mile-wide radius.

Near unlimited demonic power... all of it feeding into [Muscular] with all the energy it needed. Seeping into the fibers, permeating its cells, supercharging my strength and speed.

Bronze creaked as I squeezed.

Purple Fog bloomed under her feet. Thick as smoke, it rolled and swirled like it had a mind of its own. Puffing out in a widening flower or a snake made of a purple cloud.

I watched, but not passively. I watched it develop, and while I did, I was charging my spell totems and my shotgun with as much excess demonic power as [Intermediate Item Creation] was confident that it could sustain. This weapon that I made explicitly to bring a world of hurt to anything stronger than me... And I was tripling, quintupling its output. Geometrically and other fancy words like that. Enough to seriously harm someone High-class. Maybe even low Ultimate-class...

It's killing time.

And finally, in the purple fog behind Kronecia, two more silhouettes came walking out of the bruise-colored haze.

One had a hand raised around a rotating knot of purple fog, fingers curled like he was holding the invisible can that the purple fog was coming from. Red mage robes, round spectacles, and spiky brown hair.

The other was big. Broad through the shoulders, thick through the chest, swept-back gray hair, leather armor strained over muscle and old scars. He had the look of a man used to being the wall other folks hid behind.

"Georg," Kuroka said sharply to Red-Robes.

"Ah, Kuroka, it would seem you couldn't—"

I immediately shot the guy who didn't have a human shield or armour.

I didn't know who this Georg fella was, or what his deal was with the catgirl. But I knew he was a bit too friendly with the catnapper, and that he looked like he'd squeal in pain the most, and that was good enough for me.

Carian Phalanx-shot sang like a hundred knives being drawn from their sheathes. Spectral blue daggers came shooting through the air in the dozens, but... a thick wisp of Purple Fog was in the way. Didn't think much of it. Fog was fog, you just shoot through it. But... all the Carian daggers that cut into the purple haze simply vanished. Just went in one side and didn't come out the other, putting two and two together and I figured they were probably stolen out of the air and teleported somewhere else.

Fortunately for me, the fog was not wide enough to cover all of him.

Carian Daggers found meat.

Georg's left arm and left leg came apart in ribbons of red, robe cloth and flesh shredding together as he shouted and collapsed sideways, the rotating knot of purple fog sputtering in his hand.

"Geor—!" The big guy's eyes widened in shock, swivelling towards me just in time to see me pull the trigger with a big grin.

Rennala's Full Moon-shot was fired horizontally for the first time.

And on this bright sunny day, the moon shone bright.

I've seen the Rating Games where the 3rd Ranker, Bedeze Abaddon, launched powerful spells with all the demonic Power he had. Mine wasn't like his Fireballs that screamed their searing fury and hatred, or his Lightning Bolts that roared their judgment with a shock of thunder. No, that wasn't how the Raya Lucaria Academy made their spells. Rennala's Full Moon almost sang, the words being an epitaph from Queen Rennala herself: 'May the final moments of your mortality be graced with the Majesty of the Moon.'

The truck-sized moon hit him in the chest before he could lift his arms up to block it.

Silvery-blue light exploded, washing over him and sweeping him away in a cone of lunar ruin. Gouging a widening cone trench that reached all the way to the edge of the prismatic dome, rainbow colours rippling across the surface from the impact.

I walked forward, already reloading. Bronze shotgun cracked open, and spent spell totems misted as they clattered to the dirt.

"Georg! It's time to leave, nya!" Kuroka shouted, lowering her unconscious 'sister' down in the purple Fog. A magic circle snapping open in her hands, deep purple light spinning fast enough to blur.

But Neferpitou was already there. The shorter catgirl crossed the distance without so much a streak, all claws and blazing red eyes, and batted Kuroka's hand aside before the circle could finish turning. And for one sharp fraction of a second, the two of them were face to face. Cat to cat. A blur of claws and slitted eyes.

Just a few paces where they clawed at each other, the Georg fella was laying on the dirt, more purple fog poured out of his uninjured hand. He was looking a bit pale, maybe even half-dead, but nowhere near dead enough for my comfort.

Simple enough to fix.

My shotgun snapped closed, and Georg looked up from his grunting and bleeding just in time to find both barrels staring down at him.

"Get away from him!"

The shout hit a half-second before the man did.

The gray-haired bruiser came soaring back through the torn-open field, shirtless now, his leather armor blasted clean off his chest. Cuts striped his torso from waist to shoulder. He should have been dead, or at least, have the decency to stay down. But he crossed the distance on a chain of explosions bursting from beneath his boots. Each blast kicked him forward harder than the last, turning him into a flying brick.

I pulled both triggers in Georg's face anyway.

He was lying flat in the mess of his own blood, should've been the end of him. Except... with him down low like that, there was more Purple Fog between my barrels and his body than before. The cloud curled over him like a blanket. My shot vanished into it without a flash, and without a sound. Gone like they disappeared behind the curtains of a magician's stage show.

... I was starting to hate this fog.

Ammo wasted, the big man arrived with nothing stopping him.

I brought the spent shotgun up crosswise on instinct, catching his fist against the barrels instead of my face. The impact rang through my arms hard enough to bite bone.

Then his knuckles exploded.

A blast detonated from his fist on contact with the metal, sending my black suit jacket fluttering and bending my shotgun inward with a shriek of bronze. The barrels warped under the explosion until it looked like a crooked twig that someone had stepped on.

The gray-haired man just stood in front of me. Smoke curling off his fist and rising up past his nasty grin. "You held up against my punch? Few people have ever done that before. You're stronger than you look, you filthy devil, I'll give you that. But..." he looked down at the bent shotgun in my hands, "What are you going to do now without your toy?"

Ultimate-class strength and constitution. Yep, it couldn't be anything less than Ultimate-class.

That settled it.

We were dealing with real tough guys... But no one we couldn't kill.

"This can't get any better than this!" I laughed, tossing aside the ruined bronze shotgun. "That was my 'polite' toy. So, I reckon you earned yourself a ticket to see something bigger."

My magic circle—the eight-headed sigil of House Nebiros—spun beneath my boots. It wasn't easy putting something big in a Dimensional Pocket.

His grin fell, and his fist raised. "Like I'd let you!"

I watched it in slow motion. His muscled shadow loomed, his fist just rocketing towards me. I was prepared to take a punch or two to pull this off. Likely deprive myself of any 'Without a scratch' bonuses for this fight.

But then...

The big man blasted himself backward on instinct, explosions kicking from his feet... all to dodge the spear that came for him. The golden sunlight glinting off the spear's edge that had thrust almost close enough to kiss the skin over his jugular.

A woman in a blue china dress stood between us, her eyes cold, her green hair flowing past her shoulders, and both her hands gripping steady a long, red-tasselled spear humming with demonic power green-edged force. No battlecries, not a single wasted motion entering the fight, she just arrived to lend me a hand. Someone as powerful as him... She probably wouldn't have even nicked him. But she tried anyway.

Satan bless her.

"Bailiff!" the china girl shouted, stance steady as she kept her spear pointed at him, "Do what you must!"

One stolen second... that was all I needed.

"Viser!" I laughed as my magic circle spun faster to completion. "You're a sight for sore eyes!"


(Earlier) [Outskirts of Kuoh Town]

"My name is 衛策," she said. "Viser."

Her spear gleamed at us.

"Let whatever is left of me die with dignity. In honorable combat."

The rickety, half-rotting floorboards of the house creaked as I stepped forward and scratched my jaw, looking at the spear she brought to the shotgun fight. "I don't do honourable that well," I said. "But if you want to go down in your grave swinging, I can respect that."

"Don't," Sona's voice came just a scant moment before I could pull the trigger. Her violet eyes stayed on the girl, but the angle of her chin told me plain enough that the order was meant for me.

I looked down at her hand, then at the stray devil, then back at Sona. "Well, she asked nicely."

"Only because she does not know the last option."

"The last option?" Viser's face twisted, but she didn't move from her martial arts stance. Her spear still held steady like I'd been planted in bedrock, already resigned. "There is no way to remove the Evil Piece. You said it yourself."

"I did," Sona said calmly. "The Evil Piece cannot be removed, but the mental collapse can be halted."

"What?" The stray devil went very still.

"If another King accepts you into their peerage before the mutation fully consumes you, the Evil Piece will re-anchor. The damage will take some time to recover from, but you can remain yourself."

"That's it?!" The green-haired girl laughed harshly. "That's your grand counsel? That I should bow my head and put on another collar, like the obedient hounds your peerage became?"

The six girls standing behind Sona bristled at that, all narrow eyed and scowling. That surprised me some. I expected them to look worried or shocked. Some gotcha line how they've never thought of peerages like that before. But no, none of them looked surprised by that; in fact, they look downright insulted! Insulted that this stray devil would insinuate something about their King.

"Yes, that is precisely what I'm offering: A place in my peerage."

Now it was the stray devil who looked shocked, green brows furrowing, and mouth open as she struggled to find the words. Couldn't blame her: The offer came out of nowhere. Even I was surprised.

And before anyone else could speak up, Sona kept talking.

"I cannot give you your old peerage back, nor can I cleanse what was done to you." The Sitri heiress said, the damp light from the torn walls catching on her violet glasses, hiding her eyes for a moment. "I cannot even pretend the Evil Piece System is not without its costs. Because If you join me, you will owe obedience within its structure. However... you will also receive protection, instruction, shelter, and the right to remain a thinking person instead of becoming something that has to be hunted through the woods."

"Beautiful," The stray devil spat blood onto the molded floor as her eyes burned. "You cloak your chains in the language of order. You sound no different from my former master who once took me in beneath his banner! He too spoke of shelter and protection. Tell me: How do I know you're no better than that old monster?!"

"Because she's Sona Sitri."

Everyone turned to the tallest girl in the group, Sona's Queen Piece.

Tsubaki stepped up to stand beside her King. Her glare fierce through her teal glasses. "My clan called my power blasphemy to the Shinra Clan. They kept me apart from everyone because I was born wrong by their standards. Sona-kaichou knew exactly what I was, and knew that recruiting me would scorn the Shinra Clan. But she took me in regardless, and gave me all the opportunity to become the best version of myself. Sona-kaichou is the kindest person I've ever known, and I will not hesitate to defend her honour if you insult her again."

The china girl's eyes narrowed, her spear kept level to Sona's face. But she did not interrupt.

Momo swallowed and stepped in before the silence could harden. "My family are normal humans. They've served Sona-kaichou's House for generations. When I said I wanted to join her peerage, most people treated it like a joke." Her fingers tightened around the fading barrier circle at her wrist. "Sona didn't. She was the only one who believed in me, and I work hard everyday to prove that I'm worthy of the chance she gave me."

Tomoe thumbed the pommel of her katana, deep in thought. "My family are exorcists too. An evil spirit cursed our bloodline, and I was born carrying it. Joining Sona-kaichou's peerage saved my life." Her lips pressed together. "My family... they exiled me for accepting that cure and scorned Sona for even offering. But she did not hate them, and she cared only to ensure I had somewhere to stand afterward."

Reya gave a small shrug, though her voice was steadier than her face. "My grandmother was a witch. I barely inherited enough magic to light a candle. Joining a peerage was the only way I could reach the world she belonged to. Sona-kaichou could have chosen someone stronger, anyone else would have been. But she thought that my determination was something special, and I've been trying to prove her right ever since."

Ruruko shuffled up, rubbing her elbow. "I... I wasn't anyone special like everyone else here. I only managed to piece out that Sona and everyone else in the student council were devils and confronted them about it. Sona-kaichou let me join because I had proven smart enough to figure it out when no one else had. She told me that my intellect was something to be nurtured."

Tsubasa put a bandaged hand on her hip. "I could sense monsters and evil spirits, so I thought that meant I was the one who was supposed to deal with them. Sona-kaichou actually offered for me to join, I kept telling her, until one night when I bit off more than I could chew. I actually almost died then, but Sona-kaichou always said she'd had my back. And I'm glad that was true that night."

"I also called you suicidal," Sona said, and the blue-haired tomboy smiled.

"Every day till I joined, kaichou."

Despite herself, the stray devil stayed quiet, her lips tightening. It was a small crack, wasn't much I reckon. But it looked like the first sign that she understood these girls didn't have chains rattling between their ankles. Nothing too generous, but the room had brightened a bit, shifting half an inch away from execution, and everyone's noticed. Especially her. Her grip on the spear had changed too. Still ready and dangerous, but it wasn't the white-knuckle grip of someone committed to dying in the next ten seconds.

"And you?"

It took me a second to realize she meant me.

"Me?" I pointed at myself. "I'm not part of her peerage."

"You are not one of hers? Even better," Her eyes held on mine, sharp and hostile and tired of being handled gently. "And you do not sound like you believe in any of this. What do you think?"

Sona glanced at me then, and the look in her violet eyes said several things at once, most of them threats dressed politely. Behind her, the peerage watched me with varying degrees of dread. Fair. I hadn't exactly given the impression that I'm good with improving delicate situations like these.

With a sigh, I took off my cowboy hat to run a hand through my hair, shotgun on the crook of my arm. "I think... I preferred how my night was going before everyone started talking about their feelings."

The stray devil's mouth twitched despite herself.

"Mal..." Sona warned.

"She asked me what I think, so I'll tell her. And what I think ..." I looked back at the stray devil. "... is that I'm not moved by the tragic backstory. No offense. Well, some offense. Everyone in this room either has one already or is young enough to earn one later. Yours is uglier than most, sure, but that doesn't change the next thirty seconds."

The stray devil's eyes narrowed.

"You can die proud, or you can take the hand being offered and see if there's still something left worth doing after tonight," I said with a shake of my head. "I can't tell you what that something is. Don't know if you even have one. But if you do, I'll bet horsefeathers it'll take a whole lot more guts taking a chance with Sona's peerage to see it done than choosing the quick exit with me."

The stray devil's spear dipped by a fraction. "... There is something that still needs to be done."

No one spoke after that.

The stray devil looked at Sona again. Really looked this time. Not at the glasses, or the uniform, or the title sitting invisible on her shoulders, but at the girl underneath all that structure. Sona stood amid rot, blood, and moon-cut wreckage with her hands empty at her sides. She did not reach out. She did not smile. She did not try to make the choice beautiful.

"I will enter your peerage." The stray devil raised her gaze at last. "But this one has a condition."

"Name it." Sona didn't hesitate.

The china girl's grip tightened around the red-tasselled spear in her hands. "When the time comes to announce their deaths to their families, I do not want polite letters filled with righteous drivel, calling them beasts and unfortunate losses." Her voice lowered, and a flicker of hurt crossed her face, quickly swallowed back behind her gritted teeth. "I want their names to be spoken properly, and their families to know the truth: They did not die as craven beasts, they died struggling to return home to the people waiting for them."

For the first time since the conversation began, Sona's expression changed. Just a bit softening around the eyes.

"Yes. Of course, I'll help you."

The stray devil breathed in slowly. Her shoulders rose, then... she sank to one knee. The wood creaked under her as she bowed, spear planted on the wood and pointing straight up. Slowly, she bowed her head. It wasn't much of a dignified place here in this rotting, broken down house in the woods, but she did make it look solemn.

"Then I pledge my spear to you, Sona Sitri."

From Sona's hand, a glowing Knight Piece rose, floating in the air between them and burning with red pulses of demonic power. Red lit up the Sitri heiress' face, reflecting off her violet glasses as she stared down at the spear-wielding stray devil.

"By oath to House Sitri, and by witness of House Nebiros... I, Sona Sitri, hereby order you: Heed me, Viser. Be severed from the ruin of your former Pact, and let no corruption claim what still remains of you. I accept your spear beneath my command. And I command thee..."

The Knight Piece shot like a spell, and struck her. And Viser's teeth clenched. The red light shining over her entire body, enveloping even her spear.

"Become my Knight."


(Present Time) [Kuoh Academy]

I tipped my black cowboy hat at the china girl.

"Much obliged, Viser," I said. "But he'll take it from here."

Her silver eyes stayed on the big man, spear still leveled, voice flat as she parroted. "He?"

I stepped back from the summoning circle beneath my boots, and let it answer for me. Silver light tore through the dirt, and the air grew thick with demonic power.

The big man looked down at the magic circle that had stopped spinning.

A bronze hand came up out of the circle and seized the dirt ground. Not really a hand, more of an... oversized armoured gauntlet. Fists made of solid, magically reinforced bronze, big enough to make kittens out of horses. Another hand followed, then... A head emerged, shaped like a bronze pipe that had grown itself a dour, frowning face. Golden eyes igniting, bright and utterly humorless.

The big man took one step back...

... And Ichabod the Imperfect kept rising.

A bronze chest as broad as a car emerged from the circle. Barrier spells crawled across it in interlocking protective stacks fifteen-layers deep, each one tied to magically-reinforced bronze imbued with an obscene amount of demonic power only possible for someone who had an unlimited amount of it. Packed so dense that it was almost more demonic power than it was bronze. The prismatic dome outside rippled again, straining from the pressure of his arrival.

At this point, Ichabod the Imperfect could finally step out of the circle.

Twelve feet of reinforced bronze dragging itself into the world. Like a resurrected corpse climbing out of its grave to personally deal with something irritating who had interrupted his sleep.

His fists clenched and spectral blades snapped out from each enormous forearm, the unnaturally sharp blades glittering with motes of cold moonlight, and gleaming like it'd been made of pure stars. The Greatblade Phalanx flared to life. Spectral blue greatswords, taller than most men, formed a floating halo of swords around its head, ready to launch themselves at one who dares stay out of reach.

The whole field froze.

All of Sona's peerage stared, some whimpered.

Ichabod the Imperfect stood to his full height, golden eyes fixed on the 'big' man who'd been swallowed whole by its shadow.

Under the brim of my black cowboy hat, I smiled wide enough to hurt.


"You done screwed up now, boy."

​





- Big Bronze and Cat Complications End -​


Cats x Chimera-Ant
In which... The fight goes from Bad to Yee-haw! Real Cat-to-Cat communication occurs. And the Kuoh Academy Student Council collectively has a really bad day.
Coming soon to public release!
​

AN:

He hasn't even used [Verdance] offensively... we're still catching up to the rolls 3-4 chapters ago. We need more fights, and I promise you, we are. And it's just gonna get more fun from here.

A balance has been found in... trying to balance DxD to the other power systems entering due to Chaos Gacha.

In canon DxD, everyone's stats scale... to their magic/demonic power/etc. Everyone were all damage sponges where the only build was to build raw DPS. Every punch needed to be nuke-strength to even scratch someone... which really screws with writing a multi-power-system fic. It's already a massive problem if the local physics/power system fluctuates from scene to scene, how much more when I'm trying to mesh it with precise HxH Nen, the semi-grounded physical interactions of MHA quirks, and etc?

In this fic, the stat scaling is less: Everyone has smallish HP pools, but high armour/magic-resist/damage mitigation. Doing damage relies more on proper concentration of magic/demonic power/etc to punch through everyone's natural resistances. AKA more like HunterxHunter's Ryu.

With this, physics and power levels are better preserved and better demonstrated.

About this fic's 'Stray Devil Mutation', it's the... compassionate non-canon option. "Team DxD, if we don't capture these stray devils, they'll mutate uncontrollably, lose their sanity and become a dangerous threat to everyone around them. For the safety of themselves and innocent people, we must either return or eliminate them."

Otherwise... we get canon DxD.

"Team DxD, your mission is to round up all of 'em runaway pretend-devils. Being a stray devil is illegal under Satans' Laws, and their masters say they've done and gone stray. And that's good enough for us! Now, you can try sweet-talkin' them pretend-devils into turnin' themselves in, if you are so inclined. But we do not, I say, we do not care one whit if they claim their masters dips 'em in mayonnaise and makes 'em squeal like a devil-piglet every other night. Them pretend-devils are goin' straight back to the Underworld! Dead or alive, I say! Dead. Or. Alive!"

Ishibumi really, really tried to just drop those lore bits in Vol 1 and Vol 14, and act like it was nothing. Lmao. In this fic, it's going to be a bit of both for variety.
