#4,730
Chapter 9: Cat x Chimera

In which... The fight goes from Bad to Yee-haw! Real Cat-to-Cat communication occurs. And the Kuoh Academy Student Council collectively has a really bad day. In hell.


[Kuoh Academy]

Ichabod the Imperfect stepped out of the magic circle to a quiet Kuoh Academy.

Like watching the biggest gun of the fight climb out of its holster by itself, every soul on this ruined baseball field knew better than to move an inch... at least, not before they knew who I was gonna aim it at.

So, they stared.

A dozen pairs of eyes stared buggy-wide at the twelve-foot bronze construct, at the blue stars glittering around the Carian Slicers jutting from Ichabod's wrists... and at the shining blue halo of Greatsword Phalanx floating above his dour bronze pipe-head. Nobody moved as a passing breeze dragged dust around our boots and over the solid bronze pretzel of my shotgun, the same gun they'd been so damn proud of ruining just a few moments ago. Well, Ichabod had their attention now, and they looked mighty surprised to see him despite him being famous on the Devilnet.

I'd wager they hadn't expected the upgrades.

Folks who've seen me pull a devil-rabbit out my hat tend to get real proud about knowing how the trick works, right up until I pull out a hungry devil-coyote to eat their faces. And ain't it just nice to have overconfident enemies make carefully laid plans out of outdated news?

I gave the field one slow look, meeting their stiff eyes. It went past Georg the red-robed fog-slinger, past Kuroka the cat-woman trying to drag Koneko the cat-girl out of here, and settled my eyes on the big gray-haired bastard who'd ruined my perfectly good shotgun. The long shreds of his shirt and armour still flapping in the breeze, and whole torso still covered in angry red cuts from my Rennala's Full Moon-shot.

The cocky son of a gun still had a vicious smile. So, I matched it with one of mine as I said...


"Ichabod, Kill this guy."
​

That was all the warning he got. His eyes barely even had time to go wide before blue light burned across his face. Six spectral greatblades tore free from Ichabod's halo and speared through the air and straight at his throat.

The poor bastard tried to dodge.

An orange explosion bloomed under his boots and hurled him sideways, but he was too much man trying to outrun too much blade. All six greatblades caught him in the chest and burst against him in a high tinkling storm of starlight. They didn't skewer through. Against anybody half his strength, they would have pinned him to the dust on six separate pieces, but... they did knock him off balance.

That had been his only chance.

Ichabod was immediately on him like a falling bronze meteor.

Several tons of amber-gold bronze crossed the baseball diamond in one screaming flash of metal. Scalding steam wailed from the vents along his back and shoulders, flaring behind him like angel wings. Most folks see a 'twelve-foot bronze giant' and picture it making big, heavy and slow strides towards them. Plenty of time to make boasts and brandish their blades.

Not mine, I made my metal giant to be as fast as murder.

A bright blue arc split the air as Ichabod's spectral wristblade swept for his throat.

Unarmed, the poor bastard could only block it with his bare arm, and the Ichabod's Carian Slicer carved across them. Blood snapped into the air in a red line. That swipe would've shorn a middle-class devil clean in half.

He only bled to the bone.

Ultimate-class sons of guns were tough, and that was fine by me. Didn't matter none if it took one stroke or a hundred. Dead was dead, and I built myself a patient butcher. And unlike with Rias or Sona, Ichabod's anti-killsteal protocols were disabled. This guy wasn't somebody I was obliged to leave breathing. So, it was only a matter of time before Ichabod's giant hands were twisting his head off like a bottlecap.

Credit where it was due, though. The poor bastard didn't waste the pain.

Even with his arm cut to the bone, he was still smiling as his other fist came down on the forearm of the giant twice his size, aiming to break the wristblade at the root. And before Ichabod's other wristblade could reach him, his knuckles struck bronze, and explosion magic went off like a cannon. Steam and Purple Fog were blasted back in a burst, and... He went flying from his own attack, in a smoking arc that went from the home plate and almost all the way to the outfield. He hit the dirt in a crouch, a vicious smile still carved across his face.

An attack like that... it would have wrecked the old Ichabod.

The one that fought Rias and Sona would have lost the whole arm, a hefty chunk of his torso too, and probably half the spellwork holding him upright.

This Ichabod the Imperfect walked out of the smoke unharmed. His golden eyes burned, and his dour bronze pipe-head frowned like an unimpressed executioner who was just spat at by his 'guest.' Three of the barrier lattices woven on his bronze hide cracked apart and shattered into burning silver fragments.

The other twelve held just fine.

A breath later, the broken three were already knitting themselves back into place. Veins of wood underneath that foot-thick bronze shell pulsed with demonic power, thrumming with all the energy I was pouring into with [Verdance]. Because as long as I stayed in Kuoh's wooded region, and as long as he stayed within my sensory range... Ichabod had unlimited energy.

Seeing all this, the bleeding, grey-haired guy's grin finally evaporated. But mine only widened.

"Ichabod's really something, ain't he?" I chuckled, raising one fist.

Immediately, the twelve-foot-tall bronze giant stopped with his Carian Slicers poised and the shining blue halo of a Greatblade Phalanx reforming around his head... Just one lowered fist away from carving Heracles out of the dirt.

"Before he pulls you apart, set the record straight for me." I looked down at the bleeding gray-haired bastard. "You're Ultimate-class, aren't you?"

"Ultimate-class?!"

Blood ran down his arm and dripped from his fingers, but his voice hit the field like another explosion.

"I AM HERACLES!" He roared, ice-blue eyes wide above a mean snarl. He beat his bleeding chest with his fist. "Inheritor of the name, the soul, and the twelve labors! Before my feet, the gods of Greece laid twelve impossible tasks, and I forged twelve triumphs from every one of them! And now, I bear the Sacred Gear of Variant Detonation. You think the divisions that the Three Factions invented amount to anything now?! Ultimate-class is an insult to what I am!"

Finally, I got a name: Heracles.

"Mighty fine name," my black cowboy hat nodded with me, keeping my smile in the shade. "Hope I can spell it right for your tombstone. But I'm guessing you are one. Ultimate-class, that is. Sure, might be leaning on the lower side of, but still inside that ball park."

"Bold words for someone who needs a construct to fight for him!"

"Don't make a lick of difference to me." I scoffed.

That was the honest truth. Even if Ichabod—made of my craft and my magic—killed him, then it's just as good as if I punched him dead with my own two hands. A bullet didn't stop being mine just because it flew ahead of me.

And Ichabod the Imperfect? He was one bullet that I designed specifically to kill people with fancy 'Ultimate-class Being' titles like him.

So, it was just a question of how many of 'em do I have to kill to prove that I am one myself. 'Cause that's how it works, don't it? I take enough 'Ultimate class' crowns from enough cooling corpses, and I get one myself. Because I hadn't run away from home just to plant my boots at Mid-class or High-class just to call it a day and turn back around. No sir, I wanted to know how far this trail went.

And as far as trails go, Ultimate-class was one of the bigger landmarks that I gotta pass.

"Then fight me!" the bleeding Heracles shouted to me, his ice-blue eyes flicking to Ichabod for a moment. "If it doesn't matter to you, then fight me. Show me the strength granted to you by whatever craven cause you fight for, devil! That is, if you even have one!"

"I do." was my immediate answer. "Something that I couldn't stop thinking about actually."

Ultimate-class wasn't all on its own, but there was a moment from a few nights ago, in the warmth of my campfire, that was. A promise... held inside a mental image that I couldn't, nor did not want, to shake off.

It was the mental image of a flushed Satan Leviathan. A long leg raised over a bronze bench. Dark twin-tails, one draped across her like a curtain, trailing down between her soft bust. Her pink skirt flipped up, and a too-thin layer of pure white cotton that I could see through.

'Promise me that you'll reach Ultimate-class Devil before you think about asking me again.'

That's what Serafall had said, leaning back on her seat, legs spread and practically naked— practically offering herself up to me... What kind of devil would I be if I wasn't motivated by the idea of being the one to claim the Magical Girl Satan's hymen?! One of the most beautiful and most powerful beings in all the Earth, the Underworld, the Heavens and every other plane of existence?!

Wasn't sure if it gave me strength, but it did light a fire in me!

"Then fight me, coward!" He spat the challenge on the dirt between us.

"No..." I said, grinning. "I don't think I will."

For one clean second, Heracles did not know what to do with that. His face twisted, not with fear and not even with anger. Just the confused look of a ningen who didn't understand how one of Satan's own thought. He had called me a coward and expected my pride to rear up and drag me into punching range.

"You poor bastard," I laughed in his face a little. Just a little chuckle as I reached into my suit for some spell totems. "What kind of devil would I be... if I fought fair and square?"

His jaw clenched hard enough I heard teeth grind over the hiss of Ichabod's steam vents. The big man's wounded arm hung loose at his side, blood running from wrist to fingertip, but the rest of him stayed coiled, staring at Ichabod. Truly, this here was someone worthy of the title of Ultimate-class. Exactly what I needed him to be.

"Then you think this tin man is enough to beat me?!"

"Yep, Ichabod'll do you just fine, I reckon," I said, my grin spreading slow beneath the brim of my hat. "I built him special for killing folks with long, fancy titles like you. See, you Ultimate-class peacocks all got the same sickness for big widespread spells: Sky-burning spells, explosions, reality-bending nonsense with clauses like they were invented by a lawyer: 'I am immune to damage from anyone whose names are longer than five letters, but only if they've seen me drink an entire jar of mayonnaise on a Tuesday night.' and such. But hardly none of y'all ever plan for a plain, ugly problem..."

Ichabod's Carian Slicer wrist-blades thrummed with anticipation and bad intentions.

My smile sharpened too. "Problems like a fast and tough construct with blades sharp enough to cut through Ultimate-class resistances."

Heracles' eyes narrowed, especially when my hand pulled out of my suit.

A trio of bronze spell totems, carved with the sigil of Raya Lucaria Academy, sat between my fingers... right before I tossed them up in the air, all lazy-like. They tumbled, end over end, above the baseball field... and stopped, floating in the air as if gravity couldn't reach them anymore. Blue flashed once across their carved sigils, and the magic activated. Like a matchhead catching fire, each spell totem transformed into a blue orb, swirling and glittering with magicks. The air filling with that thin, glassy hum that most Carian Sorceries made.

Magic Downpour.

A rain of glittering sapphires opened overhead. Hundreds of glittering blue magic shards came raining down on him.

"I'll be cheering for you. Don't let me down now!"

Every blue shard and every point of light turned its face toward Heracles. Fixed at him, locked onto him, hated everything in his general direction. The tall, muscled and injured man could only look on. And with his eyes glued to the sky like that, so he didn't see it: He didn't see the moment when...

... I finally lowered my fist.

And beneath that falling blue sky of magic shards, Ichabod the Imperfect charged.



Her King had been gracious enough to tell her about it: Of her old king's final battle.

It took place in another time, in another world... And as unimaginable as it sounded, the fight had been relatively even.

The 'perfect being' that was a Chimera-Ant King who fought against a wizened leader of all the Hunters. The old ningen who summoned a hundred-armed Bodhisattva. One hundred metal hands, each one larger than a fully grown man and each one striking several times faster than the speed of sound underneath the Bodhisattva's serene face. Her old king had an exoskeleton strong enough to withstand ten-thousand blows with barely an ache. But, unable to defeat his opponent in pure speed, he had instead used the lessons of the board game Gungi to eventually find an opening that led him to dismembering the old ningen, limb from limb... only for the ningen to claim a draw by detonating a 'nuclear bomb' implanted in his chest.

Such was this bomb's power that it had slain her old king, along with her fellow Royal Guards.

And here, in a whole different world so strange, the scenario repeats.

"Your chakra might be unnatural... but it's too thin, nya," This 'Kuroka' insulted her. Black hair that flowed loosely past bare shoulders, and her yellow-sashed black 'kimono.' Both fluttering as Pitou's claws glided right across her cheek, "You're too weak to fight me. Give up, nya."

Neferpitou hissed under her breath out of frustration.

In the span of this battle alone, Pitou had landed 29 strikes, and each one of those would have killed the strongest Hunters of her world! But here in this world of 'DxD,' 'demonic power' cloaked, infused and pulsed within the soft skin of her opponent. Her claws—fearsome weapons that could break stone and the sound barrier, could shred and dismember even the strongest ningen—merely slid off this dense cloak of 'demonic power.' Barely even scraping the actual skin of her opponent. The damage mostly just tears on the other catgirl's kimono.

It infuriated her to no end.

This Kuroka was far slower than her, far less skilled than her... could manage to make a fool of her as she fought on the same battlefield as her King! All because her skin was 'thick' like that of the Satan Leviathan.

Disgraceful.

A lesser beast would have continued with this dumb, futile effort, would have continued scratching at stone until its claws split. But Pitou was not one of those lesser creatures. She was humiliated and offended, yes, and also very eager to cut open Kuroka's skull and feast on her living brains until the insulting mouth stopped forming words.

However... She was a Royal Guard Chimera-Ant, Her King's Royal Guard Chimera-Ant familiar, and he did not reward useless stubbornness.

No, only victory and obedience were rewarded with cuddles and headpats. And thus... if slaying her enemy was beyond her capabilities, then pursuing the greater objective would be better.

Neferpitou paused in her attacks, ears twitched atop her head as she mentally prepared contingency plans.

"What's wrong, nya?" Kuroka asked, breath rough but smiling sharp as she circled Pitou, yellow sash swaying at her waist. "Tired already?"

Pitou lowered her heels to the ground. Her white tail steadied behind her blue coat... And Kuroka's golden eyes narrowed as she recognised the universal (or was it multiversal now?) body language of another catgirl about to pounce. Feline instinct made her go on the defensive.

That single, momentary distraction was enough.

Pitou sprang, but not toward Kuroka who had braced for a defense... but to the side, to the smaller nekomata that lay unconscious on the dusty earth. She crossed the distance with the snap of a broken sound barrier, the soft ground compacting under foot.

Kuroka twisted too slow, one hand slashing out with a crescent blue bolt of dense magic.

It clipped only the tips of her afterimage's pale hair.

By then, Pitou had landed beside Koneko on all fours, and the unconscious girl stirred slightly at her proximity, her heartbeat fluttering too fast in Pitou's ear, like prey that quavered at a predator's approach.

Carefully—irritatingly carefully—she hooked one arm beneath Koneko's knees and the other behind her shoulders. Her gentleness was not because she cared, but because a damaged objective would displease her King. And this weak, broken thing was fragile indeed.

"Put. Her. Down."

The air abruptly went cold, and Pitou turned her head.

Kuroka's pupils narrowed into bright vertical slits amongst gold. The loose fall of her black hair rose, strand by strand, as if water had claimed it. The yellow sash at her waist snapped taut without wind. Magic thickened over her skin until Pitou could hear it hum. But they both knew the truth of the matter: Objective secured, and now her enemy was reluctant to even try to strike her.

"Now."

Pitou's lips curled higher. She liked that. She shifted Koneko higher against her chest, and the unconscious girl made a small sound.

Kuroka heard it, and her snarl grew. "Give her back!"

Pitou did not.

Seeing that, Kuroka raised one arm... A single pale hand extending from the billowing, black sleeve of her kimono, like a hand reaching out from pure darkness. And from her fingertips, black-purple fire ignited into being, with which the dark-haired nekomata used to trace symbols in thin air.

Every instinct in Pitou's spine screamed.

Not because the attack would be fast, she was rather certain that it wouldn't be. Kuroka had always been slow. But the abilities... This new world of DxD was simply unpredictable, and her current position was isolated, like a chess piece that found itself cornered on the wrong side of the board. A strategically fragile position indeed. A single attack from an unexpected vector could collapse the position and put her king under check.

Unacceptable.

"Kuroka, don't use it here!" A voice interrupted.

It was the 'Georg' ningen, Pitou recognised immediately.

His bespectacled face appeared in the Purple Fog that collected in dense clumps all across the dirt field. Blood was smeared on his nose and on his spectacles as he lay there, half-buried in the fog as if he'd made it his hospital bed. Clearly, he had been watching—or even feeling—with the fog that he generated. The same fog that touched too much of everything within the dome, that was thickening with every second that ticked by.

Pitou tensed, because she knew several Chimera-ants that had similar Nen abilities; it was concerning to see deployed against her. Especially when she didn't know the exact mechanics and capabilities of it.

Kuroka's head turned a fraction, her hands still tracing symbols. "Don't interfere, Georg!"

"Heracles, suppressed... Kuroka, emotionally compromised..." Georg's voice began enumerating, pushing up his glasses with a bloodied finger and smearing more blood on his nose and glasses. "Medical situation, increasingly dire... Situation, tactically untenable."

Pitou's lips twitched again. Immense pride warming her chest for half a heartbeat. She would have liked to glance to the other side of the battlefield, but she dared not take her eyes away from her opponents. Not that she needed to look to know that her King was winning against this 'Heracles' ningen.

Because of course he would.

"He won't like this." Georg sighed in a reluctant voice. "But you all have left me with no choice."

Then, before Pitou could even account for contingencies, everything went immensely and horribly wrong.

The Purple Fog exploded. Purple filled the ground like it hadn't before, completely covering it until she couldn't tell one step from the next. It poured out of the very dirt itself as if it had been buried underground, unfolding from right under Pitou's own feet. And even as it claimed the ground, so did it with the air, it rose violently, belching like the thick smoke from a bonfire, blocking line of sight with the buildings.


"What's happening?!" "It's through the barrier!" "Sona-kaichou, what do we do?!"
​

Shouts of panic and confusion began chorusing through. Voices that Pitou recognised as Princess Sitri and her peerage. The Purple Fog had broken containment! But amongst the voices, one made Neferpitou's heart race.


"What in tarnation—"
​

Her King! She needed to be by his side!

Pitou glanced around, trying to distinguish one rising plume of Purple Fog from the other plumes. But the purple... it swallowed everything: Light, sound, scent, and even her sense of balance somehow. Koneko's limp weight pulled against her arms. Pitou clutched her tighter, fiercely now, curling herself around the unconscious girl with her back arched and her teeth bared against the impossible task.

There!

Blue sky between the fog's closing fingers! Her legs launched her straight through on pure instinct, shooting through the air and threading the tiny gap which continued to shrink around her. Worse yet, the escape tunnel began to curve and wind. With nothing to kick off of and adjust her trajectory, that would have been the end.

But she was not out of options just yet.

With half a thought, she activated the glittering [Movement Ring] dangling on a bronze chain from her neck and mentally grasped the proffered power of flight. And just like that, Neferpitou flew. Gravity merely tugged at her, politely reminding which way was up and which way was down, but nothing more. Pitou pulled herself through the air on strings of thought, tugging her body this way and that way through the rapidly closing tunnel of fog, her speed in the air matching her land speed... that is to say: With a crack of shattered air.

The Purple Fog's closing grasp, like all others, was too slow to catch her.

In the time it took to blink, she was out, landing on one of Kuoh Academy's chimney stacks with her sleeping prize of a Koneko still in her arms.

From her vantage point, the battlefield below had become a single mass of opaque purple fog. The prismatic shield spell was nowhere to be found, and the Fog had become swollen and churning like a pile of discarded organs. It latched on the surrounding structures like a tumorous mass, the whole side of the buildings seeming to be consumed into that devouring violet murk.

And somewhere within that fog... was her King.

Her En flared and she peered inside, and her tail flicked from the nonsensical sensations she sensed. It was— Then the Fog began to dissipate, and

Neferpitou smiled overjoyed. "My King!"

Her voice rang across the field of thinning fog, but... no answer came back.

"My King...?"

The Purple Fog receded completely, but.... There was no victorious King responsible for its dissipation, rather a stark absence.

The dirt field was gone.

In its place yawned a crater, its dust rising from its crumbling edges that were carved through earth and stone alike. The academy structures fared no better. Entire sides were gone, exposing their mundane innards from ground floor to roof. Tables and chairs and shelves, cut clean down in half, fell tumbling out the missing walls and into the ravenous crater. It was as if the Fog had opened its mouth and bitten half of the structures clean away. And along with the buildings, everyone inside the fog was gone.

Her King was gone.

No... she couldn't have failed. Not again. Two Kings she had served, and with both, she had been separated when her King was in battle, when he needed his Royal Guard, when she needed her!

No, she can still... she can still fulfill her duty.

Her King was still alive, she was not a failure of a Royal Guard yet again. The Purple Fog was some kind of instantaneous transport ability. She sensed it herself! Her En had reached in expecting to push past the fog's curtain of interference but instead... slipped inside it like a hole in the wall of space, emerging out the other side.

But where was this 'other side'? Where had Her King been transported to?

She required that very valuable information.

In the past, it had been a simple task to acquire it: Find ningens who may possess such knowledge, cut open their skulls and use needles to directly stimulate their brains until they relinquished all they knew. Afterwards, they could then be freely butchered for their meat.

However, not only was she short on those, but her King also required more finesse from her. So, if she could not tear answers from random passersby, then she would need something almost as distasteful.

Allies from other nests.

Faces flicked past in her mind— some with frightened expressions, most unaware that she was even looking at them. All of them were ningens and devils who she'd seen or met in her brief travels with her King. But of all the faces, one stood out. Dark hair, violet eyes, smiles, pink, sparkles.

Her King's potential queen, the Satan Serafall.

She needed to find her. Of all the beings she had seen in this world, Satan Serafall possessed the most social power and loyalty to her king. Moreover... Their goals aligned: Her sister, the Princess Sitri, was also missing by the same displacement ability. She was a good option, one that her King would certainly approve of, provided she did not maim anyone before explaining herself.

There was only one immediate defect.

Both the Satan Serafall and the means of contacting her? Also unknown.

It was unacceptable, but not unexpected. Her King preferred to speak and act for himself; and she was only there to support him. To lie in wait, to flank the enemy, to stay in the background. And it had been the most... fun she'd had. But today, they were paying for their feeling of comfort now that she was forced to speak and act for them.

She needed some way to acquire infor—

Neferpitou flicked her ears as she felt as the girl in her arms slowly showed signs of regaining consciousness.

First came the shiver in the girl's fingers, a weak little tremor against Neferpitou's arm. Then came the change in her breathing, coming faster than the half-rest that she had been in. Her eyelids fluttered beneath pale lashes, her face tightening with confusion.

Finally, Koneko's golden eyes fluttered open, and Neferpitou's red was there to look down into them.

For one suspended instant, the still-dazed nekomata clearly did not understand what she was seeing. Cheek against her shoulder and looking up to red eyes close to her. She shifted slightly, feeling the arms holding her. And only then...

... did gold eyes flew wide open as Koneko's entire body went completely rigid.

"Don't scream. We are allies in this." Neferpitou informed her, but still tightened her grip by the smallest practical amount, only enough to prevent the predictable flailing that would waste time. She could tell that Koneko would have by how her pulse had jumped wildly. And how her pupils shrank to frightened points.

Koneko's mouth opened, but the sound that came out was strangled before it became words.

Neferpitou's ears flicked at the small broken noise. "My King had expended every effort to save you from your sister and her allies escaping with you." She said to the other girl, "And we were almost taken as well. We are allies in this."

Koneko stared at her. A faint tremble passed through her, and for a moment Neferpitou wondered if the girl had suffered some internal damage during Georg's Purple Fog displacement. But finally, she was able to speak.

"C-can you please put me down first? I promise that I.. I won't run away."

Neferpitou obliged her, setting the shorter, white-haired girl down on her feet. Brown leather shoes tapping on the rooftop's cracked stone. Idly, she noticed signs of her about to attempt escape despite giving her word... but then Kokeno properly saw the devastation by the Purple Fog: The buildings sheared in half, the massive crater that lay in the center, and the scattered school debris that had tumbled down to the crater's bottom.

"What..." Koneko said speechless, staring at the crater. "W-what happened here? Was this all Kuroka-nee?"

"No, we had cornered her, but her allies arrived to join the battle," Neferpitou explained. "We were unable to stop your sister's ally, Georg, from casting a wide-area magic that caused this. I believe it had displaced them beyond immediate reach. My King included. Which is why... You will aid me in finding him."

Koneko gulped, and while trembling, she asked.

"W-Why should I help you?"

Neferpitou's ears angled forward. The question was just as pointless as the answer was obvious: Her King was gone. All efforts must be made to locate him. The Satan Serafall had to be contacted for her resources and expertise, and currently, Koneko possessed probable routes to people who possessed routes to the Satan.

Therefore, she will assist.

But Neferpitou also understood that Koneko cannot be coerced. Which was convenient as her King was not the only one whisked away.

"Princess Sitri," she began, "her entire peerage, and also your fellow peerage members in Himejima and Yuuto... all of them were also displaced. Presumably to the same location as my King."

"Sona-san, Akeno and Yuuto were taken too?" Koneko repeated with a look of horror. She looked back toward the ruins. "E-even Nebiros-san? All of them gone... just to keep Kuroka-nee from taking me?"

"Yes."

Despite Koneko facing away from her, she could still smell the salt of forming tears. Koneko looked so small, standing at the roof's edge, looking over the destroyed school. The blue sky bearing down on her small shoulders. "Everyone... I'm sorry," she whispered, almost like a prayer. "It's all my fault again..."

Neferpitou watched her.

The optimal response would have been correction. Koneko's self-recrimination would not identify Georg's escape vector. It would not locate her King, Princess Sitri, Koneko's peerage, or Rias's displaced members. It would not determine whether they were alive, wounded, imprisoned, scattered, or dead.

But neither would letting Koneko, her only route forward, give herself to despair.

So, Neferpitou stepped closer, just close enough that Koneko would hear. "This is not your fault," she said softly. "Forces beyond your control, and beyond any reasonable expectation of your control, are not your burden."

Koneko swallowed. "But if I hadn't—"

"Your sister initiated an extraction, and her allies escalated beyond all expectations. My King Princess Sitri, her peerage, and Rias's peerage members responded. Everyone acted according to their own will and judgment, not yours," Neferpitou's tail gave one slow, irritated lash. "You have assigned yourself culpability, but it doesn't appear to be over today's events. No, it's over something else completely."

Koneko glanced back at her, tears streaming down from golden eyes as they flicked over Neferpitou then, from her red eyes to her cat ears, to her white hair, and to white tail that she flicked. And to Neferpitou's mild surprise, Koneko revealed her own ears and white tail.

"You would know, mm?!"

Neferpitou, not understanding what the other catgirl was implying, so she merely nodded once. "Mrrm."

Koneko didn't reply immediately, just stared at her with tears, and the wind moved between them to fill the quiet. Merely two white-haired catgirls standing on this rooftop under this clear blue sky, their ears flicking whenever the breeze brought up dust.

Finally, Koneko looked away again.

"Mm, you would know..." She murmured in capitulation, wiping her tears on her sleeves. "For Akeno, for Yuuto and for everyone else, I'll help you as best as I can... You mentioned Serafall-sama?"

Finally, cooperation.

"Serafall has power, resources and influence amongst the other Satans." Neferpitou began, "More importantly, Princess Sitri was also taken. Our objectives align, and she would presumably, by dint of familial ties, be well-motivated to assist in the effort. Do you know how to contact her?"

"I don't know... But I think Rias would."

Neferpitou's ear flicked in irritation at the name. Rias Gremory, the devil that had attempted to kill her King and also who they were now charged with antagonising. Her willingness to provide assistance would normally be questionable, but with two members of peerage also lost, her willingness was irrelevant, she will assist them.

"Mrrm... Acceptable," She nodded. "How do we contact her?"

"Mm, she's grounded. We'll have to take the train, ask her in person at Gremory Castle."

Neferpitou knew what a train was, and she knew that it would be too slow. "Our matter is too urgent. Do you not possess teleportation?" she asked, remembering the way Serafall-sama had simply vanished from her King's forest cabin.

"Highly illegal without authorisation." Koneko's tail twitched once. "We'd get into a lot of trouble, mm."

"The situation calls for it."

For a moment, Koneko looked as though she might argue. Her eyes flicked toward the ruined grounds and towards the places where people had been locked in heated combat merely a few minutes ago. She sighed.

"Mm, follow me."

Neferpitou followed as the other girl jumped off the roof, ran along the edge of the crater, and to the Old School building, the only structure that had been far enough to go untouched. Through the wooden hall, and up the stairs, they went. Passed the room where all this began, where Koneko's sister approached her, stepping around the shattered glass as they arrived at the dark, candlelit 'clubroom' in which Neferpitou had sensed them convening once or twice.

"Emergency teleportation circle," Koneko said, lifting her hand. A shining magic circle spun in her palm, and a larger one answered, lighting up beneath their feet with a low hum. "This will take us to Gremory Castle."

Netherpy's ears twitched once as she warily eyed the spinning magic circle. This would be her first time using 'devil magic' to displace herself, no choice but to put her trust in the other catgirl. She stepped closer, kept herself tense enough for any sudden knocks then.

The circle swallowed them in light...





... and they stepped out of the teleportation circle and onto elaborate carpet.

The air changed first.

The stench of a fresh battlefield no longer pressed down on them, the scents of blood and dust and metal and sweat. In their place was the smell of waxed floors and old woods and expensive fragrances of tea and perfumes. Neferpitou did not care for any of it. If anything, it insulted her that her urgency to locate her King wasn't shared by those who lived here. There should be alarms and shouts and the rattle of arms, but instead, she could still hear faint music playing in a distant parlour.

"Let's go," Koneko hurried ahead with a nervous looseness to her stride, as if fearing that they'll get caught.

It still felt too slow for Neferpitou. And her ear flicked in annoyance at how loud Koneko's footsteps were, but she was clearly at home here.

The teleportation chamber gave way to a narrow antechamber, then to a longer corridor lined with portraits and tall windows that showed glimpses of the 'Underworld' beyond, her King's homeworld. They passed several staff. Maids and butlers dusting and sweeping who took one look at Koneko and spared them only bowed heads.

They had almost reached the next turn when...

...A silver-haired maid appeared from the adjoining hall, pushing a tea cart. Porcelain rattled once as she stopped. Her red eyes moved from Koneko to Neferpitou, and the temperature of the hall seemed to drop by several degrees.

"Koneko?" she said. "What are you doing here with... that?"

Koneko flinched at the word. "S-she's with me, Grayfia-sama."

The maid's hands stayed lightly on the cart handle. Nothing in her posture changed, but Neferpitou felt the pressure of her attention settle like a blade against the throat. "What is this about?"

Neferpitou's tail-fur bristled. Yet another being vastly more powerful than her, she could sense, and worse was the outright hostility. Whether it was her or her King's reputation, this 'Grayfia' being possessed a negative opinion of her even in their first meeting. And every second spent explaining themselves to this was another second stolen from her King's survival in her eyes. The Satan Serafall was here. Neferpitou could sense her clearly now: above them, past the stairs, behind doors soaked through with a bright, 'sparkling' power that she had felt only once before.

Close, so close, they should not be wasting any more time.

And Neferpitou decided that they wouldn't.

She wrapped an arm around Koneko's waist, catching the girl before she could protest, and activated the second function [Movement Ring].

The stone hallway, and Grayfia's hostile stare, vanished in a blink. And immediately, they were replaced by a hall lit with warm lamplight, adorned by heavy tapestries, and commanded by a grand conference table. And around said table, the sudden scraping of chairs as several people startled slightly at Neferpitou's sudden appearance, landing in the corner of the room, one arm hooked around Koneko to keep her from falling.

"Koneko? Why do you have your ears and tail out?"

Rias was present, among other faces that Neferpitou did not recognise... save for one. Near the head of the table sat the Satan Serafall. Pink cap and with little white wings atop glossy black hair. A wand with a golden star... and violet eyes staring right at her.

"Pitou-chan? Koneko-chan?" Serafall said. "What are you doing here?"

Neferpitou nudged the other catgirl forward, and the words burst out of the shorter girl all at once.

"Kuoh Academy was attacked! Akeno, Yuuto, Sona, her entire peerage..." She swallowed, then glanced uneasily at Neferpitou. "And even Nebiros-san. Almost everyone's been taken away!"

The room immediately began to drown and freeze as, once again, Neferpitou felt the wash of dangerous 'demonic power' that she'd only felt once before.

Serafall leaned forward, black gloved hands curled into creaking fists.


"Who took Sona-chan?"
​

Koneko opened her mouth, but no answer came. She had been unconscious, and had only witnessed the aftermath. So she turned to the only one who had had seen it all.

Koneko opened her mouth, but no answer came. She had been unconscious when the worst of it happened; all she had seen was the ruinous aftermath. Her white ears flattened to the top of her head and her fingers tightened at her sides, then she turned, slowly, toward the only one in the room who had witnessed everything.

Every gaze followed.

Neferpitou stood under the room's collective gaze, slim in her blue coat, the badge on her chest catching a hard silver gleam. She only straightened, and began to tell them how everyone had been taken, her voice projecting clear across the conference hall.


"My King, the Princess Sitri and I were convening in her Student Council Room when we detected an intruder..."
​





(A day later)

Koneko sat very still in the waiting hall outside the conference room, beside a large portrait of the Gremory Founder.

She hadn't been able to sleep after they'd sent everyone away yesterday. They didn't argue about that part since Grayfia and the Satan Lucifer had to hold back the Satan Leviathan from doing something hasty. Rias was back in the human world—her parents had given her to handle the ruins of Kuoh Academy— and she was taking full advantage of the break in her grounding. Meanwhile, the two Satans and Grayfia talked behind closed doors of the conference room.

She... wasn't waiting alone though.

Koneko lifted her eyes, looking to the one standing across the red hall carpet that separated them like a river.

Neferpitou stood in her blue coat with her shoulders back and her spine straight, almost like one of the suits of armour standing all along the hall. Her red eyes stayed on the closed doors. The sheriff badge on her chest caught the hall-light every time she breathed, shining in little flashes of amber-gold brass.

Koneko kept looking at that badge even when she told herself not to. It had been given to someone who should not have existed.

That was what she could not stop thinking about.

Rias had told her enough, and everyone had whispered enough, and Kuroka had believed enough to do terrible things because of it.

Neferpitou was a chimera and a clone. Maybe even a thing made from Koneko herself, or from pieces of her blood. Koneko didn't know how that could be true, because when she looked at Neferpitou, she didn't see herself at all. Not with those scary-focused red eyes, not in the shape of her face, and not in the way Neferpitou stood as if she was never afraid of anything. Not in the way she made decisions without fear. Neferpitou wasn't like her at all.

The only thing they had in common was their white hair and their feline features...

And maybe that was why it hurt so much.

Maybe when they were making Neferpitou, they'd taken only the good pieces of her... The brave pieces she had never found when she needed them, the strong pieces that she couldn't wield, and the Senjutsu that she had always been too scared to use properly.

Koneko could still feel Neferpitou's chakra even now, even when she tried to press down every bit of Senjutsu inside herself so she would not feel too much. The other catgirl's power filled the hall like some kind of hungry monster, like it'd just pounce at her with a bite any moment now, but... she knew Neferpitou wasn't like that at all.

She's just been standing there, quiet and calm like any other polite attendant in the Gremory castle.

A stable chimera-clone... Her clone.

The thought still made Koneko's stomach knot up. She wanted to keep running away from it, say that she isn't a clone, but she knew that she couldn't anymore. Everyone else, from Rias to the Satans, believed or knew that Neferpitou was a clone made from her. Even her Kuroka-nee had believed it so much that she had massacred her peerage back in House Naberius to stop her from ever being made. She'd failed, but now she had come back today, going as far as breaking into Rias's territory.

Koneko still didn't understand what her Kuroka-nee had been trying to say to her with her ranting and rambling before she knocked her out to kidnap her. But she did understand that Neferpitou had been the one to save her.

She just kept staring down at her twiddling thumbs in her lap, because looking at her clone was still too hard for her.

"Thank you," she said softly, and winced when the quiet hall made the words sound louder than she wanted. "For saving me. From Kuroka-nee, and from the fog. I... I know you didn't have to, but you did anyway."

Neferpitou looked at her for a long moment.

Koneko couldn't tell what she was thinking. Her ears did not move much, but her tail curled slowly behind her, and that made Koneko even more nervous for some reason. Then Neferpitou seemed to think very hard about something before she gave a small nod.

"You are... worth saving, Koneko."

Koneko flinched when Neferpitou said her name. It sounded strange from her mouth. But being acknowledged like that, like she was still her own person, even by someone very dangerous... it made Koneko feel a little warmer, even if she didn't know what to do with it.

The doors opened a little.

Grayfia-sama leaned out the door's gap. Her braided silver hair, tied with ribbons, swayed as she looked at them. Her eyes stayed colder on Neferpitou than on Koneko.

"The Satans will see you now, Neferpitou," she said to the clone.

"Mrm."

Koneko stood too fast and almost tripped on the carpet, but Neferpitou was beside her before she could fall. Koneko caught herself, cheeks hot, and whispered, "Uh, thank you," again.

And when they went in together, Koneko stood just a bit closer to her.

The conference room felt different from when they had first arrived. It was too quiet now. Only three people sat at the table: Rias, Sirzechs-sama, and Serafall-sama. Koneko looked at Rias first because she knew Rias best, but her King's worried face, probably for Akeno and Yuuto, didn't help her feel better at all. Sirzechs-sama was silent. Serafall-sama sat very still and very frowning, and that frightened Koneko most of all.

On the dark wood of the table, in front of Satan Leviathan, there were two things: One was a letter, and the other... was a brass sheriff badge.

Koneko felt Neferpitou go rigid beside her.

Was it... the badge? It wasn't Neferpitou's, because her badge was still pinned on her chest. But it had the same shape, the same brass shine... Except, instead of 'Deputy - Kuoh Town', this one had 'Sheriff - Kuoh Town' emblazoned on it.

No, it couldn't be...

Serafall-sama touched the edge of the letter with two fingers, and said three simple and gut-wrenching words.


"They have demands."
​

They were too late.

Koneko looked from the badge to the letter, and could only wonder what had happened to Sona, Akeno, Yuuto and everyone else after the Purple Fog swallowed them all.





(Earlier) [Student Council Room]

Sona Sitri woke up staring at a familiar ceiling.

Dark wood panelling, rich and absorbed light well, she remembered picking out the specific tree for lumber herself. To the side, blue curtains tied with gold-string tassels fell to the floor near her head like a waterfall. The windows still shattered from when Malal and his damned nekoshou-chimera familiar had burst out of them. She still couldn't fathom why they couldn't have opened it, or at least burst through one window rather than shattering two, one for each dramatic exit.

Why was she even lying on the carpet? She didn't know, but...

Sona closed her eyes again and permitted herself one measured and tentatively relieved breath.

... But what matters was that they had won.

Their Prismatic Gaol had held. Her peerage had somehow plugged the breach where that Purple Fog began seeping through. Dimension Lost, she realised what the Sacred Gear was now... In hindsight, she should have recognised one of the 'lost' Longinuses from the description of the Purple Fog alone. Regardless, they had won against it. They had somehow prevented the Longinus from scattering them all across Earth, or from leaving their souls to dissolve in the Dimensional Gap, or just tossing them directly into a prison somewhere.

Her ribs ached dully, and her head throbbed much sharper in places like she'd taken a hard tumble somewhere. But... she was alive.

"Sona-kaichou?" someone—Tomoe, she recognized—asked worriedly to the side.

"Yes, I'm conscious," Sona murmured, then preempted the inevitable second question. "And yes, I'm fine. I don't feel injured beyond a headache and some aching ribs."

"Ah, that's good to hear, kaichou! We were so worried!"

'We.' Sona clung to the word, keeping her eyes closed as she exhaled in relief. Good, her peerage was here too. That was the last of her worries. And for one clean, very foolish second, she thought everything was alright.

She pushed herself upright, and the relief shrivelled up before she finished sitting.

"What... in Satan's name..."

She hadn't been lying down in her Student Council Room; she had been lying down in half her Student Council Room.

Two windows, three walls, no doors, but one giant wall-to-wall hole. Like a cardboard box cut in half, the room abruptly ended halfway through. Half a bookshelf, half a motivational poster about clean-as-you-go, and half an announcement corkboard. Right through the new memorandum on maximum skirt length, cut so cleanly that the laminated rules still clung to their half of reality. The oaken conference table stopped one seat short from the end too, its remaining length jutting toward open air where the rest of it should have been.

The far wall with the door was just gone entirely. The corridor beyond it too, the one that commanded an excellent view of Kuoh Academy's front courtyard.

... There was only the outside. And even that was wrong!

There was no Japanese cityscape stretching in the distance... no glimpse of the Sagami Bay glittering in the distance. Instead, there was a dusty, copper-colored plain that stretched endlessly in all directions beneath a sunless, purple sky.

Sona stared.

"What... Where..." She did not remember standing, but suddenly she was on her feet, both hands gripping her head. Then she saw it: A quarter-piece of the building lay sticking up like a ragged pyramid in the distance. "Is that the gymnasium?! WHAT HAPPENED TO MY SCHOOL?!"

Tomoe just thumbed her katana, stretching a helpless 'Ummm...'

"Get me Tsubaki." Sona said hollowly.

"It's..." Tomoe, instead of bowing and running off to summon her Queen Piece, stayed put with that hesitant expression. "It's just me and Ruruko."

Sona almost snapped her neck turning toward the redhead. "What do you mean, just you and Ruruko?! Where are the others?!"

"We don't know, kaichou. Ruruko was already carrying you when I ran into her. We just found the Student Council Room together and decided to make this our base until you woke up. Ruruko is out scouting the perimeter right now. The others must still be out there."

"I see," Sona looked out the... hole.

For a moment, Sona could only hear the wind as it whispered through the severed room.

It whistled through the broken glass of the windows, flapped the blue curtains, and dragged a thin veil of dust over the Student Council carpet. It even smelled wrong: Not one whiff of Japanese pine, or the fragrant Japanese Osmanthus flower they planted all around the Kuoh Academy campus. No, none of those things. Instead, it was this barren and parched metallic musk.

Despite her aversion, Sona forced herself to take a deep breath.

She also forced herself to step 'out' of her Student Council Room. Just one step on proper regulation tiling that was waxed last Monday... and the next step, her polished school shoes were tamping on the dry red dust.

Panic, and angry screaming, were luxuries she could not afford at the moment.

Taking stock... She had one peerage member standing behind her, at least one more nearby, and the rest missing somewhere far beyond this room that had been sliced out of their cozy Japanese town like a slice of party lasagna thrown out a twenty-story window and down to some unknown hell. And now, with one catastrophically damaged school and a largely missing peerage and a rather abysmal chance of surviving being hopelessly lost, her unofficial match with the Seekvaira Agares didn't seem as stressful anymore.

"Kaichou," Her Knight Piece said carefully, "Where are we? This doesn't look like any part of the Underworld we've been to."

"Because it isn't," Sona looked out over the coppery plain, past the broken chunk of gymnasium, past the scattered rocks, toward the endless horizon where distant flat-topped mountains stuck out in the purple horizon.

She couldn't afford to panic.

Every pureblooded devil had seen photos of places like these in their Underworld geography books. They've also heard their parents' warning to never wander out too far from their own territory, especially to the places where no other House's territory lie. And of course, who hasn't heard the occasional missing expeditions that the morning news often reports? Those well-equipped expeditions who weren't stranded with just half a Student Council Room.

"There is a very good reason why much of the Underworld is unclaimed despite our civilization being millennia older than Mankind's."

"So, this isn't just one of those borderlands?"

"No, this is..." Sona scanned the horizon, her mouth suddenly very dry. "This is the endless, untamable wastes of the Underworld. Hundreds—possibly thousands—of miles away from civilized lands. These uncharted territories are what lies past the edges of modern maps, ravaged by unstable geography, outrightly hostile magical phenomena and the occasional abyssal megafauna."

"So there aren't any locals who could help us?" said Tomoe who went very still.

Sona frowned. "The only people who call these wastelands home are rebels plotting the destruction of modern devil civilization, and backward savages still living hundreds of years in the past."





[Elsewhere in the Underworld Badlands]

"Home sweet home!"

It wasn't my Nebiros Valley, but it was my Underworld!

"Good old devil country!" I laughed, throwing my arms wide at the empty horizon like I'd just stepped off a train back home.

I didn't know what that Georg fella thought he was doing, flinging me back home to the Underworld. I reckon he figured distance would break the fight apart clean, or maybe he just bolted and his Purple Fog accidentally yanked whatever pieces of the battlefield stuck to it like gum under its shoe.

For the record, It's hard to be mad about people showing up to get a piece of me when they're paying me so handsomely!


Feat Achieved! - Sequence Breaker I
Engage in combat with a mid-story antagonists before even meeting any of the previous antagonists. And survive.
+1 Gold Gacha Ticket

...
​

I'll be honest. I hadn't the faintest idea who Kuroka, Heracles and Georg were supposed to be—or what their deal was for that matter—to warrant being a 'mid-story antagonist', but I did know that I could fix that quick: I had a truth-compelling [XX - Judgement] tarot card collecting cobwebs in my pocket for Satan knows how long now! Now if only one of them could just be courteous enough to turn up alive and restrained for me to use this tarot card on, that'd be swell.

Can't rightly picture that happening any time soon.

These bastards were tough! And after watching that Heracles fella eat a clean Rennala's Full Moon-shot to the chest and still have enough vinegar in him to go into a slugging match against Ichabod the Imperfect, I sure as heck wasn't strong enough to take them on in a straight fight myself! Just another sign that I'd picked a quarrel with beings well above my weight-class! And they're not gonna be happy with me from now on, I'd wager.

That was the bad news.

The good news is, if I'm still somehow winning said fights with the middleweights of this entire universe, then that's a sign that I'm on the right trail! Makes me feel like a real middle-class devil now!

And getting the opportunity to burn a Gold Ticket is always appreciated!


[Astral Bar] |Elite Item|
Calamity Mod - A refined bar crafted out of the depths of the Astral Infection, a bar containing refined cosmic energy that is nigh breakable, when infused with energy it is capable of releasing cosmic energies. Restock Timer: 8 Hours

...
​

... And I reckon I shouldn't touch the metal bar made out of this so-called "Astral Infection" with my bare hands. Probably shouldn't even take the damn thing out at all, if I'm being honest. But "nigh unbreakable" and "cosmic energies" sounds like the sort of devilry a curious devil like myself could do some fine, irresponsible tinkering with. Bronze, wood, and now this strange hunk of cursed star-metal... Got a fair few toys in the wagon now.

First thing's first, though...

"Where the hell am I?"

I scanned from horizon to horizon, trying to get my bearings. And not finding it, I checked the purple sky above moved [Verdance].

A tree sprouted under my boot. It punched up from the soil in a rush of green and brown. Branches snapping wide open in the air like an umbrella. And in about three heartbeats, it had lifted me sixty feet above the ground. Black Oak, common as dust back in Nebiros Valley.

"This'll make for a good landmark," I patted the trunk. A thin trickle of power seeped up through the living wood and into me, and I frowned. "I can't believe I'm saying this about ningen lands, but... I already miss the dense woods of Kuoh Town."

Infinite Demonic Power was darn near impossible to quit once you'd had a taste.

This tree barely had enough for me to sip in, but that wasn't why I formed it. From this high up, the world opened itself. The land rolled wide beneath a purple sky, empty in the way only the deep Underworld countryside could be empty. My [Byakugan] reached as far as it could, but found less than I wanted. Just dust and rocks for miles around.

But I wasn't lost yet.

I'd tucked a communication spell into Ichabod during the rebuild. It was one of those features I kicked myself for not adding the first time around, because Satan below, I sure could've used it when Rias and Sona was busy trying to beat the bronze off him. Could've told them to stop fighting the construct and making things worse. Really could've saved everybody a whole heap of bruises and broken bones... assuming, of course, Rias was the listening type.

Well, no use crying over spilled milk.

This time, the spell was there. Better yet, it came with something of a locator... for when Ichabod gets blown away, buried, banished, swallowed, or otherwise misplaced, I could point myself at him like a compass. And right then, that needle was wiggling.

I hadn't come alone. Ichabod was here.

My black sleeve fluttered in the dry wind as I lifted my arm, tuning the spell by feel. The connection tugged faintly through my demonic power, right in my bones, the gentlest nudging on my arm.

"Somewhere..." My arm swept in an arc and stopped. My finger pointed across the flat, featureless horizon. "...east-wise over there, huh?"

I made the jump, pushing off the oak trunk like an arrow off a bowstring, shooting for the horizon where red dust met purple sky.

The dry air just rushed by. And keeping low to the ground, my wings flared out once most of the speed from the initial jump wore off. The horizon stayed where it was no matter how long I chased it. a familiar feeling. It was good to be home. Just ain't natural for a devil to stay in the human world. Even less for a country devil like me to stay in the city, devil or otherwise. It was—

Blood and massive footprints.

I hit the dirt boots-first, already in a crouch right beside it.

The footprint stamped violently onto the dirt told me everything: Shoe size of 40 and with a sole pattern that I remember molding onto a certain someone's armored bronze soles. This was Ichabod alright. But the blood? Looks like Heracles went along too. I figured Georg would have taken him when he teleported away, but it looks like Heracles couldn't just leave well enough alone., and followed Ichabod to settle the score. That, or Ichabod had him tight in his grip.

Faintly with my [Byakugan], I could see more in the distance. I stood up with scoff. "Well, least nobody got bored."

The signs got clearer from there as I kept moving.

Trenches carved through the dust, thin and deep, and in between more footprints. Must be from Ichabod's Carian Slicers where Heracles managed to dodge them; the ones he couldn't dodge had more dried blood on the dust. There were also deep divots too where Greatblade Phalanx must have thrust into the dirt. And... Glassy craters glittered where sand had been cooked into little crystals.

"Ichabod didn't have fire spells built into him," I muttered, kept following. "So, Heracles was still putting up a fight, huh?"

Ichabod's and Heracles' fight kept moving in hops, long stretches of undisturbed dirt separated bloodstains and scorched craters. Even a big crater as wide as football field, lined with flash-forged glass. More of Heracles' doing, trying to keep distance to use whatever spell managed to do that, but Ichabod kept closing said distance.

But finally... I caught up.

An amber-gold shard glinted in the horizon, like a splinter stuck into the purple sky.

Ichabod the Imperfect stood in the middle of a scorched battlefield. Twelve feet of gleaming bronze standing tall under the Underworld's sunless sky. Wristblades and halo disengaged. The golden light in his eyes was dim, but not gone. He still had just a little juice to him, [Bronze Creation] told me. Not much, but truth be told, it was a wonder he had any left at all. Only two of the fifteen inscribed barrier spells were up, regenerated. That meant that, at some point, something had hit him hard enough to make all fifteen shatter at once.

The proof of it was right there: Ichabod's whole left arm was gone, clean down from the shoulder. And here I thought I overbuilt and overengineered him! But nope, Ichabod had only been just a hair tough enough to win.

And win, he did.

Because there in Ichabod's shadow, lay the body of Heracles on the ground.

He was dead, no doubt about it. Face was almost as pale as his gray hair. One leg was bent wrong, resting over a massive footprint where Ichabod and his several tons of bronze had stomped it shattered. And most telling was the hole right through his chest, his heart, and straight out the back. Any lick of moisture dries quick in these parts, and the thirsty ground had already drunk up most of the blood, leaving just a large splotch of red in the dirt around him.

But what was surprising was the smile on his pale face.

"This was exactly how you wanted to go, huh? Fighting?"

Heracles didn't answer. Some men wanted to pass away in their beds from old age, all peaceful like. Others wanted to go down fighting a bronze war construct... even if it didn't matter the end.

And it definitely didn't matter none: The moment I get even a postal code near a forest, [Bronze Creation] will be there to reform Ichabod's entire arm, [Intermediate Item Construction] to triple his barrier matrix again, and probably have make old Ichabod five-feet-taller while I'm at it too. All ready for bastards even bigger and tougher than Heracles had been.

There was no glory here.

"Still, gotta review your recording of that fight sometime." I said, with a quick glance high up to Ichabod's dimly shining eyes. "I bet you gave Ichabod here your darndest."

Ichabod the Imperfect didn't answer, still just glowering at the corpse at his feet.

"But what's all this though?" I crouched down beside the dead Heracles.

He wasn't shirtless anymore. Or maybe he was, technically, but there was not much man left uncovered enough for the word to sit right. Metal had grown out of his skin in hard plates, all covered with polished spikes. Spiked pauldrons, spiked leg bracers, spiked arm bracers. His left hand—still raised a little as if he had meant to swing one more time—was the strangest thing: Covered in a massive metal diamond as big as his torso.

Crouching down, I rested a hand on the oversized metal glove. [Structural Analysis] flared, and I stared into the distance as [Intermediate Item Construction] and [King of All Trades] chewed on the rush of know-hows, whats-its and the whole rigmarole of whatever this was.

...

... I let out a low whistle.

"Ah, so this is your Sacred Gear. Darn, would've been nice if you'd stayed alive. Nothing much [Structural Analysis] could glean from you now. Your oversized missile might have a nice warhead shape that I could use, but it's just not worth half as much as the Sacred Gear that lets you make the fool thing out of thin air in the first place!"

I gave the dead gauntlet one last pat. "Alright, let's get you scalped and leave the rest of you to the devil-buzzards."

+1 Diamond Gacha Ticket
​

"... Diamond?" I sputtered, eyes wide like dinner plates as I stared at the corpse. "Were you really that tough? Nevermind, I'll tell you what! If this gives me a good roll, I'll give you the deluxe service! Deal?"

The dead ningen just kept smiling.

"Deal!" I laughed. And, almost shaking with excitement, I burned the diamond ticket and got...


[Old Blood] |Legendary Trait|
Bloodborne - You have the blood of the Great Ones running through your veins. You have greater vitality and energy thanks to the Old Blood which also grants you resistance against Esoteric effects. Demonic power surged. Beating in my heart, pulsing under my skin, hardening my bones, thrumming to be used at every twitch of my trigger-finger. A magic circle spun in my hand, and dirt began climbing into neat mounds in the space beside him. The Dig spell was a must-have for any farmhand: Useful for anything from plowing fields, making contour trenches or making it easier to pile fence posts. Hair Aclease
'Inheritor of the name, the soul, and the twelve flavors.'
​

"Close enough," I said, knocking off his grave with the headstone. Taking off my black cowboy hat, I held it against my chest in respect. "Uh... He was a generous soul, gave me a whole lot more in death than most do in their life. And he was one tough bastard, definitely worthy of the Ultimate-class title. Oh, and he had great hair too."

A respectful second, I let pass. The grave staying quiet as it lay under the purple sky of the Underworld, in the middle of this scorched and glass-riddled battlefield where Heracles 'won.' It's what he would've wanted, I reckon.

I pressed my cowboy hat back onto my silver hair, fixed my dark suit.

"Alright, let's leave Hair Aclease to his dirtnap," I smiled back at Ichabod. "and find our way back to Kuoh before Neferpitou turns it upside down looking for me. The silly cat. I bet everyone's all worried and looking high-and-low for me right now."





[Elsewhere]

"I activated Dimension Lost prematurely."

Georg lay propped against white hospital bed pillows that did nothing to improve the color of his face. The whole Hero Faction gathered around the hospital room, looking at him with varying expressions of pity.

Cao Cao sat beside him, the True Longinus resting upright against his shoulder. Rings of worked metal hung beneath the spearhead. Every so often his fingers tapped the shaft, and the rings answered with a delicate clink.

"It was a last-ditch measure," Georg stared at the ceiling without his spectacles, voice still dry. "Our intel was bad, and the situation had deteriorated beyond my ability to salvage it. So I had to buy time and distance for us to restrategize now."

"It was the right call," Cao Cao's pale eyes did not leave him. "And Heracles?"

"The badlands, scattered with the rest of them. I expect he's not in the mood to listen to anyone now that I've cut him off from his fight."

Cao Cao gave the faintest breath of amusement. Then the spear rings clinked again.

Across the room, Perseus stood with his arms folded at their blue-haired leader, "Your gambit with Kuroka backfired."

"It has. Because of the Fake Nebiros Heir," Cao Cao said, his fingers tightened around the spear. "Our reports say that his brass golem was weak enough to be destroyed in battle against the Gremory and the Sitri heiresses. If it had remained at that level, any one of us could have batted it aside effortlessly... but no one could have anticipated that he'd rebuild it strong enough to be able to challenge Heracles in a straight on battle. It's ingenious."

For a moment, Cao Cao said nothing, tapping his finger on the spearshaft. The rings clink softly against the blessed spearhead.

Clink. Clink. Clink.

He stopped, glanced up at the ever-sharp spearpoint and frowned. "Our honorable allies," Cao Cao said at last, "have expressly forbidden me from revealing myself before the appointed hour, and I agree with them. The True Longinus should only be revealed to a worthy battle."

"The wielder of the True Longinus... grounded," Perseus pointed out, and laughs and giggles followed.

"Unfortunately true," Cao Cao graciously smiled along with the laughs for a moment before looking back at Georg. "Can you open a path to Heracles' likeliest location?"

"Yes, but only for... three people? Anymore and I'll pass out."

"You were going to do that already."

"Maybe," Georg gave a pale smile, "But then you should wish whoever is sent a pleasant walk home, because it'll be a one way trip until I wake up."

Cao Cao chuckled. And he tapped his spear's butt on the tiles, and its rings gave one clean note as they settled. A habit whenever he'd made a decision.

"Perseus, Connla and Leonardo... you're in charge of locating Heracles. Whether he's in the heat of battle or injured and unconscious, drag him back here. Turn him into stone if you have to. Let someone else find a way to turn Kuroka to their advantage. Our priority now is to make sure Heracles doesn't get himself hurt any more than he already has."

Georg slipped out a shaky hand from under his hospital bed's sheet, and with a strained look... Purple Fog spilled from beneath his palm, thickening slowly at first, then rolling upright like a curtain. The hospital wall behind the Fog vanished, replaced by a hard view of reddish plains and a purple sky.

Connla stepped through without a word, adjusting the cuff of his black sleeves. Leonardo, their most junior and fresh-faced member, followed without looking back. Perseus, hefting his sword and shield, was about to follow suit as well when.

"Perseus."

He stopped at the sound of Georg's voice, and turned around from the threshold.

Their bedridden friend held up a small glass vial, wrapped with paper seals and already filled with a captured wisp of Purple Fog. "Break this to signal to me that you need a return passage."

Perseus took the vial. He turned his head slightly. "Cao Cao."

"Yes?"

"What are you planning, sending us three in particular?"

"You three are the ones most suited to countering the capabilities of the Fake Nebiros. So, if you come across him... kill him."

"He's just fought Heracles, probably separated from his golem and chimera." Perseus scratched his head in confusion. "Isn't this a bit overkill?"

"Exactly, Doesn't it sound like the perfect time to dispose of him?" Their leader replied, his expression still courteous.

Perseus stared at the smiling Cao Cao for a bit longer, then shook his head, smiling all the while. "If you wanted to get back at him because he threw off your plans, you could have just said so."

Cao Cao just shrugged with his innocent smile, caught on his little omission. Perseus rolled his eyes.

"Fine, I'll pass it along."

Then he stepped through, and Georg's Purple Fog folded shut behind him, leaving behind only a little dust from the badlands.



[Near Heracle's Grave]

Dust crunched under foot as I began the long walk back to that tree I planted.

I walked the trail alone now.

It was surprising how easy it was now: Putting Ichabod in the pocket dimension. More efficient now, only cost like a quarter as much in Demonic Power. The doing of [Old Blood], I was sure of it. It didn't flood my noggin' with information like [King of All Trades], but it made it easier to make heads or tails about magic stuff, like seeing a puzzle with fresh eyes and spotting all the little things that I missed before.

"My best bet..." I rubbed my chin, "... would be to go looking for train tracks. Send out some bronze constructs in every direction; one of 'em is bound to come across tracks at some point. And I have been looking for an excuse to build my own train ever since I used [Structural Analysis] on the one I rode to Kuoh Town."

The badlands stretched quiet around me, not agreeing with the plan.

"'Course, if I'm really unlucky, I could just brute force my way up the skies. Won't be a pleasant ride thanks to Heavens' work, but I reckon I could build something that could last long enough to spot a town or some train tracks then!"

[King of All Trades] and [Item Creation] went spinning into drafting blueprints for that. Didn't do this often cause of how quick time passes. Even after just a few drafts, I could already see my tree in the distance: just the tiniest peak of green over the endless reddish dirt. My boots crunched over ash, gravel, and the occasional bit of glassed sand where Heracles did his explosion magic.

And then, politely, from my right—


"Hello, Mr. Undertaker."
​

Almost jumping out of my skin, I whipped around, spell totems in my hand.

She was tall. Skin like pale porcelain. Dressed in dark wools like she had just stepped out of… well, Nebiros Valley, actually. Proper nineteenth-century fashion, back when folks still dressed with an ounce of class and self-respect. Dark brown bonnet that covered much of her snow white hair. Her doll-jointed hands folded over the front of her long brown embroidered dress, layered neat for a funeral. And brown shawl drawn close over narrow shoulders as she matched my walking pace. Here in the middle of nowhere.

I remember her from somewhere, I was sure of it. My mind still racing to put a name to the pretty face.

"What... did you call me?" I stalled for time.

"Undertaker. That is your trade, is it not?" She said with her strange accent, "Coffin, grave and service. You gave him every degree of solid comfort, which a man of his circumstances could wish to receive. The work well suits you, Mr. Undertaker."

She smiled at me with her honest blue eyes and that's when I realised something...

The Plain Doll sure looked every bit as pretty as she did in Bloodborne.

- Cat x Chimera End -​

AN:

It's a broken ability to... be able to teleport things and people anywhere. Georg could literally teleport enemies straight into the Dimensional Gap for the instant KO and it was only through pure author fiat that he didn't use it more often.

Kind of wrote myself... off a cliff by escalating the fight this hard. It went from "Wouldn't it be funny if the slick city-girl Sona was lost in the 'countryside'?" to "Hey, Astral Bar and Old Blood has crazy synergy if we lean into the cosmic horror." to "We need enemies to kill with all this cool stuff we're getting." to "Oh crap, it's 9k words long and I have yet to even begin writing the Pitou-Koneko interaction that I've been hinting at."
